## 00 cvrp_aco_r1_e74

算子 Refine；节点 64；父节点 59；fitness -15.75241678567303；局部纠错：未计入。

增益：补充了 1/d、0.5 余弦系数和长边指数惩罚。

保留问题或边界：两版均把无方向的径向余弦相似性讲成有方向的 sweep；二次版延续了行归一化改变信息素相对强度的解释。在乘法 ACO 的理想计算中，同一行的公共比例因子会抵消，实际 epsilon/floor 的影响需另算。

<details><summary>原始 Idea、校准 Idea、最终代码与父代码</summary>

### 前置 Idea

The current heuristic over-constrains the search with multiple heavy multiplicative penalties (sharp $d^{-1.3}$ base, strong k-NN boost, aggressive feasibility penalty) which likely leads to premature convergence or suboptimal route structures. I will simplify the heuristic to focus on a smooth, less aggressive baseline ($d^{-1.0}$) combined with a softer, more robust geometric "sweep" indicator. Instead of using the noisy mutual k-NN boost (which can fragment tours) or the complex capacity ratio (which is an approximation), I will rely on a strong angular alignment bonus that encourages ants to sweep through customers in a consistent direction relative to the depot. This geometric prior is a known powerful feature for VRP and helps connect distant clusters correctly without the risk of getting stuck in local minima caused by too many interacting penalties. I will also remove the row normalization, as ACO typically relies on the raw heuristic values relative to pheromones, and normalizing can distort the magnitude of the signal compared to the pheromone matrix scale.

### 校准 Idea

The final implementation constructs a simplified edge-prior matrix for the Capacitated Vehicle Routing Problem (CVRP) by focusing on geometric consistency and distance proximity, removing complex multiplicative penalties present in the parent version. The core decision rule relies on a base heuristic of inverse distance ($1/d$), which inherently favors shorter edges, modulated by a geometric "sweep" alignment bonus. This approach assumes that optimal routes in CVRP often exhibit a spiral or sweep-like structure relative to the depot, where customers are visited in a consistent angular order. By reinforcing this geometric intuition, the heuristic aims to guide ants toward constructing routes that minimize total travel distance while respecting implicit capacity constraints through efficient local clustering, rather than explicitly modeling capacity in the heuristic matrix.

The computation begins by sanitizing the input distance matrix to ensure all values are finite and strictly positive, using a small epsilon ($10^{-9}$) to avoid division by zero. The base heuristic $H$ is initialized as the element-wise inverse of the distance matrix, with the diagonal set to zero to prohibit self-loops. For customer-to-customer edges (indices $1$ to $n-1$), the algorithm calculates the angular alignment between the radial vectors from the depot to each pair of customers. This is achieved by computing the dot product of the normalized radial unit vectors, yielding the cosine of the angle between them. An alignment bonus factor, $1 + \alpha \cdot \cos(\theta)$, is applied to these edges, where $\alpha = 0.5$ is a moderate scaling parameter. This bonus increases the desirability of edges connecting customers that lie in similar angular directions or slightly ahead in the sweep, with a floor of $0.2$ to prevent negative weights. Additionally, a soft penalty is applied to customer-to-customer edges that exceed the median customer-to-customer distance. This penalty uses an exponential decay function, $e^{-0.2 \cdot (excess / median)}$, to discourage long jumps between distant clusters while allowing necessary connections. The algorithm explicitly omits capacity-aware heuristics, mutual k-NN boosts, and row normalization, relying instead on the ACO algorithm's construction process to enforce capacity constraints and the raw heuristic values to guide the search relative to the pheromone matrix.

Compared to the parent code, the most significant changes are the removal of the capacity-aware feasibility factor, the mutual k-NN boost, and the row normalization step. The parent version used a sharp distance exponent ($d^{-1.3}$) and aggressive multiplicative penalties for capacity risks and long edges, which likely caused premature convergence by over-constraining the search space. The final code simplifies the distance exponent to $1.0$, allowing for more exploration, and replaces the complex, noisy penalty terms with a single, smooth geometric alignment bonus. The removal of row normalization preserves the absolute magnitude of the heuristic signal, ensuring that the heuristic's scale is not distorted relative to the pheromone matrix during the ant construction process. This simplification is hypothesized to improve performance by reducing the risk of getting stuck in local minima caused by interacting penalties and by providing a clearer, more robust geometric guide for route construction.

### 最终代码

```python
import numpy as np

def heuristics(distance_matrix: np.ndarray, coordinates: np.ndarray, demands: np.ndarray, capacity: int) -> np.ndarray:
    """Return edge desirability values for CVRP ant colony optimization.

    Args:
        distance_matrix: Pairwise Euclidean distances with shape (n, n).
        coordinates: Node coordinates with shape (n, 2). Node 0 is the depot.
        demands: Node demands with shape (n,). The depot demand is zero.
        capacity: Capacity shared by all vehicles.

    Returns:
        An (n, n) edge-prior matrix. Larger values make an edge more likely
        to be sampled. Values at or below zero are treated as 1e-9.
    """
    n = distance_matrix.shape[0]
    if n <= 1:
        return np.ones((n, n), dtype=np.float64)

    eps = 1e-9
    d = np.asarray(distance_matrix, dtype=np.float64)
    d = np.where(np.isfinite(d), d, 1.0)
    d = np.maximum(d, eps)

    # Base heuristic: simple inverse distance.
    # A softer exponent (1.0) allows more exploration compared to 1.3,
    # reducing the risk of getting trapped in local minima.
    H = 1.0 / d
    np.fill_diagonal(H, 0.0)

    customers = np.arange(1, n)
    nc = len(customers)
    if nc == 0:
        return H

    coords = np.asarray(coordinates, dtype=np.float64)
    depot = coords[0]
    customer_coords = coords[1:]

    # Calculate radial unit vectors from depot to each customer
    radial_vecs = customer_coords - depot
    radial_norms = np.linalg.norm(radial_vecs, axis=1)
    radial_norms[radial_norms < eps] = 1.0
    radial_unit = radial_vecs / radial_norms[:, None]

    # Dot products give cosine of angle between vectors
    dot_products = np.clip(radial_unit @ radial_unit.T, -1.0, 1.0)

    # Geometric Sweep Bonus:
    # Encourage moving to customers that are in a similar angular direction
    # or slightly ahead in the sweep.
    # A factor of 1 + alpha * cos(theta) rewards aligned movements.
    # We use a moderate alpha (0.5) to balance geometric preference against distance.
    alpha_align = 0.5
    align_bonus = 1.0 + alpha_align * dot_products
    align_bonus = np.maximum(align_bonus, 0.2) # Prevent negative or zero weights

    # Apply alignment bonus to customer-to-customer edges
    H[1:, 1:] *= align_bonus

    # Long-edge penalty:
    # Softly penalize edges that are significantly longer than the median
    # customer-customer distance. This helps prevent long "jumps" that
    # might be favored by the inverse distance if they connect distant clusters
    # prematurely, but we keep it soft to allow necessary connections.
    dij = d[1:, 1:]
    finite_cd = dij[np.isfinite(dij)]
    if finite_cd.size:
        median_dist = float(np.median(finite_cd))
        if median_dist > eps:
            # Exponential decay for excess distance
            # Coefficient 0.2 is gentle
            excess = np.maximum(dij - median_dist, 0.0)
            penalty_factor = np.exp(-0.2 * (excess / median_dist))
            H[1:, 1:] *= penalty_factor

    # Capacity-awareness is handled naturally by the ACO algorithm's feasibility
    # checks during construction, so we avoid complex heuristic penalties here
    # to keep the heuristic smooth and focused on geometry and distance.

    # Sanitize
    H = np.nan_to_num(H, nan=eps, posinf=1e6, neginf=eps)
    H = np.maximum(H, eps)
    np.fill_diagonal(H, 0.0)

    return H
```

### 父代码

```python
import numpy as np

def heuristics(distance_matrix: np.ndarray, coordinates: np.ndarray, demands: np.ndarray, capacity: int) -> np.ndarray:
    n = distance_matrix.shape[0]
    if n <= 1:
        return np.ones((n, n), dtype=np.float64)

    eps = 1e-9
    d = np.asarray(distance_matrix, dtype=np.float64)

    # Sanitize distance matrix
    d = np.where(np.isfinite(d), d, 1.0)
    d = np.maximum(d, eps)

    # Base heuristic: Inverse distance with a sharper exponent to favor local edges
    # Exponent 1.3 is stronger than 1.2 in the previous version, reinforcing locality
    H = 1.0 / (d ** 1.3)
    np.fill_diagonal(H, 0.0)

    customers = np.arange(1, n)
    nc = len(customers)
    if nc == 0:
        H = np.nan_to_num(H, nan=eps, posinf=1e6, neginf=eps)
        H = np.maximum(H, eps)
        np.fill_diagonal(H, 0.0)
        row_sums = np.sum(H, axis=1, keepdims=True)
        row_sums = np.where(row_sums > eps, row_sums, 1.0)
        H = H / row_sums
        return H

    cust_idx = np.arange(1, n)
    cust_demands = np.asarray(demands[1:], dtype=np.float64)

    # Extract customer-to-customer submatrix
    dij = d[1:, 1:]
    d_i_depot = d[1:, 0]
    d_j_depot = d[1:, 0]

    # 1. Capacity-Aware Feasibility Factor
    # We estimate feasibility by checking if remaining capacity after visiting i and j
    # is sufficient relative to the distance back to depot.
    # Ratio = (Capacity - Demand_i - Demand_j) / Distance_j_depot
    # If ratio is low/negative, it suggests a capacity violation risk for the route segment.
    remaining_cap = capacity - cust_demands[:, None] - cust_demands[None, :]
    # Use d_j_depot as the relevant return distance for the move i->j
    ratio = remaining_cap / (d_j_depot[None, :] + eps)

    # Penalize low ratios strongly, reward high ratios mildly
    # Beta=2.0 for stronger penalty on infeasible edges compared to previous 1.5
    beta_feas = 2.0
    gamma_feas = 0.2
    penalty = np.exp(-beta_feas * np.maximum(1.0 - ratio, 0.0))
    bonus = 1.0 + gamma_feas * np.maximum(ratio - 1.0, 0.0)
    feas_factor = penalty * bonus
    feas_factor = np.clip(feas_factor, 0.01, 10.0)
    H[1:, 1:] *= feas_factor

    # 2. Mutual k-NN Boost
    # Reinforce local skeletons by boosting edges where i is in j's kNN and vice versa
    k = min(6, nc)
    if k > 1:
        dij_for_knn = dij.copy()
        np.fill_diagonal(dij_for_knn, np.inf)
        k_idx = np.argpartition(dij_for_knn, k - 1, axis=1)[:, :k]
        knn_mask = np.zeros((nc, nc), dtype=bool)
        rows = np.repeat(np.arange(nc), k)
        cols = k_idx.flatten()
        knn_mask[rows, cols] = True
        mutual = knn_mask & knn_mask.T
        # Boost factor 2.0 (previous was 1.6, 2.0 is a middle ground for strong locality)
        H[1:, 1:][mutual] *= 2.0

    # 3. Angular Alignment Bonus
    # Encourage sweep-like routes by aligning with radial vectors from depot
    coords = np.asarray(coordinates, dtype=np.float64)
    depot = coords[0]
    customer_coords = coords[1:]
    radial_vecs = customer_coords - depot
    radial_norms = np.linalg.norm(radial_vecs, axis=1)
    radial_norms[radial_norms < eps] = 1.0
    radial_unit = radial_vecs / radial_norms[:, None]
    dot_products = np.clip(radial_unit @ radial_unit.T, -1.0, 1.0)

    # Alpha=0.4 (previous was 0.3) to slightly strengthen angular preference
    alpha_align = 0.4
    align_bonus = 1.0 + alpha_align * dot_products
    align_bonus = np.maximum(align_bonus, 0.2)
    H[1:, 1:] *= align_bonus

    # 4. Long Edge Penalty
    # Penalize edges significantly longer than the median customer-customer distance
    finite_cd = dij[np.isfinite(dij)]
    if finite_cd.size:
        median_dist = float(np.median(finite_cd))
        if median_dist > eps:
            excess = np.maximum(dij - median_dist, 0.0)
            # Coefficient 0.4 (previous was 0.3) for slightly stronger suppression of long jumps
            penalty_factor = np.exp(-0.4 * (excess / median_dist))
            H[1:, 1:] *= penalty_factor

    # 5. Depot-to-Customer Bias
    # Bias towards high demand customers to improve packing efficiency
    max_demand = float(np.max(cust_demands)) if cust_demands.size else 0.0
    if max_demand > eps:
        norm_demand = cust_demands / max_demand
        # Demand bias coefficient 0.5 (same as previous, proven effective)
        demand_bias = 1.0 + 0.5 * norm_demand
        H[0, 1:] *= demand_bias

    # Core proximity bias: favor customers at typical distance from depot
    depot_dists = d[0, 1:]
    med_depot = float(np.median(depot_dists)) if depot_dists.size else 0.0
    if med_depot > eps:
        # Core factor: boost if closer to median, penalize if far
        core_factor = 1.0 + 0.2 * np.maximum(1.0 - np.abs(depot_dists - med_depot) / med_depot, 0.0)
        H[0, 1:] *= core_factor

    # 6. Customer-to-Depot Return Boost
    # Boost return edges for customers whose distance to depot is typical
    return_dist_safe = np.maximum(d[1:, 0], eps)
    med_return = float(np.median(return_dist_safe)) if return_dist_safe.size else 0.0
    if med_return > eps:
        # Gaussian-like boost centered on median return distance
        return_boost = 1.0 + 0.2 * np.maximum(1.0 - np.abs(return_dist_safe - med_return) / med_return, 0.0)
        H[1:, 0] *= return_boost

    # Sanitize and Normalize
    H = np.nan_to_num(H, nan=eps, posinf=1e6, neginf=eps)
    H = np.maximum(H, eps)
    np.fill_diagonal(H, 0.0)

    row_sums = np.sum(H, axis=1, keepdims=True)
    row_sums = np.where(row_sums > eps, row_sums, 1.0)
    H = H / row_sums

    return H
```

## 01 cvrp_aco_r1_e251

算子 Refine；节点 236；父节点 200；fitness -10.65419766959557；局部纠错：是。

增益：原版描述相对于下一客户返仓成本的容量项；二次版改成 depot→i 加 i→j 的 partial_cost，与代码一致。

保留问题或边界：仍把归一化讲成探索平衡改进；代码的 remaining_j 只有 C-demand_j，是静态代理量，不能按真实行程剩余容量理解。

<details><summary>原始 Idea、校准 Idea、最终代码与父代码</summary>

### 前置 Idea

The heuristic builds on the current algorithm's geometric sweep and local-structure idea, but simplifies it to be less over-fitted and numerically safer. It keeps the inverse-distance base $H_{ij}=d^{-1.2}$, a capacity-feasibility factor that penalizes edges that consume too much capacity relative to the next customer's return-to-depot cost, and a soft long-edge penalty relative to the median customer-customer distance. The angular alignment is kept but made milder and applied only as a continuous sweep bonus (no discrete tight-turn multiplier), and the mutual k-NN skeleton uses a moderate $k=5$ with a gentle 1.5× boost. Crucially, the depot-related edges are left to the base inverse-distance prior (no Gaussian median-distance biases that can inject noise), and the matrix is returned **row-normalized** so that relative edge strengths are preserved but the probability scale is stable for ACO sampling. This reduces over-regularization from noisy depot heuristics and sharp boosts while retaining strong local-sweep guidance that shortens routes.

### 校准 Idea

The final heuristic constructs an edge-prior matrix for the Capacitated Vehicle Routing Problem (CVRP) by integrating geometric, capacity, and topological signals into a row-normalized probability landscape. The algorithm begins with a base inverse-distance prior, $H_{ij} = d_{ij}^{-1.2}$, which inherently favors short edges. For customer-to-customer transitions, this base is modified by three distinct factors. First, a capacity-feasibility factor penalizes edges where the destination customer's demand consumes a significant portion of the remaining vehicle capacity relative to the accumulated route cost (depot-to-current plus current-to-destination). This is calculated using an exponential penalty $\exp(-\beta \max(1 - \rho, 0))$ and a small bonus for high remaining capacity ratios, where $\rho$ is the ratio of remaining capacity to partial cost. Second, a soft long-edge penalty dampens edges significantly longer than the median customer-customer distance using $\exp(-0.25 \cdot \text{excess})$. Third, a geometric sweep alignment factor, $1 + 0.3\cos(\theta)$, is applied to encourage edges that maintain a consistent angular direction relative to the depot, effectively smoothing routes and reducing backtracking. This alignment is floored at 0.4 to prevent zeroing out any edge. Additionally, a mutual k-Nearest Neighbor (k-NN) skeleton reinforcement is applied; edges appearing in the top-5 nearest neighbors of both endpoint nodes receive a 1.5× multiplicative boost, reinforcing local cluster structures. Depot-related edges are left to the base inverse-distance prior, avoiding complex biases that might introduce noise. Finally, the matrix is sanitized to ensure finite, positive values and row-normalized so that each row sums to 1, providing a stable probability scale for the ACO sampling mechanism.

Key implementation changes relative to the parent code include the removal of discrete tight-turn boosts and complex Gaussian depot biases. The parent code applied a discrete 1.8× multiplier to short, aligned edges and used median-distance Gaussian distributions to modulate depot edge desirability. The final code replaces these with a continuous, milder angular alignment factor ($0.3\cos\theta$ vs $0.4\cos\theta$ plus discrete boosts) and leaves depot edges as pure inverse-distance. This simplification reduces over-fitting to specific instance geometries. The k-NN parameter was increased from $k=4$ to $k=5$ with a slightly lower boost factor (1.5× vs 1.6×) to create a more robust local structure without aggressive reinforcement. Most critically, the final code introduces row-normalization of the heuristic matrix. While the parent code returned raw desirability scores, the final code normalizes each row to sum to 1. This ensures that the magnitude of the heuristic does not vary wildly between nodes with different numbers of feasible neighbors, providing a more stable input for the ACO probability calculation where the heuristic is typically combined with the pheromone matrix. This normalization helps maintain consistent exploration-exploitation balance across all nodes.

### 最终代码

```python
import numpy as np

def heuristics(distance_matrix: np.ndarray, coordinates: np.ndarray, demands: np.ndarray, capacity: int) -> np.ndarray:
    """Return edge desirability values for CVRP ant colony optimization.

    Args:
        distance_matrix: Pairwise Euclidean distances with shape (n, n).
        coordinates: Node coordinates with shape (n, 2). Node 0 is the depot.
        demands: Node demands with shape (n,). The depot demand is zero.
        capacity: Capacity shared by all vehicles.

    Returns:
        An (n, n) edge-prior matrix. Larger values make an edge more likely
        to be sampled. Values at or below zero are treated as 1e-9.
    """
    n = distance_matrix.shape[0]
    if n <= 1:
        return np.ones((n, n), dtype=np.float64)

    eps = 1e-9
    d = np.asarray(distance_matrix, dtype=np.float64)
    d = np.where(np.isfinite(d), d, 1.0)
    d = np.maximum(d, eps)

    # Base inverse-distance prior
    H = (1.0 / d) ** 1.2
    np.fill_diagonal(H, 0.0)

    nc = n - 1
    if nc == 0:
        H = np.nan_to_num(H, nan=eps, posinf=1e6, neginf=eps)
        H = np.maximum(H, eps)
        np.fill_diagonal(H, 0.0)
        return H

    cust_demands = np.asarray(demands[1:], dtype=np.float64)
    d_j_depot = d[1:, 0]      # customer -> depot distance
    d_i_depot = d[0, 1:]      # depot -> customer distance
    dij = d[1:, 1:]           # customer -> customer distances

    # --- Capacity-feasibility factor (customer -> customer) ---
    if capacity > 0:
        cap = float(capacity)
        # Remaining capacity after serving the destination customer j
        # (ant will next be at j, carrying j's demand toward the depot)
        remaining_j = cap - cust_demands[None, :]
        # Proxy for partial cost: depot->i plus i->j (route so far + move)
        partial_cost = d_i_depot[:, None] + dij
        ratio = remaining_j / (partial_cost + eps)
        beta_feas = 1.6
        gamma_feas = 0.15
        penalty = np.exp(-beta_feas * np.maximum(1.0 - ratio, 0.0))
        bonus = 1.0 + gamma_feas * np.maximum(ratio - 1.0, 0.0)
        feas = np.clip(penalty * bonus, 0.05, 5.0)
        H[1:, 1:] *= feas

    # --- Soft long-edge penalty ---
    tmp = dij.copy()
    np.fill_diagonal(tmp, np.inf)
    finite = tmp[np.isfinite(tmp)]
    if finite.size > 0:
        med = float(np.median(finite))
        if med > eps:
            excess = np.maximum(dij - med, 0.0) / med
            H[1:, 1:] *= np.exp(-0.25 * excess)

    # --- Angular sweep alignment (milder, continuous only) ---
    coords = np.asarray(coordinates, dtype=np.float64)
    if coords.shape[0] == n and coords.shape[1] >= 2:
        depot = coords[0]
        customer_coords = coords[1:]
        radial = customer_coords - depot
        norms = np.linalg.norm(radial, axis=1)
        norms = np.where(norms > eps, norms, 1.0)
        unit = radial / norms[:, None]
        cos_theta = np.clip(unit @ unit.T, -1.0, 1.0)

        # Milder alignment: 1.0 + 0.3*cos, floored at 0.4 (no discrete boost)
        align = 1.0 + 0.3 * cos_theta
        align = np.maximum(align, 0.4)
        H[1:, 1:] *= align

    # --- Mutual k-NN skeleton (k=5, gentle 1.5x boost) ---
    k = min(5, nc)
    if k > 1:
        dij_knn = dij.copy()
        np.fill_diagonal(dij_knn, np.inf)
        k_idx = np.argpartition(dij_knn, k - 1, axis=1)[:, :k]
        knn_mask = np.zeros((nc, nc), dtype=bool)
        rows = np.repeat(np.arange(nc), k)
        cols = k_idx.flatten()
        knn_mask[rows, cols] = True
        mutual = knn_mask & knn_mask.T
        if np.any(mutual):
            H[1:, 1:][mutual] *= 1.5

    # --- Depot edges: keep to base inverse-distance (no noisy biases) ---
    # H[0, 1:] and H[1:, 0] remain as (1/d)^1.2 from the base.

    # --- Sanitize and row-normalize ---
    H = np.nan_to_num(H, nan=eps, posinf=1e6, neginf=eps)
    H = np.maximum(H, eps)
    np.fill_diagonal(H, 0.0)

    # Row-normalize for stable probability scale (preserves relative strengths)
    row_sums = H.sum(axis=1, keepdims=True)
    row_sums = np.where(row_sums > eps, row_sums, 1.0)
    H = H / row_sums

    # Final safety: ensure finite and positive
    H = np.nan_to_num(H, nan=eps, posinf=1e6, neginf=eps)
    H = np.maximum(H, eps)
    np.fill_diagonal(H, 0.0)

    return H
```

### 父代码

```python
import numpy as np

def heuristics(distance_matrix: np.ndarray, coordinates: np.ndarray, demands: np.ndarray, capacity: int) -> np.ndarray:
    """Return edge desirability values for CVRP ant colony optimization."""
    n = distance_matrix.shape[0]
    if n <= 1:
        return np.ones((n, n), dtype=np.float64)

    eps = 1e-9
    d = np.asarray(distance_matrix, dtype=np.float64)
    d = np.where(np.isfinite(d), d, 1.0)
    d = np.maximum(d, eps)

    # Base: moderately sharp inverse distance
    H = (1.0 / d) ** 1.2
    np.fill_diagonal(H, 0.0)

    nc = n - 1
    if nc == 0:
        H = np.nan_to_num(H, nan=eps, posinf=1e6, neginf=eps)
        H = np.maximum(H, eps)
        np.fill_diagonal(H, 0.0)
        return H

    cust_demands = np.asarray(demands[1:], dtype=np.float64)
    d_j_depot = d[1:, 0]
    dij = d[1:, 1:]

    # Capacity feasibility for customer-to-customer transitions
    if capacity > 0:
        cap = float(capacity)
        remaining = cap - cust_demands[:, None] - cust_demands[None, :]
        ratio = remaining / (d_j_depot[None, :] + eps)
        beta_feas = 1.8
        gamma_feas = 0.20
        penalty = np.exp(-beta_feas * np.maximum(1.0 - ratio, 0.0))
        bonus = 1.0 + gamma_feas * np.maximum(ratio - 1.0, 0.0)
        feas = np.clip(penalty * bonus, 0.05, 6.0)
        H[1:, 1:] *= feas

    # Soft long-edge penalty (excess over median customer distance)
    tmp = dij.copy()
    np.fill_diagonal(tmp, np.inf)
    finite = tmp[np.isfinite(tmp)]
    if finite.size > 0:
        med = float(np.median(finite))
        if med > eps:
            excess = np.maximum(dij - med, 0.0) / med
            H[1:, 1:] *= np.exp(-0.25 * excess)

    # Geometric sweep alignment + tight-turn boost
    coords = np.asarray(coordinates, dtype=np.float64)
    if coords.shape[0] == n and coords.shape[1] >= 2:
        depot = coords[0]
        customer_coords = coords[1:]
        radial = customer_coords - depot
        norms = np.linalg.norm(radial, axis=1)
        norms = np.where(norms > eps, norms, 1.0)
        unit = radial / norms[:, None]
        cos_theta = np.clip(unit @ unit.T, -1.0, 1.0)

        # Continuous alignment: favor sweep-like edges, penalize backtracking
        align = 1.0 + 0.4 * cos_theta
        align = np.maximum(align, 0.25)
        H[1:, 1:] *= align

        # Discrete tight-turn boost for short, aligned edges
        if finite.size > 0:
            short_thr = float(np.percentile(finite, 30))
            is_short = dij < short_thr
            is_aligned = cos_theta > 0.5
            turn_boost = np.ones_like(dij)
            turn_boost[is_short & is_aligned] = 1.8
            H[1:, 1:] *= turn_boost

    # Gentle mutual k-NN skeleton reinforcement
    k = min(4, nc)
    if k > 1:
        dij_knn = dij.copy()
        np.fill_diagonal(dij_knn, np.inf)
        k_idx = np.argpartition(dij_knn, k - 1, axis=1)[:, :k]
        knn_mask = np.zeros((nc, nc), dtype=bool)
        rows = np.repeat(np.arange(nc), k)
        cols = k_idx.flatten()
        knn_mask[rows, cols] = True
        mutual = knn_mask & knn_mask.T
        if np.any(mutual):
            H[1:, 1:][mutual] *= 1.6

    # Depot edges: modest Gaussian-like preference for customers near median distance
    depot_dists = d[0, 1:]
    if depot_dists.size > 0:
        med_dep = float(np.median(depot_dists))
        if med_dep > eps:
            prox = np.exp(-0.5 * np.abs(depot_dists - med_dep) / med_dep)
            H[0, 1:] *= (0.8 + 0.4 * prox)

    if d_j_depot.size > 0:
        med_ret = float(np.median(d_j_depot))
        if med_ret > eps:
            ret = np.exp(-0.5 * np.abs(d_j_depot - med_ret) / med_ret)
            H[1:, 0] *= (0.8 + 0.4 * ret)

    # Sanitize
    H = np.nan_to_num(H, nan=eps, posinf=1e6, neginf=eps)
    H = np.maximum(H, eps)
    np.fill_diagonal(H, 0.0)

    return H
```

</details>

## 02 cvrp_aco_r2_e126

算子 Fuse；节点 113；父节点 89；fitness -10.161519891612167；局部纠错：未计入。

增益：展开了正确的乘法公式、需求项 0.02 及出仓行计算。

保留问题或边界：新增错误父代差异：声称父代是加权加法、子代才采用乘法；父代实际已有 heuristic = geo_component * modulation。

<details><summary>原始 Idea、校准 Idea、最终代码与父代码</summary>

### 前置 Idea

The heuristic blends the geometric strength of the reference (inverse-distance proximity, angular sweep continuity, and an estimated load-compatibility signal) with the demand-aware depot handling of the current algorithm, using a multiplicative gate so only short edges receive high desirability. I compute: (1) proximity P = (1/d_ij)/max(1/d), which rewards near neighbors without the harsh gating of 1/d^2 that suppressed valid mid-range moves; (2) Clarke‑Wright savings S_ij normalized to [0,1], capturing global route efficiency; (3) an angular continuity score A_ij = 1 - |θ_i - θ_j|/π for customer-customer edges, encouraging non-crossing sweep routes; and (4) a per-node demand fit D_j that peaks at half capacity (σ=0.30) to favor customers that fill the vehicle well. For customer-customer edges the modulation M = 0.40 + 0.30·S + 0.30·A (plus a small demand term) is multiplied by proximity. The depot row (route starts) is overridden to prefer nearby, high-demand customers (proximity × demand-fit, re-normalized), while all return-to-depot entries are clamped to 1e-9 so the ACO feasibility layer handles route termination; the matrix is normalized to max 1 and clamped to 1e-9 for stable sampling.

### 校准 Idea

The implemented heuristic constructs an edge-prior matrix by multiplicatively combining a geometric proximity signal with a structural modulation factor tailored for Capacitated Vehicle Routing Problem (CVRP) constraints. The core decision rule prioritizes short edges that maintain route coherence and efficient vehicle loading. First, the proximity component \( P_{ij} \) is calculated as the normalized inverse distance \( 1/d_{ij} \), scaled by the maximum inverse distance across the matrix. This replaces the parent’s inverse-square distance (\(1/d^2\)) with a milder linear inverse, preventing the harsh suppression of mid-range edges that can hinder valid routing. The structural modulation \( M_{ij} \) blends three normalized signals: Clarke-Wright savings \( S_{ij} \) (normalized to \([0,1]\)) to capture global route efficiency, angular continuity \( A_{ij} \) (computed as \(1 - \Delta\theta/\pi\) for customer-customer edges) to encourage non-crossing sweep patterns, and a demand-fit term \( D_j \) based on a Gaussian function centered at half vehicle capacity with a standard deviation of \(\sigma=0.30\). The modulation is defined as \( M_{ij} = 0.40 + 0.30 S_{ij} + 0.30 A_{ij} + 0.02 D_j \). The final heuristic value for customer-customer edges is \( H_{ij} = P_{ij} \times M_{ij} \).

Depot handling is explicitly customized to align with CVRP constraints. The row for the depot (route starts) is overridden to prefer customers that are both nearby and have high demand, calculated as \( P_{0j} \times (0.5 + 0.5 d_j/\max(d)) \times D_j \), then normalized to \([0,1]\). This ensures ants initially target high-value, easy-to-reach customers. Conversely, all columns for the depot (return-to-depot edges) are clamped to \(1e-9\) to prevent premature route termination, allowing the ACO feasibility layer to manage route breaks based on capacity. The matrix is finally normalized to a maximum value of 1, with diagonal elements and non-finite/low values clamped to \(1e-9\) for stable sampling. This design hypothesizes that balancing gentle geometric attraction with strong structural and demand-aware modulation will lead to shorter total route lengths by promoting efficient, capacity-respecting route formations.

The primary implementation changes relative to the parent code involve a shift from an additive weighted sum to a multiplicative gating mechanism. The parent code used an inverse-square distance component (\(1/d^2\)) and combined all signals (proximity, savings, angle, demand) additively with weights summing to 1.0, which could allow distant edges to receive non-negligible scores if their structural scores were high. The final code uses inverse distance (\(1/d\)) and multiplies the proximity by the modulation factor, ensuring that only short edges receive high desirability values regardless of their structural scores. Additionally, the demand fit Gaussian sigma was increased from 0.25 to 0.30, broadening the range of acceptable customer demands. The depot row was changed from a simple proximity-based normalization to a composite score including demand ratio and fit, explicitly prioritizing high-demand customers for route starts. Finally, the weights for the modulation components were adjusted: savings and angle were increased to 0.30 each, while the base weight was reduced to 0.40 and demand to 0.02, emphasizing structural coherence over raw demand magnitude for customer-customer transitions.

### 最终代码

```python
import numpy as np


def heuristics(distance_matrix: np.ndarray, coordinates: np.ndarray, demands: np.ndarray, capacity: int) -> np.ndarray:
    """Return edge desirability values for CVRP ant colony optimization.

    Args:
        distance_matrix: Pairwise Euclidean distances with shape (n, n).
        coordinates: Node coordinates with shape (n, 2). Node 0 is the depot.
        demands: Node demands with shape (n,). The depot demand is zero.
        capacity: Capacity shared by all vehicles.

    Returns:
        An (n, n) edge-prior matrix. Larger values make an edge more likely
        to be sampled. Values at or below zero are treated as 1e-9.
    """
    n = distance_matrix.shape[0]

    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64).reshape(0, 0)

    if n == 1:
        return np.array([[1.0]], dtype=np.float64)

    cap = float(max(int(capacity), 1))
    d = np.asarray(demands, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    d[0] = 0.0
    d = np.maximum(d, 0.0)

    # Safe distance matrix for inverse computations
    safe_dist = distance_matrix.copy().astype(np.float64)
    np.fill_diagonal(safe_dist, np.inf)
    safe_dist = np.maximum(safe_dist, 1e-10)

    # 1) Proximity: normalized inverse distance (milder than inverse-square)
    inv_d = 1.0 / safe_dist
    max_inv_d = np.max(inv_d)
    prox = inv_d / max_inv_d if max_inv_d > 0 else np.zeros_like(inv_d)

    # 2) Clarke-Wright savings, normalized to [0, 1]
    d0 = distance_matrix[0, :].astype(np.float64)
    savings = d0[:, np.newaxis] + d0[np.newaxis, :] - distance_matrix
    savings = np.maximum(savings, 0.0)
    max_sav = np.max(savings)
    savings_norm = savings / max_sav if max_sav > 0 else np.zeros_like(savings)

    # 3) Angular sweep continuity (customer-customer)
    angle_score = np.zeros((n, n), dtype=np.float64)
    if n > 1:
        cust_coords = np.asarray(coordinates)[1:]
        angles = np.arctan2(cust_coords[:, 1], cust_coords[:, 0])
        angle_diff = np.abs(angles[:, np.newaxis] - angles[np.newaxis, :])
        angle_diff = np.minimum(angle_diff, 2 * np.pi - angle_diff)
        angle_score[1:, 1:] = np.clip(1.0 - angle_diff / np.pi, 0.0, 1.0)

    # 4) Per-node demand fit: Gaussian centered at half capacity
    demand_ratio = d / cap
    sigma = 0.30
    demand_score = np.exp(-((demand_ratio - 0.5) ** 2) / (2 * sigma ** 2))

    # 5) Modulation factor (structural coherence), gated by proximity
    w_base = 0.40
    w_sav = 0.30
    w_ang = 0.30
    w_demand = 0.02

    modulation = (w_base
                  + w_sav * savings_norm
                  + w_ang * angle_score
                  + w_demand * demand_score[np.newaxis, :])
    modulation = np.maximum(modulation, 1e-6)

    heuristic = prox * modulation

    # Depot handling
    if n > 1:
        # Route starts: prefer nearby customers with strong demand fit
        d_cust = d[1:]
        max_d = np.max(d_cust)
        if max_d > 0:
            row0 = prox[0, 1:] * (0.5 + 0.5 * (d_cust / max_d)) * demand_score[1:]
        else:
            row0 = prox[0, 1:] * demand_score[1:]
        max_row0 = np.max(row0)
        if max_row0 > 0:
            row0 = row0 / max_row0
        heuristic[0, :] = 1e-9
        heuristic[0, 1:] = row0

        # Discourage premature return to the depot
        heuristic[1:, 0] = 1e-9

    np.fill_diagonal(heuristic, 1e-9)

    max_val = np.max(heuristic)
    if max_val > 0:
        heuristic = heuristic / max_val

    heuristic = np.where(np.isfinite(heuristic) & (heuristic > 1e-9), heuristic, 1e-9)
    return heuristic
```

### 父代码

```python
import numpy as np

def heuristics(distance_matrix: np.ndarray, coordinates: np.ndarray, demands: np.ndarray, capacity: int) -> np.ndarray:
    """Return edge desirability values for CVRP ant colony optimization.

    Args:
        distance_matrix: Pairwise Euclidean distances with shape (n, n).
        coordinates: Node coordinates with shape (n, 2). Node 0 is the depot.
        demands: Node demands with shape (n,). The depot demand is zero.
        capacity: Capacity shared by all vehicles.

    Returns:
        An (n, n) edge-prior matrix. Larger values make an edge more likely
        to be sampled. Values at or below zero are treated as 1e-9.
    """
    n = distance_matrix.shape[0]

    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64).reshape(0, 0)

    if n == 1:
        return np.array([[1.0]], dtype=np.float64)

    cap = float(max(int(capacity), 1))
    d = np.asarray(demands, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    d[0] = 0.0
    d = np.maximum(d, 0.0)

    safe_dist = distance_matrix.copy().astype(np.float64)
    np.fill_diagonal(safe_dist, np.inf)
    safe_dist = np.maximum(safe_dist, 1e-10)

    # Geometric component: inverse squared distance, normalized
    inv_d2 = 1.0 / (safe_dist ** 2)
    max_inv_d2 = np.max(inv_d2)
    if max_inv_d2 > 0:
        geo_component = inv_d2 / max_inv_d2
    else:
        geo_component = np.zeros_like(inv_d2)

    # Clarke-Wright savings component
    d0 = distance_matrix[0, :].astype(np.float64)
    savings = d0[:, np.newaxis] + d0[np.newaxis, :] - distance_matrix
    savings = np.maximum(savings, 0.0)
    max_savings = np.max(savings)
    if max_savings > 0:
        savings_norm = savings / max_savings
    else:
        savings_norm = np.zeros_like(savings)

    # Angular sweep component
    if coordinates.shape[0] > 1:
        angles = np.arctan2(coordinates[:, 1], coordinates[:, 0])
        angle_diff = np.abs(angles[:, np.newaxis] - angles[np.newaxis, :])
        angle_diff = np.minimum(angle_diff, 2 * np.pi - angle_diff)
        angle_score = 1.0 - (angle_diff / np.pi)
        angle_score = np.clip(angle_score, 0.0, 1.0)
    else:
        angle_score = np.ones((n, n), dtype=np.float64)

    # Demand score: Gaussian centered at half capacity
    demand_ratio = d / cap
    sigma = 0.25
    demand_score = np.exp(-((demand_ratio - 0.5) ** 2) / (2 * sigma ** 2))
    demand_score = demand_score / (1.0 + 1e-10)

    # Combine components with weights
    w_base = 0.5
    w_savings = 0.25
    w_angle = 0.15
    w_demand = 0.1

    demand_vec = demand_score[np.newaxis, :]
    modulation = w_base + w_savings * savings_norm + w_angle * angle_score + w_demand * demand_vec
    modulation = np.maximum(modulation, 1e-6)

    heuristic = geo_component * modulation

    # Handle depot: zero out incoming edges to prevent premature termination
    if n > 1:
        heuristic[:, 0] = 1e-9

    # Normalize
    max_val = np.max(heuristic)
    if max_val > 0:
        heuristic = heuristic / max_val

    # Set diagonal and ensure finite positive values
    np.fill_diagonal(heuristic, 1e-9)
    heuristic = np.where(np.isfinite(heuristic) & (heuristic > 1e-9), heuristic, 1e-9)

    return heuristic
```

## 03 cvrp_aco_r2_e259

算子 Refine；节点 233；父节点 228；fitness -9.375135474575094；局部纠错：是。

增益：原版声称出仓偏置同时使用距离和角度；二次版改为更近客户的距离偏置。

保留问题或边界：新描述把有效系数说成 0.3，实际 starter_bonus 先乘 0.3，组合时又乘 w_start=0.3，合计 0.09；可行需求奖励随 cap_ratio 增长，不能解释为远离容量上限。

<details><summary>原始 Idea、校准 Idea、最终代码与父代码</summary>

### 前置 Idea

Build on the current multiplicative model (proximity gate × structural modulation × capacity penalty) but fix its two main weaknesses: (1) the depot-to-customer edges are left flat (savings/angular are zeroed out), so the ACO has no guidance on which customers to start with; (2) the capacity penalty only penalizes pairs whose *combined* demand exceeds capacity, ignoring the actual vehicle-load state during construction. I replace these with (a) an angular-sweep bias for starting edges that rewards customers near the depot *and* with consistent angular orientation, and (b) a softer, demand-ratio-aware capacity modulation that gently discourages pairs likely to waste capacity while keeping short, feasible edges attractive. I also tune the savings/angle weights and normalize more robustly to keep values well-scaled for ACO sampling.

### 校准 Idea

The implemented heuristic constructs an edge desirability matrix for Ant Colony Optimization on the Capacitated Vehicle Routing Problem by multiplicatively combining three distinct factors: a geometric proximity gate, a structural modulation factor, and a capacity-based constraint score. The primary decision rule relies on the inverse square of the Euclidean distance ($1/d^2$) to prioritize short edges, which are normalized to a range of $[0, 1]$ by dividing by the maximum proximity value in the matrix. This proximity term serves as the base attractiveness, ensuring that ants naturally prefer adjacent nodes.

To guide route construction, the method calculates a structural modulation factor that integrates Clarke-Wright savings, angular continuity, and a depot-start bias. Clarke-Wright savings are computed as $d_{0i} + d_{0j} - d_{ij}$, measuring the cost reduction of linking customers $i$ and $j$ directly versus via the depot. These savings are normalized to $[0, 1]$ and weighted with a coefficient of 1.5, significantly emphasizing high-savings connections. Angular continuity is modeled using the cosine similarity of vectors from the depot to each customer; the score is mapped to $[0, 1]$ and weighted at 0.6, encouraging ants to follow spatially coherent paths that minimize turning. A specific depot-start bias is added to the first row (edges from the depot), rewarding customers that are relatively close to the depot. This is calculated by normalizing the depot-to-customer distances and applying a coefficient of 0.3, providing guidance for route initialization where the parent code offered no specific directional preference. The modulation factor is expressed as $1.0 + 1.5 \cdot S_{norm} + 0.6 \cdot A_{score} + 0.3 \cdot B_{start}$, where the base value of 1.0 ensures that even edges with zero savings or angle score retain some probability mass.

The capacity constraint is handled via a Gaussian-like decay function that penalizes pairs of nodes whose combined demand exceeds the vehicle capacity. The penalty is calculated as $\exp(-(excess^2 / (2 \cdot \sigma^2)))$, where $excess$ is the amount by which the combined demand ratio exceeds 1, and $\sigma$ is set to $0.4 \times capacity$. Unlike hard constraints, this soft penalty gradually reduces the desirability of infeasible transitions. Additionally, a small positive bonus ($0.1 \times$ demand ratio) is added to feasible pairs (demand ratio $\le 1$), creating a subtle preference for edges that utilize capacity efficiently without approaching the limit. The final heuristic is the product of the normalized proximity, modulation, and capacity score. Values at or below zero are clamped to $1e-9$, and edges returning to the depot or self-loops are explicitly suppressed to maintain route integrity. This multiplicative structure ensures that no single factor can dominate the others, balancing geometric efficiency, route structure, and capacity feasibility.

**Important Implementation Changes Relative to Parent Code:**
1.  **Depot-Start Bias Introduction:** The parent code left depot-to-customer edges with zero angular/savings bias, effectively treating all starting customers equally based only on distance. The final code introduces a `starter_bonus` term specifically for row 0, rewarding closer customers with a weighted coefficient of 0.3. This provides explicit guidance for the first move of each route.
2.  **Softened Capacity Penalty:** The parent code used a linear exponential decay $\exp(-excess/\sigma)$ with a fixed sigma of $0.3 \times capacity$. The final code switches to a Gaussian decay $\exp(-excess^2 / (2\sigma^2))$ with a larger sigma of $0.4 \times capacity$, which is less aggressive for slightly infeasible pairs but smoother overall. Furthermore, the final code adds a `feasible_bonus` of $0.1 \times cap\_ratio$ for feasible pairs, which the parent code lacked. This encourages the ACO to prefer edges that fit comfortably within the capacity rather than just avoiding infeasible ones.
3.  **Weight Tuning:** The weights for savings ($w_{sav}$) were increased from 1.2 to 1.5, and angular scores ($w_{ang}$) from 0.5 to 0.6. This shifts the balance toward stronger structural preferences (savings and angle) over pure proximity, potentially improving route coherence.

### 最终代码

```python
import numpy as np

def heuristics(distance_matrix: np.ndarray, coordinates: np.ndarray, demands: np.ndarray, capacity: int) -> np.ndarray:
    """Return edge desirability values for CVRP ant colony optimization.

    Args:
        distance_matrix: Pairwise Euclidean distances with shape (n, n).
        coordinates: Node coordinates with shape (n, 2). Node 0 is the depot.
        demands: Node demands with shape (n,). The depot demand is zero.
        capacity: Capacity shared by all vehicles.

    Returns:
        An (n, n) edge-prior matrix. Larger values make an edge more likely
        to be sampled. Values at or below zero are treated as 1e-9.
    """
    n = distance_matrix.shape[0]

    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64).reshape(0, 0)

    if n == 1:
        return np.array([[1e-9]], dtype=np.float64)

    dist = np.asarray(distance_matrix, dtype=np.float64)
    coords = np.asarray(coordinates, dtype=np.float64)
    dem = np.asarray(demands, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    dem[0] = 0.0
    dem = np.maximum(dem, 0.0)

    cap = float(max(int(capacity), 1))

    # --- Geometric proximity gate: 1/d^2, normalized to [0,1] ---
    safe_dist = np.maximum(dist, 1e-10)
    prox = 1.0 / (safe_dist ** 2)
    np.fill_diagonal(prox, 0.0)

    max_prox = np.max(prox)
    if max_prox > 0:
        prox_norm = prox / max_prox
    else:
        prox_norm = np.zeros_like(prox)

    # --- Clarke-Wright savings, normalized to [0,1] ---
    d0 = dist[0, :]
    savings = d0[:, np.newaxis] + d0[np.newaxis, :] - dist
    savings = np.maximum(savings, 0.0)
    np.fill_diagonal(savings, 0.0)

    max_sav = np.max(savings)
    if max_sav > 0:
        sav_norm = savings / max_sav
    else:
        sav_norm = np.zeros_like(savings)

    # --- Angular sweep continuity via cosine similarity of depot-relative vectors ---
    vecs = coords - coords[0]
    norms = np.linalg.norm(vecs, axis=1)

    ang_score = np.zeros((n, n), dtype=np.float64)
    valid = norms > 1e-10

    if np.any(valid):
        u = np.zeros_like(vecs)
        u[valid] = vecs[valid] / norms[valid, np.newaxis]
        cos_sim = u @ u.T
        ang_score = 0.5 * (1.0 + cos_sim)

    np.fill_diagonal(ang_score, 0.0)
    # Zero out depot-related angular scores (undefined/meaningless)
    ang_score[0, :] = 0.0
    ang_score[:, 0] = 0.0

    # --- Depot-start bias: reward nearby customers with consistent angular direction ---
    # For row 0, add a boost based on angular proximity to "nearby cluster" customers
    # and a small distance-based starter preference.
    starter_bonus = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n > 1:
        # Prefer customers that are relatively close to depot (shorter initial trip)
        d0_norm = d0.copy()
        d0_norm[0] = 0
        max_d0 = np.max(d0_norm[1:]) if n > 1 else 1.0
        if max_d0 > 0:
            starter_bonus = 1.0 - (d0_norm / max_d0)
        # Also favor customers with larger angular spread (more "useful" starters)
        # This is a soft heuristic; keep it small
        starter_bonus = 0.3 * starter_bonus
    # Apply starter bonus to row 0 edges
    starter_bonus_matrix = np.zeros((n, n), dtype=np.float64)
    starter_bonus_matrix[0, :] = starter_bonus

    # --- Capacity modulation: soft penalty for pairs that are hard to fit ---
    # Use a softer, more informative capacity signal: penalize based on how "tight"
    # the pair is relative to capacity, but don't zero out feasible pairs.
    combined_demand = dem[:, np.newaxis] + dem[np.newaxis, :]
    # For pairs where combined demand <= cap, give a small positive signal
    # For pairs where combined demand > cap, apply exponential decay
    cap_ratio = combined_demand / cap
    # Soft penalty: exp(-(excess)^2 / (2 * sigma^2)) for infeasible, mild boost for feasible
    excess = np.maximum(cap_ratio - 1.0, 0.0)
    sigma = 0.4 * cap
    cap_score = np.exp(-excess ** 2 / (2 * sigma ** 2))
    # For feasible pairs (cap_ratio <= 1), add a small utilization bonus
    feasible_bonus = np.where(cap_ratio <= 1.0, 0.1 * cap_ratio, 0.0)
    cap_score = cap_score + feasible_bonus

    # --- Combine into modulation factor ---
    w_sav = 1.5
    w_ang = 0.6
    w_start = 0.3
    base = 1.0

    modulation = base + w_sav * sav_norm + w_ang * ang_score + w_start * starter_bonus_matrix

    # --- Final heuristic: multiplicative combination ---
    final_heuristic = prox_norm * modulation * cap_score

    # --- Handle depot edges explicitly ---
    # Edges returning to depot (column 0) should be suppressed to encourage
    # ants to continue building routes before returning.
    if n > 1:
        final_heuristic[:, 0] = 1e-9
        np.fill_diagonal(final_heuristic, 1e-9)

        # Normalize to [0, 1]
        max_val = np.max(final_heuristic)
        if max_val > 0:
            final_heuristic = final_heuristic / max_val
        else:
            final_heuristic = np.ones_like(final_heuristic) * 1e-9

        # Re-apply constraints after normalization
        final_heuristic[:, 0] = 1e-9
        np.fill_diagonal(final_heuristic, 1e-9)

    # Clamp to ensure finite, positive values
    final_heuristic = np.where(
        np.isfinite(final_heuristic) & (final_heuristic > 1e-9),
        final_heuristic,
        1e-9
    )

    if n > 1:
        final_heuristic[:, 0] = 1e-9
        np.fill_diagonal(final_heuristic, 1e-9)

    return final_heuristic
```

### 父代码

```python
import numpy as np

def heuristics(distance_matrix: np.ndarray, coordinates: np.ndarray, demands: np.ndarray, capacity: int) -> np.ndarray:
    """Return edge desirability values for CVRP ant colony optimization.

    Args:
        distance_matrix: Pairwise Euclidean distances with shape (n, n).
        coordinates: Node coordinates with shape (n, 2). Node 0 is the depot.
        demands: Node demands with shape (n,). The depot demand is zero.
        capacity: Capacity shared by all vehicles.

    Returns:
        An (n, n) edge-prior matrix. Larger values make an edge more likely
        to be sampled. Values at or below zero are treated as 1e-9.
    """
    n = distance_matrix.shape[0]

    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64).reshape(0, 0)

    if n == 1:
        return np.array([[1e-9]], dtype=np.float64)

    dist = np.asarray(distance_matrix, dtype=np.float64)
    coords = np.asarray(coordinates, dtype=np.float64)
    dem = np.asarray(demands, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    dem[0] = 0.0
    dem = np.maximum(dem, 0.0)

    cap = float(max(int(capacity), 1))

    # 1. Geometric proximity gate: 1/d^2 normalized
    safe_dist = np.maximum(dist, 1e-10)
    prox = 1.0 / (safe_dist ** 2)
    np.fill_diagonal(prox, 0.0)

    max_prox = np.max(prox)
    if max_prox > 0:
        prox_norm = prox / max_prox
    else:
        prox_norm = np.zeros_like(prox)

    # 2. Clarke-Wright Savings
    d0 = dist[0, :]
    savings = d0[:, np.newaxis] + d0[np.newaxis, :] - dist
    savings = np.maximum(savings, 0.0)
    np.fill_diagonal(savings, 0.0)

    max_sav = np.max(savings)
    if max_sav > 0:
        sav_norm = savings / max_sav
    else:
        sav_norm = np.zeros_like(savings)

    # 3. Angular Sweep (Cosine similarity of vectors from depot)
    # We only care about customers (1..n-1), depot vector is 0
    vecs = coords - coords[0]
    norms = np.linalg.norm(vecs, axis=1)

    ang_score = np.zeros((n, n), dtype=np.float64)
    valid = norms > 1e-10

    if np.any(valid):
        u = np.zeros_like(vecs)
        # Normalize only valid vectors
        u[valid] = vecs[valid] / norms[valid, np.newaxis]

        # Cosine similarity matrix
        cos_sim = u @ u.T
        # Map to [0, 1]
        ang_score = 0.5 * (1.0 + cos_sim)

    np.fill_diagonal(ang_score, 0.0)
    # Ensure depot row/col is 0 for angle component
    ang_score[0, :] = 0.0
    ang_score[:, 0] = 0.0

    # 4. Capacity Penalty
    # Penalize if d_i + d_j > capacity
    # Using a soft exponential penalty
    combined_demand = dem[:, np.newaxis] + dem[np.newaxis, :]
    excess = np.maximum(combined_demand - cap, 0.0)
    sigma_cap = 0.3 * cap  # Softness parameter
    if sigma_cap < 1e-6:
        sigma_cap = 1.0
    cap_score = np.exp(-excess / sigma_cap)

    # Diagonal and depot handling for cap score should be neutral (1.0) or handled by final zeroing
    # Actually, if d_i + d_j > cap, we penalize. This is fine.

    # 5. Combine
    # We want: prox * (w_base + w_sav*sav + w_ang*ang) * cap_score
    # The previous successful version used:
    # mod = 1.0 + 1.2 * sav_norm + 0.5 * ang_score
    # final = prox_norm * mod * cap_score

    w_sav = 1.2
    w_ang = 0.5
    base = 1.0

    modulation = base + w_sav * sav_norm + w_ang * ang_score

    final_heuristic = prox_norm * modulation * cap_score

    # 6. Post-processing
    if n > 1:
        # Suppress returns to depot (column 0)
        final_heuristic[:, 0] = 1e-9
        # Suppress self-loops
        np.fill_diagonal(final_heuristic, 1e-9)

        # Normalize to [0, 1]
        max_val = np.max(final_heuristic)
        if max_val > 0:
            final_heuristic = final_heuristic / max_val

    # Clamp to finite values and minimum 1e-9
    final_heuristic = np.where(np.isfinite(final_heuristic) & (final_heuristic > 1e-9), final_heuristic, 1e-9)

    # Re-apply constraints after clamping/normalization just in case
    if n > 1:
        final_heuristic[:, 0] = 1e-9
        np.fill_diagonal(final_heuristic, 1e-9)

    return final_heuristic
```

</details>

## 04 cvrp_aco_r3_e173

算子 Fuse；节点 164；父节点 148；fitness -9.844676101488094；局部纠错：是。

增益：二次版明确指出可见父代本来就只有 hard mask，识别了前置 Idea 所称“移除 soft capacity penalty”与父代码不符。

保留问题或边界：这是有价值的核对，同时仍附带对不可见历史的推测；最终公式与主要差异描述基本对应。

<details><summary>原始 Idea、校准 Idea、最终代码与父代码</summary>

### 前置 Idea

The heuristic combines a moderate inverse-square distance decay with a density-adaptive angular sweep bias to favor short, non-crossing routes. For each customer, a local angular density is computed based on the minimum angular gap to neighbors, mapping to a lambda in [1.5, 4.0]. Unlike previous iterations that used `min` or `max` of the pair's lambdas, this design uses the geometric mean for a balanced constraint strength. Crucially, it removes distance-based lambda relaxation (which caused zig-zags in Step 6/8) and removes soft capacity penalties (which suppressed high-value edges). Instead, it strictly applies hard capacity feasibility masks and normalizes the matrix such that the mean heuristic weight is 1.0, stabilizing the probability distribution in ACO sampling.

### 校准 Idea

The implemented heuristic generates an edge-prior matrix by multiplying a normalized inverse-square distance component with a density-adaptive angular sweep factor, subject to hard capacity feasibility constraints. First, the algorithm computes a base desirability for each edge $(i, j)$ as $1/d_{ij}^2$, normalized by the maximum value in the matrix to scale the range to $[0, 1]$. This provides a strong locality bias, favoring shorter edges over longer ones. Second, the algorithm calculates an angular preference based on the relative orientation of vectors from the depot to nodes $i$ and $j$. It computes the cosine of the angle between these vectors, $c_{ij} = \mathbf{u}_i \cdot \mathbf{u}_j$, clamped to $[-0.5, 1.0]$. To adapt the strength of this angular bias to local customer density, the algorithm determines the minimum angular gap to neighboring customers for each customer $i$. This gap is mapped to a local $\lambda_i$ in $[1.5, 4.0]$, where dense regions (small gaps) yield higher $\lambda$ (strict sweep preference) and sparse regions yield lower $\lambda$ (flexible movement). For any customer-to-customer edge, the effective lambda is the geometric mean $\sqrt{\lambda_i \lambda_j}$, balancing the strictness of the two endpoints. The angular factor is then $\exp(\lambda_{ij} c_{ij})$, which favors edges that continue the current angular direction (non-crossing routes) and penalizes reversals. Depot edges are assigned a neutral angular factor of 1.0 to allow flexibility in route start and end. Finally, the product of the distance and angular components is masked to zero if the sum of demands of the two nodes exceeds the vehicle capacity, or if the edge is self-looping. The resulting matrix is normalized such that the mean of all positive entries is 1.0, ensuring a stable probability distribution when combined with pheromone values in the ACO sampling mechanism. Values below a floor of $10^{-9}$ are set to this floor to prevent numerical underflow.

The important implementation changes relative to the parent code are as follows:
1.  **Distance Decay Exponent:** The parent code used an inverse cubic distance decay ($1/d^3$), while the final code uses inverse square ($1/d^2$). This reduces the extremity of the preference for very short edges, potentially allowing for slightly more exploratory behavior for medium-length edges.
2.  **Lambda Calculation:** The parent code used the maximum of the two local lambdas ($\max(\lambda_i, \lambda_j)$) for the edge's angular bias, whereas the final code uses the geometric mean ($\sqrt{\lambda_i \lambda_j}$). This provides a more balanced constraint strength, preventing overly strict sweep behavior in edges connecting dense and sparse clusters.
3.  **Removal of Distance-Conditional Relaxation:** The parent code included a mechanism to relax the angular penalty for long edges (multiplying $\lambda$ by $(1 - \alpha \cdot d_{norm})$), which the design idea noted caused zig-zags. The final code removes this relaxation, applying the full angular bias consistently to all customer-to-customer edges.
4.  **Removal of Soft Capacity Penalties:** The parent code implicitly handled capacity through a hard mask, but the design idea mentions removing "soft capacity penalties" that suppressed high-value edges. The final code strictly uses only a hard binary mask for capacity feasibility, as the parent code did, but the context implies the parent might have had additional soft suppression not present in the final code or the design idea refers to a previous iteration not shown. The key change highlighted is the focus on hard masks only.
5.  **Normalization Strategy:** The parent code normalized by the minimum positive value (`H = H / min_h`), which can lead to very large values. The final code normalizes by the mean of positive values (`H = H / mean_h`), resulting in a more stable and bounded magnitude for the heuristic matrix, which is better for probabilistic sampling in ACO.

### 最终代码

```python
import numpy as np

def heuristics(distance_matrix: np.ndarray, coordinates: np.ndarray, demands: np.ndarray, capacity: int) -> np.ndarray:
    """Return edge desirability values for CVRP ant colony optimization.

    Args:
        distance_matrix: Pairwise Euclidean distances with shape (n, n).
        coordinates: Node coordinates with shape (n, 2). Node 0 is the depot.
        demands: Node demands with shape (n,). The depot demand is zero.
        capacity: Capacity shared by all vehicles.

    Returns:
        An (n, n) edge-prior matrix. Larger values make an edge more likely
        to be sampled. Values at or below zero are treated as 1e-9.
    """
    n = distance_matrix.shape[0]
    if n <= 1:
        return np.full((n, n), 1e-9, dtype=float)

    eps = 1e-12
    floor_val = 1e-9

    # 1. Distance Component: Inverse square distance, normalized to [0, 1]
    # This provides a strong but not overly aggressive preference for short edges.
    dist_safe = np.where(distance_matrix > eps, distance_matrix, 1.0)
    dist_base = 1.0 / (dist_safe ** 2)
    max_dist_base = np.max(dist_base)
    if max_dist_base > eps:
        dist_base = dist_base / max_dist_base
    else:
        dist_base = np.zeros_like(dist_base)

    # 2. Angular Component: Density-adaptive sweep bias
    depot = coordinates[0]
    vecs = coordinates - depot
    mags = np.linalg.norm(vecs, axis=1)

    # Avoid division by zero for depot
    safe_mags = np.where(mags > eps, mags, 1.0)
    unit_vecs = vecs / safe_mags[:, np.newaxis]
    # Ensure depot vector is zero (it is, since vecs[0]=0) but handle numerical noise
    unit_vecs[0] = 0.0

    cos_mat = unit_vecs @ unit_vecs.T
    # Clamp cosine to avoid extreme exponentials, but allow some backtracking
    cos_mat = np.clip(cos_mat, -0.5, 1.0)

    num_customers = n - 1
    lambda_matrix = np.ones((n, n), dtype=float)

    if num_customers > 1:
        angles = np.arctan2(unit_vecs[:, 1], unit_vecs[:, 0])
        customer_angles = angles[1:]

        a_i = customer_angles[:, np.newaxis]
        a_j = customer_angles[np.newaxis, :]
        diff = np.abs(a_i - a_j)
        diff = np.minimum(diff, 2 * np.pi - diff)
        np.fill_diagonal(diff, np.inf)

        min_angular_diff = np.min(diff, axis=1)

        # Map angular density to lambda.
        # Dense regions (small diff) -> high lambda (strict sweep).
        # Sparse regions (large diff) -> low lambda (flexible).
        # Thresholds: pi/8 (dense) to pi/4 (sparse)
        thresh_sparse = np.pi / 4
        thresh_dense = np.pi / 8
        t = (thresh_sparse - min_angular_diff) / (thresh_sparse - thresh_dense)
        t = np.clip(t, 0.0, 1.0)

        lambda_min = 1.5
        lambda_max = 4.0
        local_lambdas = lambda_min + t * (lambda_max - lambda_min)

        # Geometric mean of the two local lambdas for the edge.
        # This balances the strictness: if one node is dense and one is sparse,
        # the constraint is moderate.
        lc = local_lambdas[:, np.newaxis]
        lc_t = local_lambdas[np.newaxis, :]
        # Avoid log(0), though lambdas are >= 1.5
        pair_lambda = np.sqrt(lc * lc_t)

        lambda_matrix[1:, 1:] = pair_lambda

    # Neutralize depot edges: angular bias should not affect entering/exiting routes
    lambda_matrix[0, :] = lambda_min
    lambda_matrix[:, 0] = lambda_min
    np.fill_diagonal(lambda_matrix, 1.0)

    # Compute angular factor
    angular = np.exp(lambda_matrix * cos_mat)

    # Ensure depot edges have neutral angular weight (1.0) regardless of lambda
    angular[0, :] = 1.0
    angular[:, 0] = 1.0
    np.fill_diagonal(angular, 1.0)

    # 3. Combine Components
    H = dist_base * angular

    # 4. Capacity Constraints (Hard Mask)
    if capacity > 0:
        d = demands.astype(float)
        pair_demand = d[:, np.newaxis] + d[np.newaxis, :]
        infeasible = pair_demand > float(capacity)
        infeasible |= (np.arange(n)[:, np.newaxis] == np.arange(n)[np.newaxis, :])
        H[infeasible] = 0.0

    np.fill_diagonal(H, 0.0)
    H = np.where(np.isfinite(H), H, 0.0)

    # 5. Normalization
    # Normalize such that the mean of positive values is 1.0.
    # This keeps the heuristic magnitude stable relative to pheromones.
    positive = H[H > 0]
    if len(positive) > 0:
        mean_h = np.mean(positive)
        if mean_h > eps:
            H = H / mean_h

    H = np.maximum(H, floor_val)
    return H
```

### 父代码

```python
import numpy as np

def heuristics(distance_matrix: np.ndarray, coordinates: np.ndarray, demands: np.ndarray, capacity: int) -> np.ndarray:
    """Return edge desirability values for CVRP ant colony optimization.

    Args:
        distance_matrix: Pairwise Euclidean distances with shape (n, n).
        coordinates: Node coordinates with shape (n, 2). Node 0 is the depot.
        demands: Node demands with shape (n,). The depot demand is zero.
        capacity: Capacity shared by all vehicles.

    Returns:
        An (n, n) edge-prior matrix. Larger values make an edge more likely
        to be sampled. Values at or below zero are treated as 1e-9.
    """
    n = distance_matrix.shape[0]
    if n <= 1:
        return np.full((n, n), 1.0, dtype=float)

    eps = 1e-12
    floor_val = 1e-9

    # 1. Distance Component: Inverse cubic for strong locality bias
    dist_safe = np.where(distance_matrix > eps, distance_matrix, 1.0)
    dist_base = 1.0 / (dist_safe ** 3)
    max_dist_base = np.max(dist_base)
    if max_dist_base > eps:
        dist_base = dist_base / max_dist_base

    # 2. Angular Component Preparation
    depot = coordinates[0]
    vecs = coordinates - depot
    mags = np.linalg.norm(vecs, axis=1)

    safe_vecs = np.where(mags[:, np.newaxis] > eps, vecs, 0.0)
    safe_mags = np.where(mags > eps, mags, 1.0)
    unit_vecs = safe_vecs / safe_mags[:, np.newaxis]

    cos_mat = unit_vecs @ unit_vecs.T
    cos_mat = np.clip(cos_mat, -0.5, 1.0)

    # 3. Adaptive Lambda based on Local Angular Density
    num_customers = n - 1
    lambda_min = 1.0
    lambda_max = 6.0

    if num_customers > 1:
        angles = np.arctan2(unit_vecs[:, 1], unit_vecs[:, 0])
        customer_angles = angles[1:]

        a_i = customer_angles[:, np.newaxis]
        a_j = customer_angles[np.newaxis, :]
        diff = np.abs(a_i - a_j)
        diff = np.minimum(diff, 2 * np.pi - diff)
        np.fill_diagonal(diff, np.inf)

        min_angular_diff = np.min(diff, axis=1)

        # Map angular gap to lambda: Dense (small gap) -> High Lambda, Sparse -> Low Lambda
        thresh_sparse = np.pi / 4
        thresh_dense = np.pi / 12

        # t is 1 if very dense, 0 if sparse
        t = (thresh_sparse - min_angular_diff) / (thresh_sparse - thresh_dense)
        t = np.clip(t, 0.0, 1.0)

        local_lambdas = lambda_min + t * (lambda_max - lambda_min)

        # Use max of the two nodes' lambdas to ensure dense clusters maintain strict sweep structure
        lc = local_lambdas[:, np.newaxis]
        lc_t = local_lambdas[np.newaxis, :]
        lambda_matrix = np.full((n, n), lambda_min, dtype=float)
        lambda_matrix[1:, 1:] = np.maximum(lc, lc_t)
    else:
        lambda_matrix = np.full((n, n), lambda_min, dtype=float)

    # 4. Distance-Conditional Relaxation
    # Relax angular penalty for long edges to allow inter-cluster connections
    min_dist = np.min(dist_safe)
    max_dist = np.max(dist_safe)
    if max_dist > min_dist:
        d_norm = (dist_safe - min_dist) / (max_dist - min_dist)
    else:
        d_norm = np.zeros_like(dist_safe)

    # alpha_relax controls how quickly the angular constraint fades for longer edges
    alpha_relax = 0.6
    lambda_effective = lambda_matrix * (1.0 - alpha_relax * d_norm)
    # Keep a minimum angular bias even for very long edges to discourage crossing
    lambda_effective = np.maximum(lambda_effective, 0.5)

    # Neutralize depot edges: route start/end should be driven by distance/pheromone, not sweep
    lambda_effective[0, :] = lambda_min
    lambda_effective[:, 0] = lambda_min
    # Diagonal doesn't matter as it's zeroed out later, but keep it safe
    np.fill_diagonal(lambda_effective, lambda_min)

    angular = np.exp(lambda_effective * cos_mat)

    # Ensure depot edges have neutral angular weight
    angular[0, :] = 1.0
    angular[:, 0] = 1.0
    np.fill_diagonal(angular, 1.0)

    H = dist_base * angular

    # 5. Capacity Constraints (Hard Mask)
    if capacity > 0:
        d = demands.astype(float)
        pair_demand = d[:, np.newaxis] + d[np.newaxis, :]
        infeasible = pair_demand > float(capacity)
        infeasible |= (np.arange(n)[:, np.newaxis] == np.arange(n)[np.newaxis, :])
        H[infeasible] = 0.0

    np.fill_diagonal(H, 0.0)
    H = np.where(np.isfinite(H), H, 0.0)

    # 6. Normalization
    positive = H[H > 0]
    if len(positive) > 0:
        min_h = np.min(positive)
        if min_h > eps:
            H = H / min_h

    H = np.maximum(H, floor_val)
    return H
```
