## 12 op_aco_r1_e99

算子 Fuse；节点 97；父节点 22；fitness 13.544；局部纠错：未计入。

增益：补充正确的 alpha 可行边密度调节与父代公式变化。

保留问题或边界：新增错误尺度解释：父代 d/remaining 无量纲；子代 d*maxlen/remaining 在整体距离放大时随之放大，却被说成提高尺度不变性。实际 slack 也是静态全预算代理。

<details><summary>原始 Idea、校准 Idea、最终代码与父代码</summary>

### 前置 Idea

The algorithm combines the adaptive tightness penalty from the reference algorithm with the momentum-enhanced base heuristic from the current algorithm. The base desirability for edge $(i, j)$ is computed as $(p_j + 0.25 p_i) / d_{ij}$, incorporating both destination prize value and a momentum term to encourage path continuity. The feasibility check uses the conservative committed cost $d_{i0} + d_{ij} + d_{j0}$ to identify edges that would violate the budget if traversed. For feasible edges, an adaptive exponential penalty $\exp(-\alpha \cdot d_{ij} \cdot \text{maxlen} / \text{remaining})$ is applied, where $\alpha$ dynamically adjusts based on the fraction of feasible edges (ranging from 2.5 to 4.5). This ensures that in tight instances, the heuristic more aggressively penalizes edges consuming large fractions of the remaining budget, while in loose instances, it allows more flexibility. The result is normalized to $[1e-9, 1.0]$ to ensure stable interaction with pheromone values in ACO.

### 校准 Idea

The algorithm implements a combined edge-prior heuristic that blends a static node-based desirability with a dynamic budget-constraint penalty. The primary decision rule for an edge $(i, j)$ is the product of a base heuristic term, which estimates the immediate reward-to-effort ratio, and an exponential penalty term that discourages moves consuming a disproportionate share of the remaining travel budget.

The base heuristic for each directed edge is calculated as $(p_j + 0.25 p_i) / d_{ij}$, where $p_i$ and $p_j$ are the prizes at the source and destination nodes, and $d_{ij}$ is the Euclidean distance. This formulation rewards edges leading to high-value nodes ($p_j$) while incorporating a momentum term ($0.25 p_i$) that favors path continuity from already valuable nodes. The denominator normalizes this reward by distance, penalizing long jumps that may be inefficient relative to the prize gained.

The dynamic component addresses the travel budget constraint `maxlen`. For each edge, the algorithm calculates a conservative "committed cost" defined as $d_{i0} + d_{ij} + d_{j0}$, representing the minimum distance required to reach node $i$, traverse to $j$, and return to the depot. If this committed cost exceeds `maxlen`, the edge is marked infeasible and assigned a minimal heuristic value. For feasible edges, a tightness penalty is applied using the formula $\exp(-\alpha \cdot d_{ij} \cdot \text{maxlen} / \text{remaining})$, where `remaining` is `maxlen` minus the committed cost. The parameter $\alpha$ is adaptive: it starts at 2.5 for loose instances (high feasibility ratio) and increases up to 4.5 for tight instances (low feasibility ratio). This scaling ensures that when the budget is nearly exhausted, the heuristic more aggressively penalizes edges that consume a large fraction of the remaining slack, whereas looser budgets allow for more flexible routing. Finally, the resulting matrix is normalized so that the maximum entry is 1.0 and all entries are at least $10^{-9}$, ensuring stable interaction with pheromone values in the Ant Colony Optimization loop.

The final code introduces three main changes relative to the parent implementation. First, it replaces the fixed power-law penalty $-3.0 \cdot (d_{ij}/\text{remaining})^{1.5}$ with a linear-exponential penalty $-\alpha \cdot (d_{ij} \cdot \text{maxlen}/\text{remaining})$. This linear-in-ratio structure provides a smoother gradient and directly incorporates the problem scale `maxlen` into the exponent, improving scale invariance compared to the parent's unitless ratio. Second, the penalty coefficient $\alpha$ is made adaptive based on the global feasibility density of the instance, rather than being fixed at 3.0. This allows the heuristic to automatically adjust its strictness: tight instances receive a stronger penalty (up to $\alpha=4.5$) to prevent budget violations, while loose instances use a weaker penalty ($\alpha=2.5$) to explore more widely. Third, the output is explicitly normalized to the range $[10^{-9}, 1.0]$ by dividing by the maximum heuristic value, whereas the parent code only enforced a minimum floor. This normalization ensures that the heuristic magnitude remains consistent across different instances, preventing pheromone dominance or under-representation of heuristic guidance during the ACO sampling process.

### 最终代码

```python
import numpy as np

def heuristics(prize: np.ndarray, distance: np.ndarray, maxlen: float) -> np.ndarray:
    """Return edge desirability values for OP ant colony optimization.

    Args:
        prize: Node prizes with shape (n,). Node 0 is the depot.
        distance: Pairwise Euclidean distances with shape (n, n).
            Diagonal entries are large sentinels so self-loops are unused.
        maxlen: Maximum allowed tour length (return-to-depot constrained).

    Returns:
        An (n, n) edge-prior matrix. Larger values make an edge more likely
        to be sampled. Values at or below zero are treated as 1e-9.
    """
    n = len(prize)

    if n == 0:
        return np.array([])

    if n == 1:
        return np.array([[1.0]])

    p = np.maximum(prize, 0.0).astype(float)
    eps = 1e-8

    # Base heuristic: prize of destination + 0.25 * prize of source, divided by distance
    safe_dist = np.where(distance > eps, distance, 1.0)
    base_heur = (p[np.newaxis, :] + 0.25 * p[:, np.newaxis]) / safe_dist

    # Compute committed cost and remaining budget
    d_i0 = distance[:, 0][:, np.newaxis]
    d_j0 = distance[0, :][np.newaxis, :]
    committed = d_i0 + distance + d_j0
    remaining = maxlen - committed

    diag_mask = ~np.eye(n, dtype=bool)
    feasible = (remaining > 0) & diag_mask

    base_heur = np.where(feasible, base_heur, 0.0)

    # Adaptive alpha based on feasibility density
    total_edges = n * n - n
    feasible_count = np.sum(feasible)
    if total_edges > 0:
        feasibility_ratio = feasible_count / total_edges
    else:
        feasibility_ratio = 1.0

    if feasibility_ratio > 0.5:
        alpha = 2.5
    else:
        alpha = 2.5 + (0.5 - feasibility_ratio) * 4.0
        alpha = min(max(alpha, 2.5), 4.5)

    # Compute tightness penalty
    safe_maxlen = max(maxlen, eps)
    safe_remaining = np.where(feasible, remaining, eps)
    ratio = distance * safe_maxlen / safe_remaining

    with np.errstate(over='ignore', under='ignore', invalid='ignore', divide='ignore'):
        penalty = np.exp(-alpha * ratio)

    h = np.where(feasible, base_heur * penalty, 0.0)
    h = np.nan_to_num(h, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    h = np.maximum(h, 1e-9)

    # Normalize to [1e-9, 1.0]
    max_h = np.max(h)
    if max_h > 0:
        h = h / max_h
    else:
        h = np.ones_like(h) * 1e-9
    h = np.maximum(h, 1e-9)

    return h
```

### 父代码

```python
import numpy as np

def heuristics(prize: np.ndarray, distance: np.ndarray, maxlen: float) -> np.ndarray:
    """Return edge desirability values for OP ant colony optimization.

    Args:
        prize: Node prizes with shape (n,). Node 0 is the depot.
        distance: Pairwise Euclidean distances with shape (n, n).
            Diagonal entries are large sentinels so self-loops are unused.
        maxlen: Maximum allowed tour length (return-to-depot constrained).

    Returns:
        An (n, n) edge-prior matrix. Larger values make an edge more likely
        to be sampled. Values at or below zero are treated as 1e-9.
    """
    n = len(prize)

    # Ensure non-negative prizes
    p = np.maximum(prize, 0.0)

    # Estimate the characteristic scale of the graph for scale-invariance
    # Use median of non-zero, non-diagonal distances to be robust to outliers
    off_diag_dist = distance[~np.eye(n, dtype=bool)]
    if len(off_diag_dist) > 0:
        scale = np.median(off_diag_dist)
        if scale < 1e-6:
            scale = 1.0
    else:
        scale = 1.0

    # Distances to/from depot
    d_to_depot = distance[:, 0]

    # Committed cost estimate: cost to reach i (conservative: d_i0) + d_ij + cost to return from j (d_j0)
    # Note: d_to_depot is symmetric in Euclidean, so d_i0 == d_0i
    d_i0 = d_to_depot[:, np.newaxis] # (n, 1)
    d_j0 = d_to_depot[np.newaxis, :] # (1, n)

    committed = d_i0 + distance + d_j0

    remaining = maxlen - committed

    diag_mask = ~np.eye(n, dtype=bool)
    feasible = (remaining > 0) & diag_mask

    eps = 1e-8

    # Base heuristic: Marginal gain of moving i->j
    # We reward high prize at j, and slightly reward continuing from a high prize i (inertia)
    # The division by distance penalizes long jumps
    # p_j is primary, p_i is secondary (0.25 weight)
    base = (p[np.newaxis, :] + 0.25 * p[:, np.newaxis]) / (distance + eps)

    # Penalty term: Exponential decay based on how much of the remaining budget the edge consumes
    # We want the penalty to depend on d_ij / remaining.
    # If remaining is small, penalty is huge.
    # To make it scale-invariant, we could normalize, but d_ij/remaining is already a ratio.
    # However, if maxlen is very large, remaining is large, penalty is ~1.
    # If maxlen is tight, remaining is small, penalty is ~0.
    # The current implementation uses a fixed exponent coefficient. Let's refine it.
    # Using a power slightly greater than 1 makes the penalty steeper when budget is tight.

    safe_remaining = np.where(feasible, remaining, 1.0)

    # Ratio of edge cost to remaining budget
    ratio = distance / safe_remaining

    # Apply penalty.
    # A coefficient of 3.0 with power 1.5 provides a strong but smooth gradient.
    # This discourages edges that eat up a significant portion of the slack.
    exponent = -3.0 * (ratio ** 1.5)

    with np.errstate(divide='ignore', invalid='ignore', over='ignore'):
        penalty = np.exp(exponent)

    # Combine
    h = base * penalty

    # Mask infeasible and diagonal
    h = np.where(feasible, h, 0.0)

    # Sanitize
    h = np.nan_to_num(h, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)

    # Ensure minimum value to avoid zero probabilities in ACO if pheromone is zero
    min_val = 1e-9
    h = np.maximum(h, min_val)

    return h
```

</details>

## 13 op_aco_r1_e339

算子 Fuse；节点 331；父节点 194；fitness 13.975999999999999；局部纠错：未计入。

增益：主要乘法项、alpha/gamma 范围、prize tilt 改动描述对应代码。

保留问题或边界：两版称原返仓因子为 bounded linear，实际为 0.7+0.3*exp(-d/L)；二次版还漏述 h_max<=eps 时整矩阵回退为 1，因此不能无条件说所有不可行边均返回 1e-9。

<details><summary>原始 Idea、校准 Idea、最终代码与父代码</summary>

### 前置 Idea

The core heuristic combines the prize-to-distance ratio of the destination node ($p_j / d_{ij}$) with three corrective factors to balance immediate gain against long-term feasibility and return cost.
1.  **Base Attractiveness**: $B_{ij} = p_j / d_{ij}$, prioritizing high-value nodes that are close.
2.  **Feasibility Penalty**: $\exp(-\alpha \cdot \frac{d_{ij}}{R_{ij}/L_{max}})$, where $R_{ij} = L_{max} - (d_{i0} + d_{ij} + d_{j0})$ is the remaining budget after hypothetically closing the tour. This term dynamically suppresses edges that consume too much of the normalized remaining slack. The exponent $\alpha$ is adaptive: it starts at 2.0 for loose instances (high feasibility ratio) and increases to 4.0 for tight instances (low feasibility ratio). This wide range allows gentle exploration in easy cases and aggressive pruning in constrained ones, which is often more effective than the narrower 2.5-3.5 range used in the current best.
3.  **Prize Tilt**: $(p_j / p_{max})^{0.1}$, clipped to $[0.5, 1.0]$. This very mild tilt ensures high-value nodes remain attractive but avoids double-counting prize importance, which is already handled by the base term.
4.  **Return Cost Factor**: $\exp(-\gamma \cdot d_{j0} / L_{max})$, where $\gamma$ is also adaptive (ranging from 0.5 to 1.5). This directly penalizes moving to nodes that are far from the depot, as such nodes consume the return budget. Using an exponential decay instead of the bounded linear form in the current algorithm provides a smoother gradient that better distinguishes between moderately far and very far nodes.

Infeasible edges (where $R_{ij} \le 0$) and self-loops are masked out. The final matrix is normalized to have a maximum of 1.0 and a minimum floor of $10^{-9}$ for numerical stability in the ACO sampling process.

### 校准 Idea

The implemented heuristic constructs an edge-prior matrix where the desirability of a directed edge $(i, j)$ is determined by a multiplicative combination of four factors: base attractiveness, a feasibility-based exponential penalty, a mild prize tilt, and a return-cost exponential penalty. The base attractiveness is defined as the ratio of the destination node's prize to the Euclidean distance from the current node ($p_j / d_{ij}$), which inherently favors high-value nodes that are close by. To ensure tour feasibility within the budget $L_{max}$, the algorithm calculates the "committed" cost for traversing edge $(i, j)$ as the sum of the distance from the current node to the depot, the edge distance, and the distance from the destination to the depot ($d_{i0} + d_{ij} + d_{j0}$). If this committed cost exceeds $L_{max}$, the edge is marked infeasible and assigned a value of $10^{-9}$. For feasible edges, a dynamic penalty term $\exp(-\alpha \cdot d_{ij} / (R_{ij}/L_{max}))$ is applied, where $R_{ij}$ is the remaining budget slack. This penalty is scaled by an adaptive parameter $\alpha$, which ranges from 2.0 for loose instances (high feasibility ratio) to 4.0 for tight instances, allowing the algorithm to aggressively prune edges that consume too much of the normalized remaining slack in constrained scenarios. Additionally, a return-cost factor $\exp(-\gamma \cdot d_{j0} / L_{max})$ penalizes moving to nodes that are far from the depot, using an adaptive $\gamma$ (0.5 to 1.5) to balance the immediate gain against the future cost of returning. A minor prize tilt factor $(p_j / p_{max})^{0.1}$, clipped to $[0.5, 1.0]$, is included to slightly favor high-value nodes without double-counting their importance, which is already captured by the base term. The final matrix is normalized to have a maximum value of 1.0, ensuring numerical stability for the ACO sampling process.

The implementation introduces several key changes relative to the parent code to refine the heuristic's behavior. First, the range for the adaptive parameter $\alpha$ is expanded from $[2.5, 3.5]$ to $[2.0, 4.0]$. This wider range allows for gentler exploration in loose instances and more aggressive pruning in tight ones, providing greater adaptability across different problem densities. Second, the "return cost" mechanism is redesigned. The parent code used a bounded linear continuity factor ($0.7 + 0.3 \cdot \exp(-d_{j0}/L_{max})$) which only varied between 0.6 and 1.0, resulting in a weak signal. The new implementation replaces this with a direct exponential penalty $\exp(-\gamma \cdot d_{j0} / L_{max})$, clipped to $[0.1, 1.0]$. This provides a smoother, more pronounced gradient that distinguishes between moderately far and very far nodes more effectively, directly discouraging moves that make returning to the depot difficult. Third, the prize tilt exponent is reduced from 0.2 to 0.1, further minimizing the impact of this factor since the base term already heavily weights prize value. These changes collectively aim to improve the balance between immediate prize collection and long-term tour feasibility, potentially leading to higher-quality tours in ACO-based solvers for the Orienteering Problem.

### 最终代码

```python
import numpy as np

def heuristics(prize: np.ndarray, distance: np.ndarray, maxlen: float) -> np.ndarray:
    """Return edge desirability values for OP ant colony optimization.

    Args:
        prize: Node prizes with shape (n,). Node 0 is the depot.
        distance: Pairwise Euclidean distances with shape (n, n).
            Diagonal entries are large sentinels so self-loops are unused.
        maxlen: Maximum allowed tour length (return-to-depot constrained).

    Returns:
        An (n, n) edge-prior matrix. Larger values make an edge more likely
        to be sampled. Values at or below zero are treated as 1e-9.
    """
    n = len(prize)

    if n == 0:
        return np.array([])
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([[1.0]])

    p = np.maximum(prize, 0.0).astype(float)
    eps = 1e-8

    if np.all(p == 0) or maxlen <= 0:
        return np.full((n, n), 1e-9)

    # Calculate base heuristic: prize / distance
    safe_dist = np.where(distance > eps, distance, 1.0)
    base_heur = p[np.newaxis, :] / safe_dist

    # Calculate committed cost and remaining slack
    # Committed cost is d(i,0) + d(i,j) + d(j,0)
    d_i0 = distance[:, 0][:, np.newaxis]
    d_j0 = distance[0, :][np.newaxis, :]
    committed = d_i0 + distance + d_j0
    remaining = maxlen - committed

    # Feasibility mask: remaining > 0 and not self-loop
    diag_mask = ~np.eye(n, dtype=bool)
    feasible = (remaining > 0) & diag_mask

    # Adaptive alpha based on feasibility ratio
    total_edges = n * n - n
    feasible_count = int(np.count_nonzero(feasible))
    feasibility_ratio = feasible_count / total_edges if total_edges > 0 else 1.0

    # Alpha ranges from 2.0 (loose) to 4.0 (tight)
    if feasibility_ratio > 0.6:
        alpha = 2.0
    else:
        # Linear interpolation from 2.0 to 4.0 as ratio goes from 0.6 to 0.0
        scale = (0.6 - feasibility_ratio) / 0.6
        alpha = 2.0 + scale * 2.0
        alpha = float(np.clip(alpha, 2.0, 4.0))

    # Adaptive gamma for return cost penalty
    # Gamma ranges from 0.5 (loose) to 1.5 (tight)
    if feasibility_ratio > 0.6:
        gamma = 0.5
    else:
        scale = (0.6 - feasibility_ratio) / 0.6
        gamma = 0.5 + scale * 1.0
        gamma = float(np.clip(gamma, 0.5, 1.5))

    # Feasibility penalty: exp(-alpha * d_ij / (remaining/maxlen))
    norm_remaining = remaining / max(maxlen, eps)
    safe_norm_rem = np.where(feasible, np.maximum(norm_remaining, eps), 1.0)

    with np.errstate(over='ignore', under='ignore', invalid='ignore', divide='ignore'):
        penalty = np.exp(-alpha * distance / safe_norm_rem)

    # Prize tilt: (p_j / p_max) ** 0.1, clipped to [0.5, 1.0]
    p_max = float(np.max(p)) if np.any(p > 0) else 1.0
    with np.errstate(over='ignore', under='ignore', invalid='ignore', divide='ignore'):
        prize_tilt = np.power(p[np.newaxis, :] / max(p_max, eps), 0.1)
    prize_tilt = np.clip(prize_tilt, 0.5, 1.0)

    # Return cost factor: exp(-gamma * d_j0 / maxlen)
    # This penalizes moving to nodes far from the depot
    with np.errstate(over='ignore', under='ignore', invalid='ignore', divide='ignore'):
        return_factor = np.exp(-gamma * d_j0 / max(maxlen, eps))
    # Clip to [0.1, 1.0] to prevent it from dominating the heuristic too much
    return_factor = np.clip(return_factor, 0.1, 1.0)

    # Combine factors
    h = base_heur * penalty * prize_tilt * return_factor

    # Mask infeasible edges
    h = np.where(feasible, h, 0.0)

    # Sanitize
    h = np.nan_to_num(h, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)

    # Normalize
    h_max = float(np.max(h))
    if h_max > eps:
        h = h / h_max
    else:
        h = np.full((n, n), 1.0)

    h = np.maximum(h, 1e-9)

    return h
```

### 父代码

```python
import numpy as np

def heuristics(prize: np.ndarray, distance: np.ndarray, maxlen: float) -> np.ndarray:
    """Return edge desirability values for OP ant colony optimization.

    Args:
        prize: Node prizes with shape (n,). Node 0 is the depot.
        distance: Pairwise Euclidean distances with shape (n, n).
            Diagonal entries are large sentinels so self-loops are unused.
        maxlen: Maximum allowed tour length (return-to-depot constrained).

    Returns:
        An (n, n) edge-prior matrix. Larger values make an edge more likely
        to be sampled. Values at or below zero are treated as 1e-9.
    """
    n = len(prize)

    if n == 0:
        return np.array([])
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([[1.0]])

    p = np.maximum(prize, 0.0).astype(float)
    eps = 1e-8

    if np.all(p == 0) or maxlen <= 0:
        return np.full((n, n), 1e-9)

    safe_dist = np.where(distance > eps, distance, 1.0)

    base_heur = p[np.newaxis, :] / safe_dist

    d_i0 = distance[:, 0][:, np.newaxis]
    d_j0 = distance[0, :][np.newaxis, :]
    committed = d_i0 + distance + d_j0
    remaining = maxlen - committed

    diag_mask = ~np.eye(n, dtype=bool)
    feasible = (remaining > 0) & diag_mask

    total_edges = n * n - n
    feasible_count = int(np.count_nonzero(feasible))
    feasibility_ratio = feasible_count / total_edges if total_edges > 0 else 1.0

    if feasibility_ratio > 0.6:
        alpha = 2.5
    else:
        scale = (0.6 - feasibility_ratio) / 0.6
        alpha = 2.5 + scale * 1.0
        alpha = float(np.clip(alpha, 2.5, 3.5))

    norm_remaining = remaining / max(maxlen, eps)
    safe_norm_rem = np.where(feasible, np.maximum(norm_remaining, eps), 1.0)

    ratio = distance / safe_norm_rem

    with np.errstate(over='ignore', under='ignore', invalid='ignore', divide='ignore'):
        penalty = np.exp(-alpha * ratio)

    p_max = float(np.max(p)) if np.any(p > 0) else 1.0
    with np.errstate(over='ignore', under='ignore', invalid='ignore', divide='ignore'):
        prize_tilt = np.power(p[np.newaxis, :] / max(p_max, eps), 0.2)
    prize_tilt = np.clip(prize_tilt, 0.5, 1.0)

    min_ret = distance[0, :]
    with np.errstate(over='ignore', under='ignore', invalid='ignore', divide='ignore'):
        continuity = 0.7 + 0.3 * np.exp(-np.maximum(min_ret, 0.0) / max(maxlen, eps))
    continuity = np.clip(continuity, 0.6, 1.0)
    continuity_row = continuity[np.newaxis, :]

    h = base_heur * penalty * prize_tilt * continuity_row
    h = np.where(feasible, h, 0.0)

    h = np.nan_to_num(h, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)

    h_max = float(np.max(h))
    if h_max > eps:
        h = h / h_max

    h = np.maximum(h, 1e-9)

    return h
```

### 前置 Idea

The current algorithm already provides a strong foundation by combining a prize-to-distance ratio, an adaptive exponential distance decay, and a soft feasibility penalty based on the lower bound of the tour length ($d_{i0} + d_{ij} + d_{j0}$). The most promising improvements involve two aspects:
1.  **Dynamic Feasibility Relaxation:** The current static lower bound $LB_{ij} = d_{i0} + d_{ij} + d_{j0}$ is overly pessimistic for nodes visited later in the tour, as it assumes the ant started the tour by going directly to $i$. To allow for more flexible routing, I will relax the feasibility constraint slightly by introducing a "slack factor" (e.g., allowing the $LB$ to exceed `maxlen` by a small margin, say 5-10%). However, a simpler and more robust improvement is to adjust the **soft penalty exponent** $\gamma$. The previous step (Step 7) tried $\gamma=2.5$ and failed, while Step 8 used $\gamma=2.0$ and succeeded. This suggests the current $\gamma=2.0$ is near-optimal, but perhaps slightly too strict for high-margin edges or too loose for low-margin ones. A key insight from Orienteering is that the "value" of an edge depends on the *remaining* budget. Since this is a static heuristic, we can approximate the dynamic nature by making the decay of the prize ratio more sensitive to the *absolute* distance consumed.
2.  **Refined Prize Decay:** Instead of just normalizing the ratio $p_j/d_{ij}$, we can incorporate a term that rewards edges that lead to nodes with high *future* potential. However, a more direct improvement often comes from adjusting the **distance decay scale** $\sigma$. Step 8 used the 30th percentile. Step 7 tried the 20th percentile and failed. This implies the heuristic was *too* local. Let's stick to the 30th percentile but perhaps adjust the decay exponent $\alpha$.
3.  **Return Edge Enhancement:** The return edge heuristic is crucial. The current heuristic for returning to the depot ($i \to 0$) uses inverse distance and exponential decay. A subtle improvement is to explicitly penalize returning from a node $i$ if $d_{i0}$ is a significant fraction of `maxlen`, even if the ant could technically return, because it indicates the ant is far from the start and likely has few options left. The current code handles this with `decay_ret`.

Let's look at the specific differences between Step 8 (14.17) and Step 7 (13.97). Step 8 used `percentile=30`, `alpha=2.0`, `gamma=2.0`, `beta=1.5`. Step 7 used `percentile=20`, `alpha=2.2`, `gamma=2.5`. The "success" of Step 8 suggests that a *more relaxed* local bias (higher percentile, lower alpha) and *softer* feasibility penalty (lower gamma) is better.

Proposed Changes:
1.  **Soften the Feasibility Penalty further:** Reduce $\gamma$ from 2.0 to 1.5 or 1.2. This allows the ant to consider edges that are closer to the budget limit, potentially uncovering high-value nodes that were previously suppressed. The hard mask ($LB > maxlen$) still prevents infeasibility.
2.  **Adjust Distance Decay:** Keep $\sigma$ at the 30th percentile but slightly reduce $\alpha$ to 1.8 to encourage slightly longer hops if the prize is high, trusting the feasibility mask to stop infeasible paths.
3.  **Return Edge Boost:** Introduce a small constant boost to the return edge value for nodes that are "close" to the depot (e.g., $d_{i0} < 0.2 \cdot maxlen$) to encourage early termination if the tour is already good, or conversely, ensure that if the ant is forced to return, it does so from the best possible spot. Actually, a simpler and more robust change is to modify the normalization of the return edge. Currently, it's normalized by the max inverse distance. This can lead to very small values if one node is extremely close to the depot. Let's normalize by the *mean* or use a different scaling.

Let's try a different angle: **Prize-Weighted Feasibility**.
The current feasibility penalty is $\text{margin}^\gamma$. This treats all edges equally in terms of budget consumption. However, an edge that gives a huge prize for a small budget cost is much better than one that gives a small prize for the same cost. The current score is $\text{ratio} \times \text{decay} \times \text{feasibility}$. Since $\text{ratio} = p/d$, the feasibility part doesn't know about $p$.
If we change the feasibility penalty to depend on the *efficiency* of the budget usage, i.e., penalize based on $\text{margin}^\gamma$ but where the "margin" is relative to the value gained? No, that's complex.

Let's stick to parameter tuning based on the history.
Step 8 was best.
Params: `pctl=30`, `alpha=2.0`, `gamma=2.0`, `beta=1.5`.
Let's try `pctl=25` (slightly more local than 30, but less than 20 which failed).
Let's try `alpha=1.8` (slightly less decay).
Let's try `gamma=1.5` (much softer feasibility penalty).

Why `gamma=1.5`? Step 7's `gamma=2.5` failed. Step 8's `gamma=2.0` worked. The trend suggests softer is better. If we go too soft, ants might try to go to far nodes and get stuck. But the hard mask prevents infeasibility. The soft penalty guides *probabilistic* choice. A softer penalty allows exploration of edges with lower margin, which might lead to better prize collection if the pheromones guide the ant well.

Let's also look at the return edge. `beta=1.5` was good. Let's try `beta=1.2`.

Another idea: **Adaptive Alpha**. If the instance is sparse (few nodes per unit length), we might want a higher alpha to prevent long jumps. If dense, lower. The percentile already adapts to density.

Let's implement the version with:

This should encourage slightly longer, more valuable hops and be less aggressive about cutting off edges near the budget limit, relying on the ACO pheromones to avoid bad paths while the heuristic opens up more options.

### 校准 Idea

The heuristic computes edge desirability by combining prize efficiency, spatial proximity, and budget feasibility. For intermediate moves $i \to j$ ($j \neq 0$), the score is the product of three terms: a normalized prize-to-distance ratio ($p_j / d_{ij}$), an exponential distance decay ($\exp(-\alpha d_{ij} / \sigma)$), and a soft feasibility penalty ($\text{margin}^{\gamma}$). The feasibility margin is derived from the lower bound $LB = d_{i0} + d_{ij} + d_{j0}$, representing the minimum additional distance required to visit $j$ and return to the depot. Edges where $LB > \text{maxlen}$ are strictly masked to zero. The return edge $i \to 0$ is scored using inverse distance and exponential decay, masked if the direct round trip exceeds the budget. The output matrix is normalized to [0,1] with a floor of $10^{-9}$.

Key parameters and their effects: The spatial scale $\sigma$ is set to the 25th percentile of non-zero distances, establishing a local focus. The decay exponent $\alpha=1.8$ reduces the penalty for longer hops compared to stricter settings, allowing ants to consider distant high-prize nodes. The feasibility exponent $\gamma=1.5$ softens the penalty for edges with low remaining budget, encouraging exploration near the constraint limit while the hard mask ensures infeasibility. The return decay $\beta=1.2$ slightly favors closer returns but is less aggressive than higher values.

Relative to the parent code, the primary changes are parameter adjustments to balance exploration and exploitation. The spatial scale percentile was lowered from 30 to 25, making the heuristic slightly more local. The decay exponent $\alpha$ was reduced from 2.0 to 1.8, and the feasibility exponent $\gamma$ was reduced from 2.0 to 1.5. This combination hypothesizes that a more relaxed local bias and softer feasibility penalty will allow the ACO algorithm to discover better tours by considering a wider set of candidate edges, particularly those near the budget limit, without relying solely on strict local preferences. The return edge decay $\beta$ was also reduced from 1.5 to 1.2, providing a slightly less restrictive preference for immediate return, though the impact is likely minor compared to the intermediate move adjustments. These changes aim to improve the heuristic's adaptability to instances where the optimal tour requires traversing a mix of local and longer-range edges under tight budget constraints.

### 最终代码

```python
import numpy as np

def heuristics(prize: np.ndarray, distance: np.ndarray, maxlen: float) -> np.ndarray:
    """Return edge desirability values for OP ant colony optimization.

    Args:
        prize: Node prizes with shape (n,). Node 0 is the depot.
        distance: Pairwise Euclidean distances with shape (n, n).
            Diagonal entries are large sentinels so self-loops are unused.
        maxlen: Maximum allowed tour length (return-to-depot constrained).

    Returns:
        An (n, n) edge-prior matrix. Larger values make an edge more likely
        to be sampled. Values at or below zero are treated as 1e-9.
    """
    n = prize.shape[0]
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros((0, 0), dtype=float)
    if n == 1:
        return np.full((1, 1), 1e-9, dtype=float)

    eps = 1e-8
    safe_maxlen = float(max(maxlen, eps))

    p = np.asarray(prize, dtype=float).copy()
    p[0] = 0.0
    D = np.asarray(distance, dtype=float)

    Dsafe = D.copy()
    Dsafe = np.where(Dsafe > eps, Dsafe, np.finfo(float).max)
    np.fill_diagonal(Dsafe, np.finfo(float).max)

    H = np.zeros((n, n), dtype=float)

    p_j = p[1:][None, :]
    D_ij = Dsafe[:, 1:]

    # Calculate prize-to-distance ratio
    ratio = p_j / D_ij
    ratio = np.where(np.isfinite(ratio) & (ratio > 0), ratio, 0.0)
    max_r = np.max(ratio) if ratio.size > 0 else 0.0
    if max_r > 0:
        ratio_norm = ratio / max_r
    else:
        ratio_norm = np.zeros_like(ratio)

    # Determine adaptive scale for distance decay
    finite_off = Dsafe[~np.eye(n, dtype=bool)].ravel()
    finite_off = finite_off[np.isfinite(finite_off) & (finite_off > 0)]
    if len(finite_off) > 0:
        # Use 25th percentile to balance local vs global, slightly more local than 30th
        scale = float(np.percentile(finite_off, 25))
        scale = max(scale, 0.05 * safe_maxlen)
        scale = min(scale, 0.5 * safe_maxlen)
    else:
        scale = 0.25 * safe_maxlen

    # Exponential decay for distance. alpha=1.8 to be less aggressive than 2.0
    alpha = 1.8
    decay = np.exp(-alpha * D_ij / scale)
    decay = np.where(np.isfinite(decay), decay, 0.0)

    # Feasibility margin based on lower bound: d(i,0) + d(i,j) + d(j,0)
    d_i0 = Dsafe[:, 0]
    d_j0 = Dsafe[1:, 0]

    LB = d_i0[:, None] + D_ij + d_j0[None, :]
    feasible = LB <= safe_maxlen

    margin = (safe_maxlen - LB) / safe_maxlen
    margin = np.clip(margin, 0.0, 1.0)

    # Softer feasibility penalty. gamma=1.5 allows more exploration of tight-margin edges
    gamma = 1.5
    soft = margin ** gamma

    score = ratio_norm * decay * soft
    score = np.where(feasible, score, 0.0)

    H[:, 1:] = score

    # Return edge heuristic (i -> 0)
    inv_d = np.zeros(n)
    valid_d = d_i0 > eps
    inv_d[valid_d] = 1.0 / d_i0[valid_d]

    max_inv = np.max(inv_d) if np.any(valid_d) else 1.0
    if max_inv > 0:
        inv_d_norm = inv_d / max_inv
    else:
        inv_d_norm = np.zeros_like(inv_d)

    # Slightly softer decay for return to avoid over-penalizing mid-distance returns
    beta = 1.2
    decay_ret = np.exp(-beta * d_i0 / safe_maxlen)
    decay_ret = np.where(np.isfinite(decay_ret), decay_ret, 0.0)

    H_ret = inv_d_norm * decay_ret

    # Only allow return if the direct round trip is feasible
    ret_feasible = 2 * d_i0 <= safe_maxlen
    H_ret = np.where(ret_feasible, H_ret, 0.0)

    H[:, 0] = H_ret

    # Normalize and sanitize
    max_H = np.max(H)
    if np.isfinite(max_H) and max_H > 0:
        H = H / max_H
    else:
        H = np.ones_like(H) * 1e-9

    H = np.where(np.isfinite(H) & (H > 0), H, 0.0)
    np.fill_diagonal(H, 0.0)

    H = np.clip(H, 1e-9, 1.0)
    np.fill_diagonal(H, 1e-9)

    return H
```

### 父代码

```python
import numpy as np

def heuristics(prize: np.ndarray, distance: np.ndarray, maxlen: float) -> np.ndarray:
    """Return edge desirability values for OP ant colony optimization.

    Args:
        prize: Node prizes with shape (n,). Node 0 is the depot.
        distance: Pairwise Euclidean distances with shape (n, n).
            Diagonal entries are large sentinels so self-loops are unused.
        maxlen: Maximum allowed tour length (return-to-depot constrained).

    Returns:
        An (n, n) edge-prior matrix. Larger values make an edge more likely
        to be sampled. Values at or below zero are treated as 1e-9.
    """
    n = prize.shape[0]
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros((0, 0), dtype=float)
    if n == 1:
        return np.full((1, 1), 1e-9, dtype=float)

    eps = 1e-8
    safe_maxlen = float(max(maxlen, eps))

    p = np.asarray(prize, dtype=float).copy()
    p[0] = 0.0
    D = np.asarray(distance, dtype=float)

    Dsafe = D.copy()
    Dsafe = np.where(Dsafe > eps, Dsafe, np.finfo(float).max)
    np.fill_diagonal(Dsafe, np.finfo(float).max)

    H = np.zeros((n, n), dtype=float)

    # Handle intermediate moves (j != 0)
    p_j = p[1:][None, :]
    D_ij = Dsafe[:, 1:]

    # 1. Prize-distance ratio, normalized by max
    ratio = p_j / D_ij
    ratio = np.where(np.isfinite(ratio) & (ratio > 0), ratio, 0.0)
    max_r = np.max(ratio) if ratio.size > 0 else 0.0
    if max_r > 0:
        ratio_norm = ratio / max_r
    else:
        ratio_norm = np.zeros_like(ratio)

    # 2. Spatial decay
    finite_off = Dsafe[~np.eye(n, dtype=bool)].ravel()
    finite_off = finite_off[np.isfinite(finite_off) & (finite_off > 0)]
    if len(finite_off) > 0:
        # 30th percentile for a balance between local focus and robustness
        scale = float(np.percentile(finite_off, 30))
        scale = max(scale, 0.05 * safe_maxlen)
        scale = min(scale, 0.5 * safe_maxlen)
    else:
        scale = 0.25 * safe_maxlen

    alpha = 2.0
    decay = np.exp(-alpha * D_ij / scale)
    decay = np.where(np.isfinite(decay), decay, 0.0)

    # 3. Feasibility margin
    d_i0 = Dsafe[:, 0]
    d_j0 = Dsafe[1:, 0]

    LB = d_i0[:, None] + D_ij + d_j0[None, :]
    feasible = LB <= safe_maxlen

    margin = (safe_maxlen - LB) / safe_maxlen
    margin = np.clip(margin, 0.0, 1.0)

    gamma = 2.0
    soft = margin ** gamma

    score = ratio_norm * decay * soft
    score = np.where(feasible, score, 0.0)

    H[:, 1:] = score

    # Handle return moves (j = 0)
    inv_d = np.zeros(n)
    valid_d = d_i0 > eps
    inv_d[valid_d] = 1.0 / d_i0[valid_d]

    max_inv = np.max(inv_d) if np.any(valid_d) else 1.0
    if max_inv > 0:
        inv_d_norm = inv_d / max_inv
    else:
        inv_d_norm = np.zeros_like(inv_d)

    beta = 1.5
    decay_ret = np.exp(-beta * d_i0 / safe_maxlen)
    decay_ret = np.where(np.isfinite(decay_ret), decay_ret, 0.0)

    H_ret = inv_d_norm * decay_ret

    # Return is feasible if the round trip is within budget
    ret_feasible = 2 * d_i0 <= safe_maxlen
    H_ret = np.where(ret_feasible, H_ret, 0.0)

    H[:, 0] = H_ret

    # Normalize and sanitize
    max_H = np.max(H)
    if np.isfinite(max_H) and max_H > 0:
        H = H / max_H
    else:
        # Fallback if all scores are 0 (e.g. very tight budget or invalid input)
        H = np.ones_like(H) * 1e-9

    H = np.where(np.isfinite(H) & (H > 0), H, 0.0)
    np.fill_diagonal(H, 0.0)

    H = np.clip(H, 1e-9, 1.0)
    np.fill_diagonal(H, 1e-9)

    return H
```

<details><summary>原始 Idea、校准 Idea、最终代码与父代码</summary>

### 前置 Idea

The heuristic constructs an edge-prior matrix $\eta$ for the Orienteering Problem (OP) by evaluating the desirability of moving from node $i$ to node $j$ based on three multiplicative factors: prize efficiency, spatial proximity, and budget feasibility. For intermediate moves ($j \neq 0$), the score is the product of a normalized prize-to-distance ratio $r_{ij} = (p_j/d_{ij})/S$, where $S = \max(\bar{r}, 0.5 r_{\max})$ is a hybrid scale that balances outlier sensitivity with robust distribution, and is clipped to $[0, 2.0]$ to prevent single high-prize nodes from dominating the landscape. A spatial decay term $\exp(-\alpha d_{ij}/\sigma)$ with $\alpha=2.2$ and adaptive $\sigma$ (5th percentile of distances, clamped to $[0.02 L, 0.30 L]$) encourages local clustering. A feasibility margin term $((L - LB_{ij})/L)^{\gamma}$ with $\gamma=1.2$, where $LB_{ij} = d_{i0} + d_{ij} + d_{j0}$, softly penalizes moves that consume too much budget, strictly zeroing out infeasible edges. Return edges ($j=0$) are scored using inverse distance and a decay term $\exp(-\beta d_{i0}/L)$ with $\beta=1.2$, scaled by a prize bias factor $(0.6 + 0.4 p_i/\max(p))$ to encourage returns from high-value nodes. The final matrix is normalized such that intermediate edges max out at 1.0 and return edges at 0.5, ensuring ants prioritize prize collection over termination while maintaining a viable probability of returning. This configuration balances local exploitation with sufficient exploration to maximize total prize within the travel budget.

### 校准 Idea

The implemented algorithm constructs a heuristic matrix $\eta$ for Ant Colony Optimization on the Orienteering Problem by calculating the desirability of transitioning from node $i$ to node $j$ using a multiplicative model of prize efficiency, spatial proximity, and budget feasibility. For intermediate moves ($j \neq 0$), the heuristic score is the product of three terms: a normalized prize-to-distance ratio, a spatial decay factor, and a feasibility margin. The prize efficiency is computed as $p_j / d_{ij}$ and normalized by a hybrid scale $S = \max(\bar{r}, 0.5 r_{\max})$, where $\bar{r}$ and $r_{\max}$ are the mean and maximum of the positive ratios, respectively. This normalization is clipped to $[0, 2.0]$ to prevent outlier prizes from dominating the search landscape. The spatial decay term is $\exp(-\alpha d_{ij} / \sigma)$ with $\alpha=2.2$, where $\sigma$ is an adaptive parameter set to the 5th percentile of finite off-diagonal distances, clamped to $[0.02 L, 0.30 L]$, encouraging local clustering. The feasibility term is $((L - LB_{ij})/L)^{\gamma}$ with $\gamma=1.2$, where $LB_{ij} = d_{i0} + d_{ij} + d_{j0}$ represents the minimum tour length if moving from $i$ to $j$ and returning to the depot. Edges that violate the budget constraint ($LB_{ij} > L$) are strictly zeroed out. Return edges ($j=0$) are scored using the inverse of the distance to the depot, normalized by the maximum inverse distance, multiplied by a decay term $\exp(-\beta d_{i0}/L)$ with $\beta=1.2$ and a prize bias factor $0.6 + 0.4 p_i / \max(p)$ to prioritize returning from high-value nodes. Return edges are only feasible if $2 d_{i0} \leq L$. The final matrix is normalized such that the maximum intermediate edge score is 1.0 and the maximum return edge score is 0.5, ensuring ants prefer prize collection over termination while maintaining a viable return probability. Values at or below zero are clipped to $10^{-9}$, and diagonal entries are set to $10^{-9}$ to prevent self-loops. This configuration balances exploitation of high-prize, nearby nodes with exploration through the spatial decay and feasibility terms, aiming to maximize total collected prize within the travel budget.

The important implementation change relative to the parent code is the clipping upper bound for the normalized prize-to-distance ratio, which was reduced from 2.5 in the parent code to 2.0 in the final code. This adjustment slightly restricts the maximum contribution of high prize-to-distance ratios to the heuristic score, potentially reducing the influence of outlier nodes with exceptionally high prize values relative to their distance. The rest of the algorithm remains identical, including the adaptive sigma calculation, the feasibility margin exponent, the return edge scoring formula, and the normalization strategy. This change may lead to more balanced heuristic values and potentially improved exploration behavior, as extreme ratios are less likely to dominate the sampling process. However, the impact on performance is expected to be modest, as the ratio is already normalized by a scale that includes the maximum ratio, and the 2.0 clip is only slightly stricter than the 2.5 clip. The main design decisions, such as the multiplicative structure, the adaptive sigma, and the feasibility margin, are unchanged, so the overall behavior of the heuristic should be similar to the parent code, with only a minor reduction in the influence of high prize-to-distance edges.

### 最终代码

```python
import numpy as np

def heuristics(prize: np.ndarray, distance: np.ndarray, maxlen: float) -> np.ndarray:
    """Return edge desirability values for OP ant colony optimization.

    Args:
        prize: Node prizes with shape (n,). Node 0 is the depot.
        distance: Pairwise Euclidean distances with shape (n, n).
            Diagonal entries are large sentinels so self-loops are unused.
        maxlen: Maximum allowed tour length (return-to-depot constrained).

    Returns:
        An (n, n) edge-prior matrix. Larger values make an edge more likely
        to be sampled. Values at or below zero are treated as 1e-9.
    """
    n = prize.shape[0]
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros((0, 0), dtype=float)
    if n == 1:
        return np.full((1, 1), 1e-9, dtype=float)

    eps = 1e-8
    L = max(float(maxlen), eps)

    p = np.asarray(prize, dtype=float).copy()
    p[0] = 0.0
    D = np.asarray(distance, dtype=float)

    Dsafe = D.copy()
    Dsafe = np.where(Dsafe > eps, Dsafe, np.finfo(float).max)
    np.fill_diagonal(Dsafe, np.finfo(float).max)

    H = np.zeros((n, n), dtype=float)

    if n > 1:
        p_j = p[1:][None, :]
        D_ij = Dsafe[:, 1:]

        ratio = p_j / D_ij
        ratio = np.where(np.isfinite(ratio) & (ratio > 0), ratio, 0.0)

        pos = ratio > 0
        if np.any(pos):
            r_max = float(np.max(ratio[pos]))
            r_mean = float(np.mean(ratio[pos]))
            scale_r = max(r_mean, 0.5 * r_max)
            scale_r = max(scale_r, eps)
            ratio_norm = ratio / scale_r
        else:
            ratio_norm = np.zeros_like(ratio)

        ratio_norm = np.clip(ratio_norm, 0.0, 2.0)

        off_diag_mask = ~np.eye(n, dtype=bool)
        finite_off = Dsafe[off_diag_mask].ravel()
        finite_off = finite_off[np.isfinite(finite_off) & (finite_off > eps) & (finite_off < np.finfo(float).max)]

        if len(finite_off) > 0:
            sigma = float(np.percentile(finite_off, 5))
            sigma = max(sigma, 0.02 * L)
            sigma = min(sigma, 0.30 * L)
        else:
            sigma = 0.1 * L

        alpha = 2.2
        decay = np.exp(-alpha * D_ij / sigma)
        decay = np.where(np.isfinite(decay), decay, 0.0)

        d_i0 = Dsafe[:, 0]
        d_j0 = Dsafe[1:, 0]
        LB = d_i0[:, None] + D_ij + d_j0[None, :]

        feasible = LB <= L
        margin = (L - LB) / L
        margin = np.clip(margin, 0.0, 1.0)

        gamma = 1.2
        feas = margin ** gamma
        feas = np.where(feasible, feas, 0.0)
        feas = np.where(np.isfinite(feas), feas, 0.0)

        score_int = ratio_norm * decay * feas
        score_int = np.where(np.isfinite(score_int) & (score_int > 0), score_int, 0.0)
        H[:, 1:] = score_int

        ret_feasible = 2 * d_i0 <= L

        inv_d = np.zeros(n)
        valid_d = d_i0 > eps
        inv_d[valid_d] = 1.0 / d_i0[valid_d]

        max_inv = float(np.max(inv_d)) if np.any(valid_d) else 1.0
        if max_inv > 0:
            inv_n = inv_d / max_inv
        else:
            inv_n = np.zeros_like(d_i0)

        beta = 1.2
        decay_ret = np.exp(-beta * d_i0 / L)
        decay_ret = np.where(np.isfinite(decay_ret), decay_ret, 0.0)

        max_p = float(np.max(p)) if np.any(p > 0) else 0.0
        if max_p > 0:
            p_norm = p / max_p
        else:
            p_norm = np.zeros_like(p)

        prize_bias = 0.6 + 0.4 * p_norm

        H_ret = inv_n * decay_ret * prize_bias
        H_ret = np.where(ret_feasible, H_ret, 0.0)
        H[:, 0] = H_ret

        max_int = float(np.max(H[:, 1:])) if n > 1 else 0.0
        if max_int > 0 and np.isfinite(max_int):
            H[:, 1:] = H[:, 1:] / max_int

        max_ret = float(np.max(H[:, 0])) if n > 1 else 0.0
        if max_ret > 0 and np.isfinite(max_ret):
            H[:, 0] = H[:, 0] / max_ret
            H[:, 0] = np.clip(H[:, 0], 0.0, 0.5)

    H = np.where(np.isfinite(H) & (H > 0), H, 0.0)
    np.fill_diagonal(H, 0.0)
    H = np.clip(H, 1e-9, 1.0)
    np.fill_diagonal(H, 1e-9)

    return H
```

import numpy as np

def heuristics(prize: np.ndarray, distance: np.ndarray, maxlen: float) -> np.ndarray:
    """Return edge desirability values for OP ant colony optimization.

    Args:

    n = prize.shape[0]
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros((0, 0), dtype=float)
    if n == 1:
        return np.full((1, 1), 1e-9, dtype=float)

    p[0] = 0.0
    D = np.asarray(distance, dtype=float)

    Dsafe = D.copy()
    Dsafe = np.where(Dsafe > eps, Dsafe, np.finfo(float).max)
    np.fill_diagonal(Dsafe, np.finfo(float).max)

    H = np.zeros((n, n), dtype=float)

    if n > 1:
        p_j = p[1:][None, :]

        ratio = p_j / D_ij

        pos = ratio > 0

            r_max = float(np.max(ratio[pos]))
            r_mean = float(np.mean(ratio[pos]))
            scale_r = max(r_mean, 0.5 * r_max)
            scale_r = max(scale_r, eps)
            ratio_norm = ratio / scale_r
        else:
            ratio_norm = np.zeros_like(ratio)

        ratio_norm = np.clip(ratio_norm, 0.0, 2.5)

        off_diag_mask = ~np.eye(n, dtype=bool)
        finite_off = Dsafe[off_diag_mask].ravel()
        finite_off = finite_off[np.isfinite(finite_off) & (finite_off > eps) & (finite_off < np.finfo(float).max)]

        if len(finite_off) > 0:
            sigma = float(np.percentile(finite_off, 5))
            sigma = max(sigma, 0.02 * L)
            sigma = min(sigma, 0.30 * L)
        else:
            sigma = 0.1 * L

        alpha = 2.2
        decay = np.exp(-alpha * D_ij / sigma)
        decay = np.where(np.isfinite(decay), decay, 0.0)

        d_i0 = Dsafe[:, 0]
        d_j0 = Dsafe[1:, 0]
        LB = d_i0[:, None] + D_ij + d_j0[None, :]

        feasible = LB <= L
        margin = (L - LB) / L
        margin = np.clip(margin, 0.0, 1.0)

        gamma = 1.2
        feas = margin ** gamma
        feas = np.where(feasible, feas, 0.0)
        feas = np.where(np.isfinite(feas), feas, 0.0)

        score_int = ratio_norm * decay * feas
        score_int = np.where(np.isfinite(score_int) & (score_int > 0), score_int, 0.0)
        H[:, 1:] = score_int

        ret_feasible = 2 * d_i0 <= L

        inv_d = np.zeros(n)
        valid_d = d_i0 > eps
        inv_d[valid_d] = 1.0 / d_i0[valid_d]

        max_inv = float(np.max(inv_d)) if np.any(valid_d) else 1.0
        if max_inv > 0:
            inv_n = inv_d / max_inv
        else:
            inv_n = np.zeros_like(d_i0)

        beta = 1.2
        decay_ret = np.exp(-beta * d_i0 / L)
        decay_ret = np.where(np.isfinite(decay_ret), decay_ret, 0.0)

        max_p = float(np.max(p)) if np.any(p > 0) else 0.0
        if max_p > 0:
            p_norm = p / max_p
        else:
            p_norm = np.zeros_like(p)

        prize_bias = 0.6 + 0.4 * p_norm

        H_ret = inv_n * decay_ret * prize_bias
        H_ret = np.where(ret_feasible, H_ret, 0.0)
        H[:, 0] = H_ret

        max_int = float(np.max(H[:, 1:])) if n > 1 else 0.0
        if max_int > 0 and np.isfinite(max_int):
            H[:, 1:] = H[:, 1:] / max_int

        max_ret = float(np.max(H[:, 0])) if n > 1 else 0.0

            H[:, 0] = np.clip(H[:, 0], 0.0, 0.5)

    H = np.where(np.isfinite(H) & (H > 0), H, 0.0)

    np.fill_diagonal(H, 1e-9)
