## 16 op_aco_r3_e107

算子 Fuse；节点 103；父节点 69；fitness 13.947999999999999；局部纠错：未计入。

增益：补充实际四项乘法公式、指数变化与非有限检查。

保留问题或边界：主体计算有依据；remaining/slack 是输入全预算下的静态余量，不含实际前缀旅行长度。floor 是数值下限，最终可行性由评估器动态 mask 保证。

<details><summary>原始 Idea、校准 Idea、最终代码与父代码</summary>

### 前置 Idea

Build a four-factor multiplicative edge desirability that combines (1) a prize-efficiency term using the 90th-percentile prize normalization with a modest power of 1.1 to favor high-value nodes without over-amplifying outliers, (2) a scale-invariant proximity term `(median / d_ij)^0.5` that penalizes long edges relative to the graph's typical distance, (3) a budget-headroom term `(slack / maxlen)^0.4` where slack is the remaining budget after moving to node j and returning to the depot—this shallow power keeps near-budget edges competitive to encourage full utilization, (4) a directional bias with a reduced weight of 0.08 to gently encourage moves that reduce distance to the depot without overly constraining exploration in tour mid-sections. Critical changes from the parent: hard feasibility masking (infeasible edges set to 1e-9 rather than just zeroed) ensures the pheromone-update never sees invalid moves, explicit depot-exit enforcement prevents starting with an impossible round-trip, and robust handling of sentinel/non-finite distances via finite filtering before median computation. Normalization to [1e-9, 1] keeps the heuristic numerically stable for the ACO probability calculation.

### 校准 Idea

The implemented algorithm constructs an edge-prior matrix for Ant Colony Optimization by computing a multiplicative desirability score for every directed edge $(i, j)$ in the graph, conditioned on the Orienteering Problem constraints. The primary decision rule identifies edges that maximize the ratio of collected prize to travel cost while preserving sufficient budget for the mandatory return to the depot. The calculation begins by establishing a robust scale reference using the median of finite off-diagonal distances, which serves as a baseline for proximity comparisons. Node prizes are normalized by the 90th percentile of the prize distribution to prevent extreme outliers from dominating the heuristic, ensuring that the desirability reflects relative value rather than absolute magnitude. The core desirability for edge $(i, j)$ is the product of four terms: (1) a prize-efficiency term $(\frac{p_j}{p_{ref}} / d_{ij})^{1.1}$, which favors high-value nodes that are close to the current position, with an exponent of 1.1 to sharpen the preference for efficient movements without completely suppressing moderate options; (2) a scale-invariant proximity term $(\frac{med\_dist}{d_{ij}})^{0.5}$, which penalizes long edges relative to the graph's typical distance scale, ensuring that the heuristic remains effective regardless of the absolute coordinate system; (3) a budget-headroom term $(\frac{slack}{maxlen})^{0.4}$, where slack is the remaining budget after traversing edge $(i, j)$ and returning to the depot. This shallow exponent allows edges that consume most of the budget to remain competitive, encouraging full utilization of the travel constraint; and (4) a directional bias $1 + 0.08 \tanh(\frac{d_{i,depot} - d_{j,depot}}{med\_dist})$, which gently favors moves that reduce the distance to the depot, aiding tour closure. Crucially, the algorithm enforces hard feasibility by setting the desirability of any edge that violates the return-to-depot constraint to $10^{-9}$ (effectively zero for sampling), and specifically blocks depot exits where the round-trip distance exceeds the budget. The final matrix is normalized to the range $[10^{-9}, 1]$, ensuring numerical stability for the ACO probability calculation. This approach hypothesizes that by explicitly incorporating budget slack and relative proximity, the ants will construct tours that efficiently cluster high-value nodes within the spatial and temporal limits, thereby maximizing the total collected prize compared to heuristics that ignore the global budget constraint during edge selection.

The implementation differs from the parent code in several specific parameters and robustness checks that refine the heuristic's sensitivity. First, the exponent for the prize-efficiency term was reduced from 1.3 to 1.1. This change lessens the amplification of outlier prizes, making the heuristic more stable and preventing the ants from being overly attracted to a single high-value node if it is slightly suboptimal in terms of distance. Second, the exponent for the proximity term was changed from 0.6 to 0.5, and the headroom exponent from 0.5 to 0.4. These reductions flatten the gradient of the desirability function, allowing more edges to remain viable candidates during the sampling process. This is intended to maintain exploration diversity in the mid-section of the tour, where strict adherence to the most "efficient" edge might lead to dead ends or poor prize collection. Third, the directional bias weight was reduced from 0.15 to 0.08. This makes the preference for moves toward the depot much more subtle, preventing the heuristic from prematurely closing the tour and underutilizing the available budget. Finally, the code adds explicit checks for non-finite values in the `round_trip_depot` calculation, ensuring that sentinel values or infinite distances in the depot row are correctly identified as infeasible, whereas the parent code relied on standard comparison operators which might not handle `inf` or `nan` as robustly in all edge cases. These adjustments collectively shift the heuristic from a more aggressive exploitation strategy to a balanced approach that favors robustness and budget utilization over immediate local optimality.

### 最终代码

```python
import numpy as np

def heuristics(prize: np.ndarray, distance: np.ndarray, maxlen: float) -> np.ndarray:
    """Return edge desirability values for OP ant colony optimization.

    Args:
        prize: Node prizes with shape (n,). Node 0 is the depot.
        distance: Pairwise Euclidean distances with shape (n, n).
            Diagonal entries are large sentinels so self-loops are unused.
        maxlen: Maximum allowed tour length (return-to-depot constrained).

    Returns:
        An (n, n) edge-prior matrix. Larger values make an edge more likely
        to be sampled. Values at or below zero are treated as 1e-9.
    """
    n = len(prize)
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([[1e-9]])

    prize = np.asarray(prize, dtype=float)
    distance = np.asarray(distance, dtype=float)
    prize = np.maximum(prize, 0.0)

    if np.max(prize) <= 0:
        return np.full((n, n), 1e-9)

    if maxlen <= 0:
        return np.full((n, n), 1e-9)

    # Robust median computation using only finite off-diagonal entries
    off_mask = ~np.eye(n, dtype=bool)
    off_finite = distance[off_mask]
    off_finite = off_finite[np.isfinite(off_finite)]
    if off_finite.size > 0:
        med_dist = float(np.median(off_finite))
    else:
        med_dist = 1.0
    med_dist = max(med_dist, 1e-8)

    eps = 1e-8
    # Handle non-finite or sentinel distances
    dist_safe = np.where(np.isfinite(distance), distance, np.finfo(float).max)
    dist_safe = np.maximum(dist_safe, eps)

    # Distance to depot for each node, with fallback
    dist_to_depot = distance[:, 0].copy()
    dist_to_depot = np.where(np.isfinite(dist_to_depot), dist_to_depot, med_dist)

    # Prize normalization: use 90th percentile to avoid outlier domination
    p_ref = max(float(np.percentile(prize, 90)) if n > 1 else np.max(prize), 1e-12)
    p_scale = prize / p_ref

    # Factor 1: Prize efficiency with moderate exponent
    # (prize_j / p_ref)^1.1 / d_ij^1.1
    base = (p_scale[np.newaxis, :] / dist_safe) ** 1.1

    # Factor 2: Relative proximity
    rel_factor = (med_dist / dist_safe) ** 0.5

    # Factor 3: Budget headroom
    cost_estimate = dist_safe + dist_to_depot[np.newaxis, :]
    infeasible_mask = cost_estimate > maxlen
    slack = np.maximum(0.0, maxlen - cost_estimate)
    headroom = (slack / maxlen) ** 0.4
    headroom[infeasible_mask] = 0.0
    # Small floor for tight-but-feasible edges
    headroom = np.where(infeasible_mask, 0.0, np.maximum(headroom, 1e-4))

    # Factor 4: Directional bias (gentle)
    delta_d = dist_to_depot[:, np.newaxis] - dist_to_depot[np.newaxis, :]
    direction_factor = 1.0 + 0.08 * np.tanh(delta_d / med_dist)

    # Combine all factors
    h = base * rel_factor * headroom * direction_factor

    # Hard feasibility mask
    h[infeasible_mask] = 0.0

    # Self-loops
    diag_idx = np.diag_indices(n)
    h[diag_idx] = 0.0

    # Depot exits: enforce round-trip feasibility from depot
    round_trip_depot = 2.0 * distance[0, :]
    infeasible_depot_exits = ~np.isfinite(round_trip_depot) | (round_trip_depot > maxlen)
    if np.any(infeasible_depot_exits):
        h[0, infeasible_depot_exits] = 0.0

    # Sanitize
    h = np.nan_to_num(h, nan=1e-9, posinf=1e6, neginf=1e-9)
    h = np.maximum(h, 1e-9)

    # Normalize to [1e-9, 1]
    max_h = np.max(h)
    if max_h > 1e-9:
        h = h / max_h
        h = np.maximum(h, 1e-9)

    return h
```

### 父代码

```python
import numpy as np

def heuristics(prize: np.ndarray, distance: np.ndarray, maxlen: float) -> np.ndarray:
    """Return edge desirability values for OP ant colony optimization.

    Args:
        prize: Node prizes with shape (n,). Node 0 is the depot.
        distance: Pairwise Euclidean distances with shape (n, n).
            Diagonal entries are large sentinels so self-loops are unused.
        maxlen: Maximum allowed tour length (return-to-depot constrained).

    Returns:
        An (n, n) edge-prior matrix. Larger values make an edge more likely
        to be sampled. Values at or below zero are treated as 1e-9.
    """
    n = len(prize)
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([[1e-9]])

    prize = np.asarray(prize, dtype=float)
    distance = np.asarray(distance, dtype=float)
    prize = np.maximum(prize, 0.0)

    if np.max(prize) <= 0:
        return np.full((n, n), 1e-9)

    if maxlen <= 0:
        return np.full((n, n), 1e-9)

    # Robust median distance from off-diagonal, finite entries
    off_mask = ~np.eye(n, dtype=bool)
    off_finite = distance[off_mask]
    off_finite = off_finite[np.isfinite(off_finite)]
    if off_finite.size > 0:
        med_dist = np.median(off_finite)
    else:
        med_dist = 1.0
    med_dist = max(float(med_dist), 1e-8)

    eps = 1e-8
    dist_safe = np.maximum(distance, eps)
    dist_safe = np.where(np.isfinite(dist_safe), dist_safe, np.finfo(float).max)

    dist_to_depot = distance[:, 0].copy()
    dist_to_depot = np.where(np.isfinite(dist_to_depot), dist_to_depot, med_dist)

    # Prize scaling: normalize by a robust high-percentile to dampen outliers but keep relative order
    p_max = np.max(prize)
    p_ref = max(float(np.percentile(prize, 90)) if n > 1 else p_max, 1e-12)
    p_scale = prize / p_ref  # values in [0, ~1.x]

    # Base local efficiency: prize per unit distance, with a sharper exponent via extra factor
    base = p_scale[np.newaxis, :] / dist_safe
    # Sharpen by raising to a power >1 (equiv. stronger preference)
    base = base ** 1.3

    # Relative proximity factor: normalize against median, exponent 0.6
    rel_factor = (med_dist / dist_safe) ** 0.6

    # Feasibility and budget headroom
    cost_estimate = dist_safe + dist_to_depot[np.newaxis, :]
    infeasible_mask = cost_estimate > maxlen
    slack = np.maximum(0.0, maxlen - cost_estimate)
    headroom = (slack / maxlen) ** 0.5
    headroom[infeasible_mask] = 0.0
    # Small floor to keep gradient near zero for tight-but-feasible edges
    headroom = np.where(infeasible_mask, 0.0, np.maximum(headroom, 1e-3))

    # Directional bias: prefer moves that reduce distance to depot (closer to closure)
    delta_d = dist_to_depot[:, np.newaxis] - dist_to_depot[np.newaxis, :]
    # Scale by median for smooth tanh; slightly stronger bias (0.15)
    direction_factor = 1.0 + 0.15 * np.tanh(delta_d / med_dist)

    h = base * rel_factor * headroom * direction_factor

    # Hard infeasible edges -> 0
    h[infeasible_mask] = 0.0

    # Self-loops -> 0
    diag_idx = np.diag_indices(n)
    h[diag_idx] = 0.0

    # Depot exit infeasibility: if round trip from depot to j exceeds budget, cannot start there
    round_trip_depot = 2.0 * distance[0, :]
    infeasible_depot_exits = round_trip_depot > maxlen
    if np.any(infeasible_depot_exits):
        h[0, infeasible_depot_exits] = 0.0

    # Sanitize
    h = np.nan_to_num(h, nan=1e-9, posinf=1e6, neginf=1e-9)
    h = np.maximum(h, 1e-9)

    # Normalize to [1e-9, 1] for consistent scale with pheromones
    max_h = np.max(h)
    if max_h > 1e-9:
        h = h / max_h
        h = np.maximum(h, 1e-9)

    return h
```

</details>

## 17 op_aco_r3_e204

算子 Pivot；节点 197；父节点 170；fitness 7.368；局部纠错：是。

增益：前置 Idea 为 top-k efficient prizes/B；二次版改成半径 R=B/2 内的 prize 及分母 d_ij+d_j0，后者与代码吻合。

保留问题或边界：关键新叙述仍错：代码的向量赋值没有实现累计和，不能得到半径内全部奖品总和；每对 i,j 还扫描长度 n 的 mask，摘要所谓 O(log n) range query 没覆盖实际 O(n) 扫描。

<details><summary>原始 Idea、校准 Idea、最终代码与父代码</summary>

### 前置 Idea

Instead of a purely local, myopic edge score (prize/distance × slack), this heuristic models the **global marginal value density** achievable in the remaining budget. For each directed edge $(i, j)$, it estimates the average prize per unit distance that could be collected starting from $j$, assuming we have a local budget $B_{ij} = \text{maxlen} - d_{ij} - d_{j0}$ (the distance remaining after going to $j$ and returning to depot). This is computed as the sum of the top $k$ "efficient" prizes in the vicinity of $j$ (nodes within a radius derived from $B_{ij}$ and the global prize density) divided by $B_{ij}$. This captures the **opportunity cost** of choosing $j$: a move to $j$ is valuable not just for $j$'s own prize, but for the cluster of high-value nodes it unlocks within the remaining budget. This global cluster-aware density replaces the local greediness with a strategic, density-weighted assessment, breaking the "myopic trap" where an ant chooses a single high-prize node that leaves no budget for nearby other prizes.

### 校准 Idea

The final code implements a cluster-aware edge-prior heuristic that estimates the global marginal value density achievable from each directed edge $(i, j)$. Instead of the parent code’s myopic ratio of local prize to distance, this approach evaluates the total prize potential within a radius derived from the remaining budget after traversing $(i, j)$ and returning to the depot. Specifically, for each edge, it calculates the remaining budget $B_{rem} = \text{maxlen} - d_{ij} - d_{j0}$ and sets a search radius $R = 0.5 \cdot B_{rem}$. It then sums the prizes of all nodes within this radius of node $j$ (using pre-sorted distance arrays and cumulative sums for efficiency) to estimate the "cluster value" $V_j$. The edge score is proportional to $V_j / (d_{ij} + d_{j0})$, adjusted by a directional bias that favors moves reducing distance to the depot. This design hypothesis is that prioritizing edges leading to dense prize clusters with sufficient budget headroom improves total collected prize compared to maximizing individual edge efficiency, as it accounts for the opportunity cost of budget allocation across the tour.

The implementation differs significantly from the parent code in three key ways. First, it replaces the parent’s local prize-to-distance ratio with a global cluster value calculation, requiring $O(n^2 \log n)$ preprocessing to sort distances and compute cumulative prize sums for each node. This enables $O(\log n)$ range queries during the main loop, though the nested $O(n^2)$ loop over all edges remains a computational cost. Second, the parent used a multiplicative combination of relative proximity, slack, and directional factors, while the final code integrates budget feasibility directly into the cluster radius, making the heuristic adaptive to the specific remaining budget for each edge. Third, the parent applied a hard feasibility mask after computing scores, whereas the final code skips infeasible edges entirely during the loop, avoiding unnecessary computation. Both versions normalize outputs to $[10^{-9}, 1]$ and handle edge cases like zero prizes or non-finite distances similarly, but the final code’s cluster-based valuation is the core innovation, aiming to capture strategic value beyond immediate local gains.

### 最终代码

```python
import numpy as np

def heuristics(prize: np.ndarray, distance: np.ndarray, maxlen: float) -> np.ndarray:
    """Return edge desirability values for OP ant colony optimization.

    Args:
        prize: Node prizes with shape (n,). Node 0 is the depot.
        distance: Pairwise Euclidean distances with shape (n, n).
            Diagonal entries are large sentinels so self-loops are unused.
        maxlen: Maximum allowed tour length (return-to-depot constrained).

    Returns:
        An (n, n) edge-prior matrix. Larger values make an edge more likely
        to be sampled. Values at or below zero are treated as 1e-9.
    """
    prize = np.asarray(prize, dtype=float)
    distance = np.asarray(distance, dtype=float)
    n = prize.shape[0]

    if n == 1:
        return np.array([[1e-9]])

    prize = np.maximum(prize, 0.0)
    if np.max(prize) <= 0 or maxlen <= 0:
        return np.full((n, n), 1e-9)

    # Handle non-finite distances
    big = np.finfo(float).max
    dist_safe = np.where(np.isfinite(distance), distance, big)
    dist_safe = np.maximum(dist_safe, 1e-8)

    d0 = distance[:, 0].copy()
    d0 = np.where(np.isfinite(d0), d0, 1.0)
    d0 = np.maximum(d0, 1e-8)

    # Global scale estimation
    off_mask = ~np.eye(n, dtype=bool)
    off_finite = dist_safe[off_mask]
    off_finite = off_finite[off_finite < big]
    if off_finite.size > 0:
        med_dist = float(np.median(off_finite))
        max_dist = float(np.max(off_finite))
    else:
        med_dist = 1.0
        max_dist = 1.0
    med_dist = max(med_dist, 1e-8)

    # Estimate global prize density: average prize per unit area (approximated by distance^2)
    # We use a robust measure: median prize / median distance
    med_prize = np.median(prize[1:]) if n > 1 else 0.0
    global_density = med_prize / (med_dist * med_dist) if med_dist > 0 else 0.0
    if global_density <= 0:
        global_density = 1e-9

    # For each node j, pre-compute a "potential density" profile
    # H_ij = (Prize_j + Sum of nearby high-value prizes within budget) / (d_ij + d_j0)
    # But we want to estimate the density achievable *from* j.

    # Let's define for each node j, a value V_j(B) = total prize collectable from j
    # with remaining budget B (after returning to depot).
    # We approximate this by summing prizes of nodes within radius R_j = B / 2
    # (since they need to be visited and potentially returned from, or chained).
    # Actually, a better approximation for marginal density is:
    # For edge i->j, the remaining budget for *further* collection is
    # B_rem = maxlen - d_ij - d_j0.
    # The value of this move is prize_j + expected value of next moves.
    # We approximate the "next moves value" by the density of prizes in the vicinity of j.

    # Precompute for each node j, a sorted list of (distance_to_other, prize_of_other)
    # to allow quick summation of top-k prizes within a radius.
    # Since n is likely small enough for O(n^2 log n) preprocessing, we do this.

    # Sort distances and prizes for each node j
    sorted_indices = np.argsort(dist_safe, axis=1)
    sorted_dists = np.take_along_axis(dist_safe, sorted_indices, axis=1)
    sorted_prizes = np.take_along_axis(prize[sorted_indices], np.arange(n)[:, np.newaxis], axis=0)

    # Cumulative sum of prizes for quick range queries
    cum_prizes = np.zeros((n, n))
    cum_prizes[:, 0] = sorted_prizes[:, 0]
    cum_prizes[:, 1:] = sorted_prizes[:, 1:] + cum_prizes[:, :-1]

    h = np.zeros((n, n))

    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            if i == j:
                continue

            d_ij = dist_safe[i, j]
            d_j0 = d0[j]

            # Hard feasibility: must be able to return to depot
            if d_ij + d_j0 > maxlen:
                continue

            # Remaining budget for further collection from j
            B_rem = maxlen - d_ij - d_j0
            if B_rem <= 0:
                # Can only get prize_j
                h[i, j] = prize[j] / (d_ij + d_j0)
                continue

            # Estimate value of cluster around j within B_rem
            # A node k is "reachable" from j within B_rem if d_jk + d_k0 <= B_rem + d_j0
            # (approximation: we can visit k and still return).
            # Let's use a radius R = B_rem * 0.5 to be conservative.
            R = B_rem * 0.5

            # Find nodes within R of j
            # Using pre-sorted data
            mask = sorted_dists[j, :] <= R
            # Exclude j itself? No, include it.
            # But we already count prize_j separately?
            # Let's define Total_Value = prize_j + sum(prizes of other nodes within R that are feasible)

            # Simple approximation: sum of top prizes within R
            if np.any(mask):
                # Get the prizes of nodes within R
                # We need to be careful with the cumulative sum approach
                # Let's just sum directly for simplicity if n is small,
                # or use the sorted array.
                # Since we have sorted_dists and sorted_prizes,
                # we can find the index where dist <= R
                idx = np.searchsorted(sorted_dists[j, :], R, side='right')
                if idx > 0:
                    # Sum of prizes of first idx nodes
                    sum_cluster = cum_prizes[j, idx - 1]
                    # This includes j itself (dist 0)
                    # So Total_Value = sum_cluster
                    # But we should only count nodes that can actually be visited.
                    # This is a heuristic approximation.

                    # The marginal gain of moving i->j is:
                    # Value = sum_cluster
                    # Cost = d_ij + d_j0 (to get to j and return)
                    # But we are using B_rem which is the budget *after* returning.
                    # So the "efficiency" is (Value_j + Value_cluster) / (d_ij + d_j0)

                    h[i, j] = sum_cluster / (d_ij + d_j0)
                else:
                    h[i, j] = prize[j] / (d_ij + d_j0)
            else:
                h[i, j] = prize[j] / (d_ij + d_j0)

    # Add a directional bias: prefer moves that reduce distance to depot
    delta_d = d0[:, np.newaxis] - d0[np.newaxis, :]
    direction_factor = 1.0 + 0.1 * np.tanh(delta_d / med_dist)

    h = h * direction_factor

    # Normalize
    h = np.nan_to_num(h, nan=1e-9, posinf=big, neginf=1e-9)
    h = np.maximum(h, 1e-9)

    max_h = np.max(h)
    if max_h > 1e-9:
        h = h / max_h
        h = np.maximum(h, 1e-9)

    return h
```

### 父代码

```python
import numpy as np

def heuristics(prize: np.ndarray, distance: np.ndarray, maxlen: float) -> np.ndarray:
    """Return edge desirability values for OP ant colony optimization.

    Args:
        prize: Node prizes with shape (n,). Node 0 is the depot.
        distance: Pairwise Euclidean distances with shape (n, n).
            Diagonal entries are large sentinels so self-loops are unused.
        maxlen: Maximum allowed tour length (return-to-depot constrained).

    Returns:
        An (n, n) edge-prior matrix. Larger values make an edge more likely
        to be sampled. Values at or below zero are treated as 1e-9.
    """
    prize = np.asarray(prize, dtype=float)
    distance = np.asarray(distance, dtype=float)
    n = prize.shape[0]

    if n == 1:
        return np.array([[1e-9]])

    prize = np.maximum(prize, 0.0)
    if np.max(prize) <= 0:
        return np.full((n, n), 1e-9)

    if maxlen <= 0:
        return np.full((n, n), 1e-9)

    # Calculate median distance for normalization
    off_mask = ~np.eye(n, dtype=bool)
    off_finite = distance[off_mask]
    off_finite = off_finite[np.isfinite(off_finite)]
    if off_finite.size > 0:
        med_dist = float(np.median(off_finite))
    else:
        med_dist = 1.0
    med_dist = max(med_dist, 1e-8)

    eps = 1e-6
    big = np.finfo(float).max
    dist_safe = np.where(np.isfinite(distance), distance, big)
    dist_safe = np.maximum(dist_safe, eps)

    # Distance from each node to depot
    d0 = distance[:, 0].copy()
    d0 = np.where(np.isfinite(d0), d0, med_dist)
    d0 = np.maximum(d0, eps)

    # 1. Base local efficiency: prize_j / d_ij
    base = prize[np.newaxis, :] / dist_safe

    # 2. Relative proximity factor
    rel_factor = (med_dist / dist_safe) ** 0.6

    # 3. Directional bias: favor moves that reduce distance to depot
    # delta_d[i, j] = d_i0 - d_j0. If positive, j is closer to depot than i.
    delta_d = d0[:, np.newaxis] - d0[np.newaxis, :]
    direction_factor = 1.0 + 0.1 * np.tanh(delta_d / med_dist)

    # 4. Budget headroom factor
    # Slack is the remaining budget if we go i->j->0.
    cost = dist_safe + d0[np.newaxis, :]
    slack = np.maximum(0.0, maxlen - cost)
    slack_norm = slack / maxlen
    # Use exponent 0.5 as in the best performing step (Step 5)
    slack_factor = np.maximum(slack_norm, 0.0) ** 0.5

    h = base * rel_factor * slack_factor * direction_factor

    # 5. Apply hard feasibility mask
    # An edge (i, j) is infeasible if d_ij + d_j0 > maxlen
    # Note: This is a necessary condition for feasibility if we were to end the tour at j,
    # but in OP, we can continue. However, if d_ij + d_j0 > maxlen, it implies that
    # even if we went directly to j and back, we'd be over budget.
    # Wait, the hard mask in Step 5 was: if d_ij + d_j0 > maxlen, set to 0.
    # This is actually a strong constraint. Is it always valid?
    # If d_ij + d_j0 > maxlen, can we still visit j?
    # The tour is 0 -> ... -> i -> j -> ... -> 0.
    # Total length >= d_ij + d_j0 ? No, total length is sum of edges.
    # The direct return d_j0 is the minimum cost to return from j.
    # So if d_ij + d_j0 > maxlen, it means that even if we were at i, went to j, and returned directly, we would exceed maxlen.
    # Since any valid tour visiting j must include the path to j and the path from j to 0 (which is at least d_j0),
    # and the path to j from 0 is at least d_0i + d_ij (triangle inequality: d_0j <= d_0i + d_ij, so d_0i + d_ij >= d_0j, but that doesn't help bound the whole tour).
    # Actually, if d_ij + d_j0 > maxlen, it doesn't strictly mean the tour is infeasible, because we haven't paid the cost 0->...->i yet.
    # However, Step 5 found this mask very effective. Why?
    # If d_ij + d_j0 > maxlen, it suggests the edge is very long or j is very far from depot.
    # Let's stick to the mask used in Step 5 as it yielded the highest fitness.

    infeasible = (dist_safe + d0[np.newaxis, :]) > maxlen
    h[infeasible] = 0.0

    # Zero out self-loops
    diag_idx = np.diag_indices(n)
    h[diag_idx] = 0.0

    # Sanitize
    h = np.nan_to_num(h, nan=1e-9, posinf=big, neginf=1e-9)
    h = np.maximum(h, 1e-9)

    # Normalize to [1e-9, 1]
    max_h = np.max(h)
    if max_h > 1e-9:
        h = h / max_h
        h = np.maximum(h, 1e-9)

    return h
```

</details>

## 18 tsp_construct_r1_e55

算子 Fuse；节点 38；父节点 29；fitness -8.722592309666492；局部纠错：是。

增益：将原版较大剩余集阶段的 O(n log n) 改成 O(n^2)，给出了实际四项评分。

保留问题或边界：纠正仅针对启发式阶段；尾部 bitmask DP 仍是指数代价。摘要未突出阈值由 12 提到 15；“immediate weight dominant”也并非所有 n 都成立。

<details><summary>原始 Idea、校准 Idea、最终代码与父代码</summary>

### 前置 Idea

Use a two-tier strategy. For small remaining sets (<= 15), finish exactly with bitmask DP so the tail is optimal. For larger sets, pick the next node from a modest candidate beam (nearest to the current node, plus the best 2-step "toward-destination" node and the most isolated node to prevent stranding), and score each candidate with a cheap, vectorized forward-completion estimate that blends the immediate edge, a 2-step continuation cost that accounts for where we must eventually go (toward the destination), an isolation penalty for nodes that would be expensive to connect later, and a mild destination pull. This keeps per-step work low (O(n log n)) while looking ahead far enough to beat the myopic 2-step reference and avoids the expensive simulated 2-opt of the current algorithm.

### 校准 Idea

The algorithm employs a hybrid strategy that switches between exact dynamic programming for small subproblems and a heuristic beam search for larger instances. For any remaining set of 15 or fewer nodes, the function delegates to `_exact_dp_next`, which computes the optimal tour completion using bitmask dynamic programming. This ensures that the "tail" of the TSP tour is solved optimally, preventing myopic errors in the final stretch. For larger instances, the algorithm constructs a candidate beam of size $K = \min(12, n)$ centered on nodes nearest to the current position, while explicitly including the node with the best 2-step forward completion potential and the most isolated node (highest minimum distance to other unvisited nodes) to mitigate stranding risks.

The core decision rule for the heuristic phase is a linear weighted sum of four normalized cost components. For each candidate node $u$, the score is calculated as $Score(u) = w_{cur} \frac{d(cur, u)}{scale_{cur}} + w_{fwd} \frac{fwd2(u)}{scale_{fwd}} + w_{nn} \frac{nn(u)}{scale_{nn}} + w_{dest} \frac{d(u, dest)}{scale_{dest}}$. The term $d(cur, u)$ captures the immediate travel cost. The term $fwd2(u)$ represents a 2-step lookahead, defined as $\min_{v \neq u} (d(u, v) + d(v, dest))$, which evaluates the cost of visiting a neighbor $v$ and then proceeding toward the destination; this is computed via matrix-vector addition and minimization. The term $nn(u)$ is the minimum distance from $u$ to any other unvisited node, serving as a penalty for nodes that are far from the remaining cluster, thus encouraging the selection of nodes that maintain connectivity. The term $d(u, dest)$ is a direct pull toward the destination, weighted by an adaptive factor $w_{dest}$ that increases as the number of unvisited nodes decreases, reinforcing the need to close the tour.

Key parameters include the beam size $K=12$, which balances computational overhead against search breadth, and the weights $w_{cur}, w_{fwd}, w_{nn}, w_{dest}$. The immediate cost weight $w_{cur}$ is dominant but dynamically adjusted to sum to 1.0 with the others, ensuring that as the algorithm nears completion (high $t$), the destination pull increases relative to the immediate edge cost. This approach is significantly faster than the parent algorithm because it avoids the expensive simulation of nearest-neighbor paths and iterative 2-opt repairs for each candidate. Instead, it relies on precomputed distance metrics and a single vectorized scoring pass. This reduction in per-step complexity from $O(K \cdot n^2)$ to approximately $O(n^2)$ (for submatrix extraction) plus $O(K)$ for scoring is expected to yield substantial speedups while maintaining competitive tour quality by combining local optimality at the tail with a robust, lookahead-informed heuristic for the bulk of the tour.

### 最终代码

```python
import numpy as np


def _exact_dp_next(current_node: int, destination_node: int, unvisited_nodes: np.ndarray, distance_matrix: np.ndarray) -> int:
    """Exact DP for small remainders: pick the first node that leads to the
    optimal completion of the sub-tour (current -> ... -> destination)."""
    m = len(unvisited_nodes)
    if m == 0:
        return destination_node
    if m == 1:
        return int(unvisited_nodes[0])

    size = 1 << m
    inf = float('inf')

    # f[mask, i] = min cost to visit exactly the nodes in mask, ending at i (i in mask),
    # then going to destination.
    f = np.full((size, m), inf, dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(m):
        f[1 << i, i] = distance_matrix[unvisited_nodes[i], destination_node]

    for mask in range(1, size):
        for last_idx in range(m):
            if not (mask & (1 << last_idx)):
                continue
            if mask == (1 << last_idx):
                continue
            rem = mask ^ (1 << last_idx)
            best = inf
            # best = min over nxt in rem of d(last, nxt) + f[rem, nxt]
            for nxt in range(m):
                if rem & (1 << nxt):
                    val = distance_matrix[unvisited_nodes[last_idx], unvisited_nodes[nxt]] + f[rem, nxt]
                    if val < best:
                        best = val
            f[mask, last_idx] = best

    full_mask = (1 << m) - 1
    best_total = inf
    best_node = -1
    for i in range(m):
        total = distance_matrix[current_node, unvisited_nodes[i]] + f[full_mask, i]
        if total < best_total:
            best_total = total
            best_node = unvisited_nodes[i]
    return int(best_node)


def select_next_node(current_node: int, destination_node: int, unvisited_nodes: np.ndarray, distance_matrix: np.ndarray) -> int:
    """
    Select the next node using a cheap forward-completion scoring over a small
    candidate beam, with exact DP for small tails.
    """
    n_unvisited = int(len(unvisited_nodes))

    if n_unvisited == 0:
        return destination_node
    if n_unvisited == 1:
        return int(unvisited_nodes[0])

    # Exact tail for small remainders
    if n_unvisited <= 15:
        return _exact_dp_next(current_node, destination_node, unvisited_nodes, distance_matrix)

    # Distances to/from current and destination
    d_cur = distance_matrix[current_node, unvisited_nodes].astype(np.float64)
    d_dest = distance_matrix[unvisited_nodes, destination_node].astype(np.float64)

    # Submatrix among unvisited nodes
    sub = distance_matrix[np.ix_(unvisited_nodes, unvisited_nodes)].astype(np.float64).copy()
    np.fill_diagonal(sub, np.inf)

    # Nearest-neighbor distance within unvisited set (isolation proxy)
    nn_dist = sub.min(axis=1)

    # 2-step forward completion: min over other v of d(u,v) + d(v, dest)
    # B[u, v] = d(u,v) + d(v, dest); min over v != u
    B = sub + d_dest[np.newaxis, :]
    fwd2 = B.min(axis=1)  # diagonal is inf, so min skips self

    # Build a modest beam: nearest to current, plus best fwd2 node, plus most isolated node
    K = min(12, n_unvisited)
    beam = list(np.argsort(d_cur)[:K])

    # Add the node with best 2-step forward completion if not present
    best_fwd_idx = int(np.argmin(fwd2))
    if best_fwd_idx not in beam:
        beam.append(best_fwd_idx)

    # Add the most isolated node (max nn_dist) if not present
    iso_idx = int(np.argmax(nn_dist))
    if iso_idx not in beam:
        beam.append(iso_idx)

    # Compute score over the beam
    # Normalize scales for stable weighting
    scale_cur = float(np.max(d_cur)) if np.max(d_cur) > 0 else 1.0
    scale_fwd = float(np.max(fwd2)) if np.max(fwd2) > 0 else 1.0
    scale_nn = float(np.max(nn_dist)) if np.max(nn_dist) > 0 else 1.0
    scale_dest = float(np.max(d_dest)) if np.max(d_dest) > 0 else 1.0

    # Adaptive weight on destination pull: stronger as we approach the end
    t = 1.0 / (1.0 + n_unvisited / 20.0)  # small when many remain, larger near end
    w_dest = 0.15 + 0.35 * t
    w_fwd = 0.35
    w_nn = 0.10
    w_cur = 1.0 - w_dest - w_fwd - w_nn  # keep immediate edge dominant
    if w_cur < 0.1:
        w_cur = 0.1

    # Vectorize over beam
    b_idx = np.array(beam, dtype=int)
    s_cur = d_cur[b_idx] / scale_cur
    s_fwd = fwd2[b_idx] / scale_fwd
    s_nn = nn_dist[b_idx] / scale_nn
    s_dest = d_dest[b_idx] / scale_dest

    scores = w_cur * s_cur + w_fwd * s_fwd + w_nn * s_nn + w_dest * s_dest

    best_pos = int(np.argmin(scores))
    best_idx = b_idx[best_pos]
    return int(unvisited_nodes[best_idx])
```

### 父代码

```python
import numpy as np

def _exact_dp_next(current_node: int, destination_node: int, unvisited_nodes: np.ndarray, distance_matrix: np.ndarray) -> int:
    """Exact DP for small remainders: pick the first node that leads to the
    optimal completion of the sub‑tour (current -> ... -> destination)."""
    m = len(unvisited_nodes)
    if m == 0:
        return destination_node
    if m == 1:
        return int(unvisited_nodes[0])

    size = 1 << m
    inf = float('inf')

    f = np.full((size, m), inf, dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(m):
        f[1 << i, i] = distance_matrix[unvisited_nodes[i], destination_node]

    for mask in range(1, size):
        for last_idx in range(m):
            if not (mask & (1 << last_idx)):
                continue
            if mask == (1 << last_idx):
                continue
            rem = mask ^ (1 << last_idx)
            best = inf
            for nxt in range(m):
                if rem & (1 << nxt):
                    val = distance_matrix[unvisited_nodes[last_idx], unvisited_nodes[nxt]] + f[rem, nxt]
                    if val < best:
                        best = val
            f[mask, last_idx] = best

    full_mask = (1 << m) - 1
    best_total = inf
    best_node = -1
    for i in range(m):
        total = distance_matrix[current_node, unvisited_nodes[i]] + f[full_mask, i]
        if total < best_total:
            best_total = total
            best_node = unvisited_nodes[i]
    return int(best_node)

def _nn_path_cost(start_node, path_nodes, distance_matrix):
    """Calculate the cost of a path starting from start_node visiting path_nodes in order."""
    if len(path_nodes) == 0:
        return 0.0
    cost = distance_matrix[start_node, path_nodes[0]]
    for i in range(len(path_nodes) - 1):
        cost += distance_matrix[path_nodes[i], path_nodes[i+1]]
    return cost

def _two_opt(path, distance_matrix, destination_node, current_node):
    """
    Apply 2-opt improvement to the path.
    path is a list of node IDs representing the sequence of unvisited nodes.
    The full tour is: current_node -> path[0] -> ... -> path[-1] -> destination_node -> current_node
    We optimize the segment current_node -> path -> destination_node.
    """
    if len(path) < 3:
        return path

    # Create a copy to avoid modifying the original
    p = list(path)
    n = len(p)

    # We treat the path as a cycle segment from current_node to destination_node.
    # To do 2-opt, we need to consider the connections:
    # current_node -> p[0]
    # p[i] -> p[i+1]
    # p[-1] -> destination_node

    # Standard 2-opt on an open path with fixed endpoints:
    # We reverse a segment p[i..j] and check if the cost decreases.
    # Cost before: d(curr, p[0]) + sum(d(p[i], p[i+1])) + d(p[-1], dest)
    # After reversing p[i..j]:
    # If i=0: d(curr, p[j]) + d(p[j-1], p[j+1]) ... (careful with boundaries)
    # Let's implement a standard 2-opt for open paths.

    improved = True
    while improved:
        improved = False
        for i in range(n - 1):
            # i is the index of the first node in the segment to reverse
            # The edge before p[i] is either (curr, p[0]) if i==0 or (p[i-1], p[i])
            if i == 0:
                edge_prev_node = current_node
                edge_prev_val = distance_matrix[current_node, p[0]]
            else:
                edge_prev_node = p[i-1]
                edge_prev_val = distance_matrix[p[i-1], p[i]]

            # The edge after p[j] is either (p[n-1], dest) if j==n-1 or (p[j], p[j+1])
            # We are trying to reverse p[i..j]
            # The edge entering p[i] is (u, p[i]) where u is curr or p[i-1]
            # The edge entering p[j+1] is (p[j], v) where v is dest or p[j+1]

            # Actually, simpler approach:
            # Remove edges (u, p[i]) and (p[j], v)
            # Add edges (u, p[j]) and (p[i], v)
            # where u is the node before p[i] in the current sequence,
            # and v is the node after p[j] in the current sequence.

            for j in range(i + 1, n):
                # Determine u and v
                if i == 0:
                    u = current_node
                else:
                    u = p[i-1]

                if j == n - 1:
                    v = destination_node
                else:
                    v = p[j+1]

                # Current cost of these two edges
                c1 = distance_matrix[u, p[i]]
                c2 = distance_matrix[p[j], v]
                old_cost = c1 + c2

                # New cost after reversing p[i..j]
                # The new edges are (u, p[j]) and (p[i], v)
                # Wait, if we reverse p[i..j], the sequence becomes ... u, p[j], ..., p[i], v ...
                # So the new edges are (u, p[j]) and (p[i], v)
                c3 = distance_matrix[u, p[j]]
                c4 = distance_matrix[p[i], v]
                new_cost = c3 + c4

                if new_cost < old_cost - 1e-10:
                    # Perform the reversal
                    p[i:j+1] = p[i:j+1][::-1]
                    improved = True
                    # Restart search from the beginning to find more improvements
                    break
            if improved:
                break
    return p

def select_next_node(current_node: int, destination_node: int, unvisited_nodes: np.ndarray, distance_matrix: np.ndarray) -> int:
    """
    Select the next node using a Lookahead 2-Opt Repair Heuristic.
    For each candidate in a beam, simulate the rest of the tour using Nearest Neighbor,
    apply 2-opt repair, and pick the candidate that yields the lowest estimated tour length.
    """
    n_unvisited = int(len(unvisited_nodes))

    if n_unvisited == 0:
        return destination_node
    if n_unvisited == 1:
        return int(unvisited_nodes[0])

    if n_unvisited <= 12:
        return _exact_dp_next(current_node, destination_node, unvisited_nodes, distance_matrix)

    # Identify candidate nodes
    # 1. Nearest neighbors to current node
    cur_d = distance_matrix[current_node, unvisited_nodes]

    # 2. Nearest to destination (to help closing the loop)
    dest_d = distance_matrix[unvisited_nodes, destination_node]

    # 3. Most isolated (highest min distance to other unvisited) to avoid stranding
    sub = distance_matrix[np.ix_(unvisited_nodes, unvisited_nodes)].astype(np.float64).copy()
    np.fill_diagonal(sub, np.inf)
    nn_dist = sub.min(axis=1)

    # Beam construction
    K = min(10, n_unvisited)

    # Indices of top K nearest to current
    beam_indices = list(np.argsort(cur_d)[:K])

    # Add index of node nearest to destination
    dest_idx = int(np.argmin(dest_d))
    if dest_idx not in beam_indices:
        beam_indices.append(dest_idx)

    # Add index of most isolated node
    iso_idx = int(np.argmax(nn_dist))
    if iso_idx not in beam_indices:
        beam_indices.append(iso_idx)

    beam_indices = np.array(beam_indices, dtype=int)
    candidates = unvisited_nodes[beam_indices]

    best_cost = float('inf')
    best_candidate = -1

    # Pre-compute submatrix for NN steps to speed up
    # We will simulate NN path for each candidate

    for idx in beam_indices:
        candidate_node = int(candidates[np.where(beam_indices == idx)[0][0]]) # Just use unvisited_nodes[idx] directly
        candidate_node = int(unvisited_nodes[idx])

        # Simulate the rest of the tour
        remaining = list(unvisited_nodes)
        remaining.remove(candidate_node)

        if not remaining:
            est_cost = distance_matrix[current_node, candidate_node] + distance_matrix[candidate_node, destination_node]
        else:
            # Start from candidate_node
            path = [candidate_node]
            last_node = candidate_node
            rem_set = set(remaining)

            # Nearest Neighbor construction
            while rem_set:
                # Find nearest to last_node among rem_set
                # Convert rem_set to list for indexing? Or just iterate
                # For efficiency, we can use the precomputed submatrix
                # But rem_set changes, so we need to check distances to all in rem_set
                # This is O(m^2) for the whole simulation, which is okay for m < 500

                best_next = -1
                best_dist = float('inf')
                # Optimization: Instead of iterating all, we can use argsort on a slice
                # But rem_set is a set, so we need to mask or list it
                # Let's convert to list once per candidate
                rem_list = list(rem_set)
                dists = distance_matrix[last_node, rem_list]
                min_idx = np.argmin(dists)
                best_next = rem_list[min_idx]
                best_dist = dists[min_idx]

                path.append(best_next)
                last_node = best_next
                rem_set.remove(best_next)

            # Path is now: candidate -> ... -> last_node
            # Full tour segment: current -> candidate -> ... -> last_node -> destination
            base_cost = distance_matrix[current_node, candidate_node] + _nn_path_cost(candidate_node, path[1:], distance_matrix) + distance_matrix[last_node, destination_node]

            # Apply 2-opt to the path part (candidate -> ... -> last_node)
            # The path to optimize is path[1:] because candidate is fixed as the next step
            # Wait, the 2-opt should optimize the entire segment from current to destination?
            # No, the choice is the *next* node. The subsequent path can be optimized.
            # So we fix the edge current->candidate.
            # The rest is path[1:] ending at destination.
            # We can apply 2-opt to path[1:] with start=current_node? No, start is candidate.
            # The segment is candidate -> path[1][0] -> ... -> path[-1] -> destination
            # We can treat candidate as the "current_node" for the 2-opt of the sub-path path[1:]

            if len(path) > 1:
                optimized_tail = _two_opt(path[1:], distance_matrix, destination_node, candidate_node)
                # Recalculate cost with optimized tail
                if optimized_tail:
                    tail_cost = _nn_path_cost(candidate_node, optimized_tail, distance_matrix) + distance_matrix[optimized_tail[-1], destination_node]
                else:
                    tail_cost = distance_matrix[candidate_node, destination_node]
                est_cost = distance_matrix[current_node, candidate_node] + tail_cost
            else:
                est_cost = distance_matrix[current_node, candidate_node] + distance_matrix[candidate_node, destination_node]

        if est_cost < best_cost:
            best_cost = est_cost
            best_candidate = candidate_node

    return int(best_candidate)
```

算子 Fuse；节点 167；父节点 143；fitness -5.83294977028907；局部纠错：未计入。

增益：补充 K=min(24,max(12,N//3))、目标点候选、全局路径状态，以及 DP 10→12、2-opt 8→10。

保留问题或边界：主要规则与代码对应；“无显著额外开销”等运行效率判断未由本次核查证明，候选集合裁剪也可能丢掉某些特殊候选。

<details><summary>原始 Idea、校准 Idea、最终代码与父代码</summary>

### 前置 Idea

The algorithm employs a hybrid strategy that switches between exact dynamic programming (Held-Karp) for small remaining sub-problems and a sophisticated heuristic beam search for larger instances.
1. **Exact DP**: For $N \le 12$, it uses exact DP to guarantee optimal next moves, ensuring perfect tail construction.
2. **Heuristic Beam Search**: For $N > 12$, it constructs a diverse candidate beam of size $K$ (scaled with $N$). The beam includes nodes based on:
   - Proximity to the current node (to maintain local continuity).
   - Low composite cost ($d(current, v) + d(v, destination)$) to balance immediate and closing costs.
   - High isolation (max distance to nearest neighbor) to prevent stranding nodes that are hard to reach.
3. **High-Fidelity Evaluation**: Each candidate is evaluated by simulating the completion of the tour using a Nearest Neighbor (NN) heuristic followed by a robust Best-Improvement 2-Opt local search (up to 10 passes). This provides an accurate estimate of the total tour length resulting from choosing that candidate.
4. **Stateful Geometric Penalty**: To mitigate the "myopia" of pure cost-based lookahead, the algorithm maintains a global tour history. It applies a geometric penalty to candidates that would cause a "U-turn" or backtracking (where the candidate is significantly closer to the previously visited node than to the current node). This encourages smooth, sweeping paths.
5. **Selection**: The candidate with the minimum combined score (Estimated Tour Cost + Geometric Penalty) is selected.

This approach combines the global structural awareness of 2-Opt lookahead with local geometric consistency, aiming to outperform both the stateless cost-only approach (Current) and the potentially less robust reference implementation by optimizing the balance between exploration, exploitation, and path smoothness.

### 校准 Idea

The implemented algorithm employs a hybrid strategy that switches between exact dynamic programming for small sub-problems and a heuristic beam search with high-fidelity lookahead for larger instances. For instances with 12 or fewer unvisited nodes, the algorithm utilizes the Held-Karp dynamic programming approach to guarantee the optimal next move, ensuring perfect tail construction. For larger instances ($N > 12$), it constructs a diverse candidate beam of size $K$, which is adaptively scaled as $\min(24, \max(12, N/3))$. The beam is populated using four distinct criteria to ensure diversity and structural awareness: proximity to the current node (to maintain local continuity), low composite cost ($d(current, v) + d(v, destination)$) to balance immediate and closing costs, high isolation (max distance to nearest neighbor) to prevent stranding hard-to-reach nodes, and proximity to the destination node. Each candidate in the beam is evaluated by simulating the completion of the tour using a Nearest Neighbor (NN) heuristic, followed by a Best-Improvement 2-Opt local search (up to 10 passes) to refine the path and estimate the total tour length. This high-fidelity evaluation allows the algorithm to assess the global structural impact of each choice rather than relying on myopic local costs. Furthermore, a stateful geometric penalty is applied to candidates that would cause a "U-turn" or backtracking (where the candidate is significantly closer to the previously visited node than to the current node), encouraging smooth, sweeping paths. The candidate with the minimum combined score (Estimated Tour Cost + Geometric Penalty) is selected.

Important implementation changes relative to the parent code include the introduction of a global state mechanism (`GLOBAL_TOUR` and `TSP_START_NODE`) to track the tour history across function calls. This state enables the calculation of the geometric penalty, which was not present in the parent code, allowing the algorithm to penalize backtracking moves that create inefficient loops. The exact DP threshold was increased from 10 to 12 nodes, expanding the range where optimal solutions are guaranteed without significant computational overhead. The beam construction was streamlined by removing the redundant "sweep potential" calculation loop, which was computationally expensive and less directly useful than the isolation and composite cost metrics. The 2-Opt optimization was enhanced by increasing the maximum passes from 8 to 10, improving the quality of the lookahead estimates. Additionally, the code structure was refactored for clarity and efficiency, separating helper functions for path length calculation and NN completion, and ensuring robust handling of edge cases where the tour state is inconsistent or reset. These changes collectively improve the algorithm's ability to balance local geometric consistency with global structural optimization, potentially leading to shorter tour lengths compared to the parent implementation.

### 最终代码

```python
import numpy as np

# Global state to track the tour for geometric consistency
GLOBAL_TOUR = []
TSP_START_NODE = -1

def _exact_dp_next(current_node: int, destination_node: int, unvisited_nodes: np.ndarray, distance_matrix: np.ndarray) -> int:
    """
    Exact DP for small remainders.
    f[mask, i] = min cost to start at i, visit all nodes in mask, and end at destination_node.
    """
    m = len(unvisited_nodes)
    if m == 0:
        return destination_node
    if m == 1:
        return int(unvisited_nodes[0])

    size = 1 << m
    inf = float('inf')

    f = np.full((size, m), inf, dtype=np.float64)

    for i in range(m):
        f[1 << i, i] = distance_matrix[unvisited_nodes[i], destination_node]

    for mask in range(1, size):
        for last_idx in range(m):
            if not (mask & (1 << last_idx)):
                continue

            if mask == (1 << last_idx):
                continue

            rem = mask ^ (1 << last_idx)
            best = inf

            for prev_idx in range(m):
                if rem & (1 << prev_idx):
                    val = distance_matrix[unvisited_nodes[last_idx], unvisited_nodes[prev_idx]] + f[rem, prev_idx]
                    if val < best:
                        best = val
            f[mask, last_idx] = best

    full_mask = (1 << m) - 1
    best_total = inf
    best_node = -1
    for i in range(m):
        total = distance_matrix[current_node, unvisited_nodes[i]] + f[full_mask, i]
        if total < best_total:
            best_total = total
            best_node = unvisited_nodes[i]

    return int(best_node)

def _nn_completion_fast(last, rem_list, dist):
    """
    Fast Nearest Neighbor completion.
    """
    if len(rem_list) == 0:
        return [last], 0.0

    path = [last]
    cur = last
    total = 0.0
    rem = list(rem_list)

    while rem:
        arr = np.asarray(rem, dtype=int)
        d = dist[cur, arr]
        idx = int(np.argmin(d))
        nxt = int(arr[idx])
        total += float(d[idx])
        path.append(nxt)
        rem.pop(idx)
        cur = nxt

    return path, total

def _two_opt_best_improve_open(p, dist, first, last, max_passes=10):
    """
    Best-improvement 2-opt for an open path [first] + p + [last].
    Returns the optimized path (excluding endpoints first and last).
    """
    n = len(p)
    if n < 3:
        return p

    p = list(p)
    improved_any = False
    for _ in range(max_passes):
        best_gain = 1e-9
        best_i, best_j = -1, -1

        for i in range(n - 1):
            u = first if i == 0 else p[i - 1]
            a = p[i]

            for j in range(i + 1, n):
                b = p[j]
                v = last if j == n - 1 else p[j + 1]

                old = dist[u, a] + dist[b, v]
                new = dist[u, b] + dist[a, v]
                gain = old - new

                if gain > best_gain:
                    best_gain = gain
                    best_i = i
                    best_j = j

        if best_i != -1:
            p[best_i:best_j + 1] = p[best_i:best_j + 1][::-1]
            improved_any = True
        else:
            break

    return p

def _open_path_len(p, dist, first, last):
    """Calculate total length of path: first -> p[0] -> ... -> p[-1] -> last."""
    if not p:
        return float(dist[first, last])

    s = float(dist[first, p[0]])
    for k in range(1, len(p)):
        s += float(dist[p[k - 1], p[k]])
    s += float(dist[p[-1], last])
    return s

def _geometric_penalty(prev_node, current_node, candidate_node, dist):
    """
    Penalizes U-turns or backtracking moves.
    """
    if prev_node == -1:
        return 0.0

    d_pc = float(dist[prev_node, current_node])
    d_cc = float(dist[current_node, candidate_node])
    d_pv = float(dist[prev_node, candidate_node])

    # If the candidate is much closer to the previous node than to the current node,
    # it suggests a U-turn or backtracking.
    if d_pv < 0.5 * d_cc:
        return (d_cc - d_pv) * 0.5

    return 0.0

def select_next_node(current_node: int, destination_node: int, unvisited_nodes: np.ndarray, distance_matrix: np.ndarray) -> int:
    """
    Hybrid Lookahead Beam Search with Geometric Consistency Penalty and Robust 2-Opt.
    """
    global GLOBAL_TOUR, TSP_START_NODE

    # Maintain global state for geometric consistency
    if len(GLOBAL_TOUR) == 0:
        TSP_START_NODE = current_node
        GLOBAL_TOUR.append(current_node)
    else:
        # Reset if the tour state is inconsistent (e.g., new instance or reset)
        if GLOBAL_TOUR[-1] != current_node:
            GLOBAL_TOUR = [current_node]
            TSP_START_NODE = current_node

    n_unvisited = int(len(unvisited_nodes))

    if n_unvisited == 0:
        return destination_node

    if n_unvisited == 1:
        next_node = int(unvisited_nodes[0])
        GLOBAL_TOUR.append(next_node)
        return next_node

    # Use exact DP for small instances
    if n_unvisited <= 12:
        next_node = _exact_dp_next(current_node, destination_node, unvisited_nodes, distance_matrix)
        GLOBAL_TOUR.append(next_node)
        return int(next_node)

    prev_node = GLOBAL_TOUR[-2] if len(GLOBAL_TOUR) >= 2 else TSP_START_NODE

    # Precompute distances for beam construction
    cur_d = distance_matrix[current_node, unvisited_nodes].astype(np.float64)
    dest_d = distance_matrix[unvisited_nodes, destination_node].astype(np.float64)
    composite = cur_d + dest_d

    # Adaptive beam size
    K = min(24, max(12, n_unvisited // 3))

    # Build beam indices from multiple criteria
    idx_near = np.argsort(cur_d)[:max(1, K//2)]
    idx_comp = np.argsort(composite)[:max(1, K//2)]

    sub = distance_matrix[np.ix_(unvisited_nodes, unvisited_nodes)].astype(np.float64).copy()
    np.fill_diagonal(sub, np.inf)
    if sub.size > 0:
        nn_dist = sub.min(axis=1)
        idx_isolated = np.argmax(nn_dist)
    else:
        idx_isolated = 0

    idx_dest = np.argmin(dest_d)
    idx_best_comp = np.argmin(composite)

    beam_indices = set(idx_near.tolist())
    beam_indices.update(idx_comp.tolist())
    beam_indices.add(int(idx_isolated))
    beam_indices.add(int(idx_dest))
    beam_indices.add(int(idx_best_comp))

    # Trim beam if it exceeds K
    if len(beam_indices) > K:
        sorted_beams = sorted(beam_indices, key=lambda i: composite[i])
        beam_indices = set(sorted_beams[:K])

    best_score = float('inf')
    best_node_id = -1
    unvisited_ids = unvisited_nodes.astype(int)

    for idx in beam_indices:
        cand = int(unvisited_ids[idx])

        # Identify remaining nodes
        mask = np.ones(n_unvisited, dtype=bool)
        mask[idx] = False
        remaining_nodes = unvisited_ids[mask]

        if len(remaining_nodes) > 0:
            # Simulate completion using NN
            path, _ = _nn_completion_fast(cand, remaining_nodes, distance_matrix)
            inner_path = path[1:] if len(path) > 1 else []

            # Refine with 2-Opt
            if len(inner_path) >= 3:
                refined_inner = _two_opt_best_improve_open(inner_path, distance_matrix, cand, destination_node)
                seg_len = _open_path_len(refined_inner, distance_matrix, cand, destination_node)
                tail_cost = float(distance_matrix[current_node, cand]) + seg_len
            else:
                # Handle small tails without 2-Opt
                tail_cost = float(distance_matrix[current_node, cand])
                if inner_path:
                    prev = cand
                    for node in inner_path:
                        tail_cost += float(distance_matrix[prev, node])
                        prev = node
                    tail_cost += float(distance_matrix[prev, destination_node])
                else:
                    tail_cost += float(distance_matrix[cand, destination_node])
        else:
            tail_cost = float(distance_matrix[current_node, cand]) + float(distance_matrix[cand, destination_node])

        # Apply geometric penalty
        geo_pen = _geometric_penalty(prev_node, current_node, cand, distance_matrix)

        score = tail_cost + geo_pen

        if score < best_score:
            best_score = score
            best_node_id = cand

    if best_node_id == -1:
        best_node_id = int(unvisited_ids[int(np.argmin(cur_d))])

    GLOBAL_TOUR.append(best_node_id)
    return int(best_node_id)
```

### 父代码

```python
import numpy as np

def _exact_dp_next(current_node: int, destination_node: int, unvisited_nodes: np.ndarray, distance_matrix: np.ndarray) -> int:
    """
    Exact DP for small remainders.
    f[mask, i] = min cost to start at i, visit all nodes in mask, and end at destination_node.
    """
    m = len(unvisited_nodes)
    if m == 0:
        return destination_node
    if m == 1:
        return int(unvisited_nodes[0])

    size = 1 << m
    inf = float('inf')

    f = np.full((size, m), inf, dtype=np.float64)

    for i in range(m):
        f[1 << i, i] = distance_matrix[unvisited_nodes[i], destination_node]

    for mask in range(1, size):
        for last_idx in range(m):
            if not (mask & (1 << last_idx)):
                continue

            if mask == (1 << last_idx):
                continue

            rem = mask ^ (1 << last_idx)
            best = inf

            for prev_idx in range(m):
                if rem & (1 << prev_idx):
                    val = distance_matrix[unvisited_nodes[last_idx], unvisited_nodes[prev_idx]] + f[rem, prev_idx]
                    if val < best:
                        best = val
            f[mask, last_idx] = best

    full_mask = (1 << m) - 1
    best_total = inf
    best_node = -1
    for i in range(m):
        total = distance_matrix[current_node, unvisited_nodes[i]] + f[full_mask, i]
        if total < best_total:
            best_total = total
            best_node = unvisited_nodes[i]

    return int(best_node)

def _fast_nn_completion_3step(cand_node: int, rem_list: np.ndarray, dist: np.ndarray) -> float:
    """
    Estimates the cost of the next 3 steps using NN, then returns to destination.
    If fewer than 3 nodes remain, handles appropriately.
    """
    rem = list(rem_list)
    if not rem:
        return 0.0

    total = 0.0
    cur = cand_node
    steps = 0
    max_steps = min(3, len(rem))

    for _ in range(max_steps):
        arr = np.asarray(rem, dtype=int)
        d = dist[cur, arr]
        idx = int(np.argmin(d))
        nxt = int(arr[idx])
        total += float(d[idx])
        rem.pop(idx)
        cur = nxt
        steps += 1

    # Cost to finish remaining nodes roughly (using average distance to dest as a heuristic proxy for tail)
    if rem:
        arr = np.asarray(rem, dtype=int)
        # Heuristic: cost to finish is roughly sum of dist to dest, but NN is better.
        # To keep it fast, we just add the min dist to dest for the last one and assume the rest is covered by the main 2-opt.
        # Actually, for the score comparison, we just need a consistent relative metric.
        # The 2-opt will handle the rest. This 3-step is just to break ties on "immediate" geometry.
        pass

    return total

def _fast_two_opt(p: list, dist: np.ndarray, first: int, last: int, max_passes: int = 8) -> list:
    """
    Fast Best-Improvement 2-Opt for an open path [first] + p + [last].
    Returns the optimized path (excluding endpoints first and last).
    """
    n = len(p)
    if n < 3:
        return p

    p = list(p)

    for _ in range(max_passes):
        best_gain = 1e-9
        best_i, best_j = -1, -1

        for i in range(n - 1):
            u = first if i == 0 else p[i - 1]
            a = p[i]

            for j in range(i + 1, n):
                b = p[j]
                v = last if j == n - 1 else p[j + 1]

                old = dist[u, a] + dist[b, v]
                new = dist[u, b] + dist[a, v]
                gain = old - new

                if gain > best_gain:
                    best_gain = gain
                    best_i = i
                    best_j = j

        if best_i != -1:
            p[best_i:best_j + 1] = p[best_i:best_j + 1][::-1]
        else:
            break

    return p

def _calc_sweep_potential(current_node: int, candidate_node: int, remaining_nodes: np.ndarray, dist: np.ndarray) -> float:
    """
    Calculates a sweep potential score. Lower is better.
    Encourages choosing a candidate where the turn from (current->candidate) to (candidate->next_best) is smooth.
    Uses the dot product of vectors in a projected space if coordinates were available,
    but here we only have distances. So we approximate smoothness by checking if the candidate is not a 'dead end'
    relative to its best neighbor in the remaining set.

    Actually, without coordinates, true angle calculation is impossible.
    We approximate "smoothness" by ensuring the candidate is close to its best neighbor in the remaining set,
    which implies it can easily continue the path.
    """
    if len(remaining_nodes) == 0:
        return 0.0

    # The 'sweep' idea in distance-only space:
    # If d(candidate, next_best) is small relative to d(current, candidate), it's a good continuation.
    # We already capture this in the NN 3-step.
    # Let's use a different proxy: The cost to enter the candidate and leave to its best neighbor.
    # If this is low, it's a good "gateway".

    arr = remaining_nodes
    d_out = dist[candidate_node, arr]
    min_out = np.min(d_out)
    d_in = dist[current_node, candidate_node]

    # Return a ratio or sum that favors smooth transitions
    # If min_out is very large compared to d_in, it's a spike.
    return d_in + min_out

def select_next_node(current_node: int, destination_node: int, unvisited_nodes: np.ndarray, distance_matrix: np.ndarray) -> int:
    """
    Hybrid Lookahead Beam Search with 2-Opt and Geometric Heuristics.
    """
    n_unvisited = int(len(unvisited_nodes))

    if n_unvisited == 0:
        return destination_node

    if n_unvisited == 1:
        return int(unvisited_nodes[0])

    if n_unvisited <= 10:
        return _exact_dp_next(current_node, destination_node, unvisited_nodes, distance_matrix)

    K = min(24, max(12, n_unvisited // 3))

    cur_d = distance_matrix[current_node, unvisited_nodes].astype(np.float64)
    dest_d = distance_matrix[unvisited_nodes, destination_node].astype(np.float64)
    composite = cur_d + dest_d

    beam_set = set()

    # 1. Nearest to current
    beam_set.update(int(i) for i in np.argsort(cur_d)[:K//2])

    # 2. Best composite (detour)
    beam_set.update(int(i) for i in np.argsort(composite)[:K//2])

    # 3. Most isolated (max min-dist) to prevent stranding
    sub = distance_matrix[np.ix_(unvisited_nodes, unvisited_nodes)].astype(np.float64).copy()
    np.fill_diagonal(sub, np.inf)
    if sub.size > 0:
        nn_dist = sub.min(axis=1)
        beam_set.add(int(np.argmax(nn_dist)))

        # 4. Best "sweep" potential (smoothest entry/exit)
        sweep_scores = np.zeros(len(unvisited_nodes))
        for i in range(len(unvisited_nodes)):
            cand = int(unvisited_nodes[i])
            rem_mask = np.ones(len(unvisited_nodes), dtype=bool)
            rem_mask[i] = False
            rem_nodes = unvisited_nodes[rem_mask]
            if len(rem_nodes) > 0:
                d_out = distance_matrix[cand, rem_nodes]
                sweep_scores[i] = distance_matrix[current_node, cand] + np.min(d_out)
            else:
                sweep_scores[i] = 0
        beam_set.add(int(np.argmin(sweep_scores)))

    # 5. Nearest to destination
    beam_set.add(int(np.argmin(dest_d)))

    beam = [i for i in beam_set if 0 <= i < n_unvisited]

    if not beam:
        return int(unvisited_nodes[int(np.argmin(cur_d))])

    best_score = float('inf')
    best_node_id = -1
    unvisited_ids = unvisited_nodes.astype(int)

    # Pre-calculate stranding penalty: cost of connecting the farthest nodes to destination
    # We use the max of (dist(node, dest) - avg_dist) as a proxy for stranding risk if left behind?
    # Actually, the 2-opt will handle the specific ordering. The stranding penalty is more about
    # not picking a node that leaves a very isolated node disconnected.

    for idx in beam:
        cand = int(unvisited_ids[idx])
        remaining = [x for x in unvisited_ids if x != cand]

        if len(remaining) > 0:
            # 1. NN Path
            path = []
            cur = cand
            rem = list(remaining)
            nn_len = 0.0
            while rem:
                arr = np.asarray(rem, dtype=int)
                d = distance_matrix[cur, arr]
                idx_min = int(np.argmin(d))
                nxt = int(arr[idx_min])
                nn_len += float(d[idx_min])
                path.append(nxt)
                rem.pop(idx_min)
                cur = nxt

            # 2. 2-Opt Refinement
            if len(path) >= 3:
                refined_inner = _fast_two_opt(path, distance_matrix, cand, destination_node, max_passes=8)

                # Calculate refined length
                last_node = refined_inner[-1] if refined_inner else cand
                # Open path length: cand -> ... -> last -> destination
                # Wait, _fast_two_opt returns the inner path. The endpoints are cand and destination.
                # So the path is [cand] + refined_inner + [destination]

                # Calculate length of [cand] + refined_inner + [destination]
                total_len = 0.0
                prev = cand
                for node in refined_inner:
                    total_len += float(distance_matrix[prev, node])
                    prev = node
                total_len += float(distance_matrix[prev, destination_node])

                tail_cost = float(distance_matrix[current_node, cand]) + total_len
            else:
                # If path was short, use nn_len + last to dest
                last_node = path[-1] if path else cand
                tail_cost = float(distance_matrix[current_node, cand]) + nn_len + float(distance_matrix[last_node, destination_node])
        else:
            tail_cost = float(distance_matrix[current_node, cand]) + float(distance_matrix[cand, destination_node])

        # Additional heuristic: Penalize if the candidate is far from its best neighbor in remaining (spike)
        # This is captured somewhat by the 2-opt, but let's add a small penalty for "loneliness"
        if len(remaining) > 0:
            d_out = distance_matrix[cand, np.asarray(remaining, dtype=int)]
            min_out = np.min(d_out)
            d_in = distance_matrix[current_node, cand]
            # If min_out is much larger than d_in, it's a spike
            spike_penalty = max(0, min_out - d_in) * 0.1
            tail_cost += spike_penalty

        if tail_cost < best_score:
            best_score = tail_cost
            best_node_id = cand

    if best_node_id == -1:
        best_node_id = int(unvisited_ids[int(np.argmin(cur_d))])

    return int(best_node_id)
```
