## 24 vrptw_construct_r1_e172

算子 Refine；节点 164；父节点 152；fitness -22.9721190284228；局部纠错：未计入。

增益：补充 travel+return、continuation 归一化、urgency 与低容量奖励的具体规则。

保留问题或边界：仍把单项归一化说成整体尺度不变；实际同时加 raw travel/wait 与无量纲项。W_CONT 紧窗基础 0.55 后还可加 0.15；服务时间猜测阈值从 2→3 未说明。

<details><summary>原始 Idea、校准 Idea、最终代码与父代码</summary>

### 前置 Idea

Improve the dynamic savings-continuity heuristic by (1) using an absolute-distance savings term that is always non-negative and comparable to waiting/continuation costs, (2) normalizing continuation cost to [0,1] so the lookahead weight is scale-invariant, (3) introducing a "remaining demand coverage" bonus that prefers customers whose demand fills the remaining capacity, reducing wasted capacity and promoting fuller routes, (4) smoothing urgency with sqrt-decay for better discrimination, and (5) tuning adaptive weights more aggressively in tight-window and low-capacity regimes.

### 校准 Idea

The implemented algorithm utilizes a multi-objective scoring mechanism to select the next customer, prioritizing route efficiency, time-window feasibility, and capacity utilization. The core decision rule minimizes a composite score composed of five weighted components: total travel distance, waiting time, normalized continuation cost, urgency, and capacity coverage.

First, the algorithm performs strict feasibility filtering. It rejects candidates exceeding remaining capacity or violating time windows. It also performs a "return-to-depot" check, ensuring that visiting a candidate and then returning to the depot would still arrive before the depot's closing time. This prevents routes that become infeasible after the next visit.

The scoring calculation relies on specific transformations to balance different cost scales. The "savings" term is redefined as `travel + return_dist`, representing the total distance incurred by visiting a node and returning to the depot. By minimizing this, the heuristic inherently prefers nodes that are geographically closer to the current position or closer to the depot, reducing overall route length. The "continuation cost" evaluates the minimum cost to reach another feasible customer from the current candidate. Crucially, this cost is min-max normalized to the range [0, 1]. This normalization makes the heuristic scale-invariant; the weight assigned to continuation (`W_CONT`) remains meaningful regardless of the absolute magnitude of distances in the problem. The "urgency" metric uses a square-root decay function on slack time (`1 / (1 + sqrt(slack))`). Unlike linear or log-based decay, this provides smoother discrimination for tight windows, allowing the heuristic to gently prioritize urgent nodes without excessive sensitivity to small slack variations.

The algorithm adapts its weights based on contextual variables. `W_CONT` is increased significantly (up to 0.55) when slack ratios are low (< 0.25), reflecting the high value of maintaining route connectivity when time buffers are thin. Similarly, `W_CONT` increases with the number of candidates (`n > 10`), promoting longer chains of customers to avoid route fragmentation. The "capacity coverage" bonus (`W_CAP`) is activated only in low-capacity regimes (when remaining capacity is less than 2-3 times the minimum demand of feasible nodes). In these scenarios, it boosts the score of customers with high demand relative to remaining capacity, thereby filling the vehicle more completely. This reduces the number of partially filled vehicles and improves fleet utilization. The base weight for capacity is zero, ensuring that this term does not interfere with standard distance optimization when capacity is not a bottleneck.

Important implementation changes relative to the parent code include: (1) Redefining the savings term from `travel - return_dist` to `travel + return_dist` to ensure non-negative, additive cost components. (2) Normalizing the continuation cost to [0, 1] to allow stable weight tuning across different distance scales. (3) Introducing the capacity coverage bonus, which was absent in the parent, specifically targeting low-capacity scenarios to maximize load factor. (4) Modifying the urgency function from logarithmic to square-root decay for improved discrimination in tight time windows. (5) Refining adaptive weight logic to more aggressively increase continuity weights in tight-window regimes and to activate capacity weights based on demand-to-capacity ratios rather than absolute capacity values.

### 最终代码

```python
import numpy as np

def _infer_service_time(time_windows: np.ndarray) -> float:
    """Infer a global service time from the median time-window span.
    If the median span is small (<= 3.0), treat it as a service time; otherwise 0.0."""
    if time_windows is None or len(time_windows) < 2:
        return 0.0
    spans = []
    for i in range(len(time_windows)):
        if len(time_windows[i]) >= 2:
            sp = float(time_windows[i][1]) - float(time_windows[i][0])
            if sp > 0:
                spans.append(sp)
    if not spans:
        return 0.0
    med_span = float(np.median(spans))
    if med_span <= 3.0:
        return med_span
    return 0.0

def select_next_node(current_node: int, depot: int, unvisited_nodes: np.ndarray, rest_capacity: float,
                     current_time: float, demands: np.ndarray, distance_matrix: np.ndarray,
                     time_windows: np.ndarray) -> int:
    if unvisited_nodes is None or len(unvisited_nodes) == 0:
        return depot

    candidates = np.asarray(unvisited_nodes, dtype=int).ravel()
    if candidates.size == 0:
        return depot

    dmat = np.asarray(distance_matrix, dtype=float)
    tw = np.asarray(time_windows, dtype=float)
    svc = _infer_service_time(tw)

    # Capacity filter
    if demands is not None and rest_capacity is not None:
        cap_ok = demands[candidates].astype(float) <= rest_capacity + 1e-9
        candidates = candidates[cap_ok]
        if candidates.size == 0:
            return depot

    n = int(candidates.size)
    if n == 0:
        return depot
    if n == 1:
        return int(candidates[0])

    travel = dmat[current_node, candidates]
    arrival = current_time + travel
    tw_start = tw[candidates, 0]
    tw_end = tw[candidates, 1]

    service_start = np.maximum(arrival, tw_start)
    slack = tw_end - service_start
    feasible_tw = slack >= -1e-9

    if not np.all(feasible_tw):
        candidates = candidates[feasible_tw]
        travel = travel[feasible_tw]
        slack = slack[feasible_tw]
        service_start = service_start[feasible_tw]
        tw_start = tw_start[feasible_tw]
        tw_end = tw_end[feasible_tw]
        n = int(candidates.size)
        if n == 0:
            return depot
        if n == 1:
            return int(candidates[0])

    depart_times = service_start + svc
    waiting = np.maximum(0.0, service_start - arrival)

    # Depot return feasibility
    return_dist = dmat[candidates, depot]
    depot_arrival = depart_times + return_dist
    depot_tw_end = float('inf')
    if depot < len(tw) and len(tw[depot]) >= 2:
        depot_tw_end = float(tw[depot][1])

    can_return = depot_arrival <= depot_tw_end + 1e-9
    if not np.all(can_return):
        candidates = candidates[can_return]
        travel = travel[can_return]
        slack = slack[can_return]
        service_start = service_start[can_return]
        depart_times = depart_times[can_return]
        return_dist = return_dist[can_return]
        tw_start = tw_start[can_return]
        tw_end = tw_end[can_return]
        waiting = waiting[can_return]
        n = int(candidates.size)
        if n == 0:
            return depot
        if n == 1:
            return int(candidates[0])

    # Absolute savings: travel + return - direct (always >= 0)
    savings = travel + return_dist

    # Two-step continuation cost
    cont_cost = np.full(n, fill_value=return_dist, dtype=float)
    if n > 1:
        d_cc = dmat[candidates[:, None], candidates[None, :]]
        arrival_jk = depart_times[:, None] + d_cc
        wait_jk = np.maximum(0.0, tw_start[None, :] - arrival_jk)
        cost_jk = d_cc + wait_jk
        idx = np.arange(n)
        cost_jk[idx, idx] = float('inf')
        feasible_jk = arrival_jk <= tw_end[None, :] + 1e-9
        cost_jk = np.where(feasible_jk, cost_jk, float('inf'))
        min_next = np.min(cost_jk, axis=1)
        has_successor = ~np.isinf(min_next)
        cont_cost = np.where(has_successor, min_next, return_dist)

    # Normalize continuation cost to [0, 1]
    cmin = cont_cost.min() if n > 0 else 0.0
    cmax = cont_cost.max() if n > 0 else 0.0
    cont_norm = (cont_cost - cmin) / (cmax - cmin + 1e-9) if cmax > cmin else np.zeros(n)

    # Urgency: sqrt-decay for smoother discrimination
    urgency_raw = 1.0 / (1.0 + np.sqrt(np.maximum(0.0, slack)))
    max_urg = np.max(urgency_raw) if n > 0 else 1.0
    urgency = urgency_raw / max_urg if max_urg > 0 else np.zeros(n)

    # Capacity coverage bonus: fraction of remaining capacity used
    cap_ratio = np.zeros(n)
    if demands is not None and rest_capacity is not None and rest_capacity > 1e-9:
        cap_ratio = demands[candidates].astype(float) / rest_capacity
        cap_ratio = np.clip(cap_ratio, 0.0, 1.0)

    # Adaptive weights
    avg_slack = float(np.mean(slack)) if n > 0 else 1.0
    max_slack = float(np.max(slack)) if n > 0 else 1.0
    slack_ratio = avg_slack / (max_slack + 1e-6)

    W_SAVE = 1.0
    W_WAIT = 0.25
    W_CONT = 0.35
    W_URG = 0.15
    W_CAP = 0.0  # base capacity weight

    # Tight windows: increase continuation and urgency
    if slack_ratio < 0.25:
        W_CONT = 0.55
        W_URG = 0.25
    elif slack_ratio < 0.45:
        W_CONT = 0.45
        W_URG = 0.20

    # Many candidates: stronger continuity to avoid fragmentation
    if n > 20:
        W_CONT += 0.15
    elif n > 10:
        W_CONT += 0.08

    # Low capacity: boost capacity coverage
    if demands is not None and rest_capacity is not None and rest_capacity > 1e-9:
        min_dem = float(np.min(demands[candidates])) if n > 0 else 0.0
        if rest_capacity < 2.0 * min_dem + 1e-9:
            W_CAP = 0.20
        elif rest_capacity < 3.0 * min_dem + 1e-9:
            W_CAP = 0.10

    # Score: minimize savings + waiting + continuation - urgency - capacity bonus
    scores = (
        W_SAVE * savings +
        W_WAIT * waiting +
        W_CONT * cont_norm -
        W_URG * urgency -
        W_CAP * cap_ratio
    )

    best_idx = int(np.argmin(scores))
    return int(candidates[best_idx])
```

### 父代码

```python
import numpy as np

def _infer_service_time(time_windows: np.ndarray) -> float:
    """
    Infer service time from time windows.
    If the median time window span is very small (<= 2.0), it might represent service time.
    Otherwise, assume 0.0 for safety as standard VRPTW often has service time = 0
    or separate parameter not provided here.
    """
    if time_windows is None or len(time_windows) < 2:
        return 0.0

    spans = []
    for i in range(len(time_windows)):
        if len(time_windows[i]) >= 2:
            sp = float(time_windows[i][1]) - float(time_windows[i][0])
            if sp > 0:
                spans.append(sp)

    if not spans:
        return 0.0

    med_span = float(np.median(spans))

    # Threshold adjusted to be more conservative about assuming service time
    if med_span <= 2.0:
        return med_span

    return 0.0

def select_next_node(current_node: int, depot: int, unvisited_nodes: np.ndarray, rest_capacity: float, current_time: float, demands: np.ndarray, distance_matrix: np.ndarray, time_windows: np.ndarray) -> int:
    """
    Select the next node using a dynamic savings and connectivity heuristic.
    """
    if unvisited_nodes is None or len(unvisited_nodes) == 0:
        return depot

    candidates = np.asarray(unvisited_nodes, dtype=int).ravel()
    if candidates.size == 0:
        return depot

    dmat = np.asarray(distance_matrix, dtype=float)
    tw = np.asarray(time_windows, dtype=float)

    service_time = _infer_service_time(tw)

    # 1. Hard Filter: Capacity
    if demands is not None and rest_capacity is not None:
        cap_ok = demands[candidates].astype(float) <= rest_capacity + 1e-9
        candidates = candidates[cap_ok]
        if candidates.size == 0:
            return depot

    # 2. Hard Filter: Time Window Feasibility
    n = int(candidates.size)
    if n == 0:
        return depot
    if n == 1:
        return int(candidates[0])

    travel = dmat[current_node, candidates]
    arrival = current_time + travel
    tw_start = tw[candidates, 0]
    tw_end = tw[candidates, 1]

    service_start = np.maximum(arrival, tw_start)
    slack = tw_end - service_start
    feasible_tw = slack >= -1e-9

    if not np.all(feasible_tw):
        candidates = candidates[feasible_tw]
        travel = travel[feasible_tw]
        slack = slack[feasible_tw]
        service_start = service_start[feasible_tw]
        tw_start = tw_start[feasible_tw]
        tw_end = tw_end[feasible_tw]
        n = int(candidates.size)
        if n == 0:
            return depot
        if n == 1:
            return int(candidates[0])

    depart_times = service_start + service_time
    waiting = np.maximum(0.0, service_start - arrival)

    # 3. Hard Filter: Return to Depot Feasibility
    return_dist = dmat[candidates, depot]
    depot_arrival = depart_times + return_dist

    # Get depot time window if available
    depot_tw_end = float('inf')
    if depot < len(tw) and len(tw[depot]) >= 2:
        depot_tw_end = float(tw[depot][1])

    can_return = depot_arrival <= depot_tw_end + 1e-9
    if not np.all(can_return):
        candidates = candidates[can_return]
        travel = travel[can_return]
        slack = slack[can_return]
        service_start = service_start[can_return]
        depart_times = depart_times[can_return]
        return_dist = return_dist[can_return]
        tw_start = tw_start[can_return]
        tw_end = tw_end[can_return]
        waiting = waiting[can_return]
        n = int(candidates.size)
        if n == 0:
            return depot
        if n == 1:
            return int(candidates[0])

    # 4. Scoring Components
    # Savings: d(cur, j) - d(j, depot)
    savings = travel - return_dist

    # Connectivity / Continuation Cost
    # For each candidate j, find the min cost to go to another candidate k (feasible)
    # or to depot.
    # We prefer candidates that have a feasible next customer.

    cont_cost = np.full(n, fill_value=return_dist, dtype=float)

    if n > 1:
        # Distance from all candidates to all other candidates
        d_cc = dmat[candidates[:, None], candidates[None, :]]

        # Feasibility of moving from j to k
        # Depart j at depart_times[j], travel d_cc[j,k], arrive at depart_times[j] + d_cc[j,k]
        # Must be <= tw_end[k]

        arrival_jk = depart_times[:, None] + d_cc

        # Waiting at k
        wait_jk = np.maximum(0.0, tw_start[None, :] - arrival_jk)

        # Cost to move from j to k
        cost_jk = d_cc + wait_jk

        # Mask diagonal (j cannot go to itself)
        idx = np.arange(n)
        cost_jk[idx, idx] = float('inf')

        # Check if arrival is feasible
        feasible_jk = arrival_jk <= tw_end[None, :] + 1e-9
        cost_jk = np.where(feasible_jk, cost_jk, float('inf'))

        # For each j, the best next step cost is min(cost_jk[j, :])
        # If no feasible k exists, cost is inf. We handle inf later.
        min_next_cost = np.min(cost_jk, axis=1)

        # If min_next_cost is inf, it means j is a dead-end (must go to depot).
        # The cost of being a dead-end is the return_dist.
        # However, we want to penalize dead-ends more heavily or differentiate them.
        # Here we just use return_dist as the cost, but we track dead_ends separately.

        has_successor = ~np.isinf(min_next_cost)

        # For nodes with successors, the continuation cost is min_next_cost.
        # For nodes without, it is return_dist.
        cont_cost = np.where(has_successor, min_next_cost, return_dist)

        # Additional penalty for dead-ends?
        # The previous versions added penalties. Let's rely on the savings term
        # and a slight boost to continuation weight for dead ends.

    # 5. Urgency
    # Higher urgency for tighter windows
    urgency_raw = 1.0 / (1.0 + np.log1p(np.maximum(0, slack)))
    max_urgency = np.max(urgency_raw)
    if max_urgency > 0:
        urgency = urgency_raw / max_urgency
    else:
        urgency = np.zeros(n)

    # 6. Adaptive Weights
    # Base weights
    W_SAVE = 1.0
    W_WAIT = 0.2
    W_CONT = 0.3
    W_URG = 0.15

    # Adjust W_CONT based on candidate density
    # If many candidates, we should prioritize connectivity to keep options open.
    # If few candidates, immediate savings are more critical.
    if n > 10:
        W_CONT = 0.4
    elif n > 20:
        W_CONT = 0.5

    # Adjust W_URG if slack is tight
    avg_slack = np.mean(slack)
    max_slack = np.max(slack) if n > 0 else 1.0
    slack_ratio = avg_slack / (max_slack + 1e-6)

    if slack_ratio < 0.3:
        W_URG = 0.25

    # Calculate Score
    # Score = W_SAVE * savings + W_WAIT * waiting + W_CONT * cont_cost - W_URG * urgency * (travel + 1)

    scores = (
        W_SAVE * savings +
        W_WAIT * waiting +
        W_CONT * cont_cost -
        W_URG * urgency * (travel + 1.0)
    )

    best_idx = int(np.argmin(scores))
    return int(candidates[best_idx])
```

</details>

## 25 vrptw_construct_r1_e202

算子 Refine；节点 192；父节点 182；fitness -19.44103886398589；局部纠错：未计入。

增益：二次版把最终的 continuation 与 urgency 权重条件写了出来。

保留问题或边界：新增严重父代比较错误：声称 implementation identical。实际父代 slack_norm>0.8 调小 W_CONT、<0.2 设置 W_URG=.2；子代 <.5 时 W_CONT*=1.3、<.3 时 W_URG*=1.5。

<details><summary>原始 Idea、校准 Idea、最终代码与父代码</summary>

### 前置 Idea

The heuristic selects the next customer by minimizing a composite score that blends immediate travel savings with a two-step lookahead for route continuity, while adapting weights based on time-window tightness. First, candidates are hard-filtered for capacity, time-window feasibility, and depot-return feasibility. For each feasible candidate, the algorithm computes: (1) a savings metric ($d_{cur,j} - d_{j,depot}$) to favor route extension, (2) a waiting time penalty, (3) an urgency metric ($1/(1+\text{slack})$) to prioritize tight windows, and (4) a continuation cost estimating the cheapest feasible next step after serving candidate $j$. A key improvement is the addition of a distance-normalized urgency term and a refined continuation cost that explicitly accounts for waiting times in the lookahead, while using adaptive weights that increase continuation importance when slack is low and urgency importance when windows are critically tight. The service time is inferred from the median time-window span to ensure realistic feasibility checks.

### 校准 Idea

The implemented heuristic selects the next node by minimizing a composite cost function that balances immediate travel efficiency, route extension potential, and future feasibility constraints. First, the algorithm performs strict feasibility filtering on the input candidate set. It removes nodes that violate vehicle capacity limits or exceed customer time windows (ensuring the arrival time plus any waiting does not exceed the window end). Crucially, it also applies a "look-ahead" filter to eliminate nodes that cannot be served in time to return to the depot before its closing time, thereby preventing infeasible routes. For the remaining feasible candidates, the algorithm computes several metrics: a savings metric defined as the difference between the distance from the current node to the candidate and the distance from the candidate to the depot (favoring nodes that extend the route cheaply), a waiting time penalty, and an urgency metric derived from the remaining time slack within the customer's window ($1/(1+\text{slack})$), normalized to the maximum urgency in the set.

The core decision rule relies on a two-step continuation cost calculation. For each candidate $i$, the algorithm estimates the minimum cost to proceed to any other feasible candidate $j$ (or the depot) after completing service at $i$. This lookahead accounts for travel distance and a weighted penalty for waiting time at the subsequent node, ensuring that the chosen node leaves the vehicle in a position to efficiently serve remaining customers. The final score is a weighted sum of these components: $Score = W_{sav} \cdot Savings + W_{wait} \cdot Waiting + W_{cont} \cdot ContCost - W_{urg} \cdot Urgency \cdot (Travel + 1)$. The urgency term is subtracted to prioritize tight-window customers, with its influence scaled by the travel distance to maintain magnitude balance.

The algorithm adapts its weights based on the average slack of the feasible candidates. If the average slack is low (indicating tight time windows), the weight for the continuation cost ($W_{cont}$) is increased by a factor of 1.3 to emphasize route continuity and feasibility preservation. If the slack is extremely low, the weight for urgency ($W_{urg}$) is further increased by a factor of 1.5 to aggressively prioritize customers with imminent deadlines. Service time is inferred heuristically from the median time-window span of the first few nodes, assuming a small service time relative to tight windows. This adaptive weighting allows the heuristic to shift focus from simple savings maximization in loose scenarios to careful feasibility management and urgency handling in tight scenarios. By integrating a two-step lookahead with adaptive weighting, the method aims to reduce the number of vehicles needed and total travel distance while strictly respecting time-window constraints, effectively balancing local optimality with global route feasibility.

The implementation is identical to the provided parent code. There are no changes relative to the parent code; the final code preserves all logic, including the specific adaptive weight adjustments ($W_{cont} \times 1.3$ if $slack\_norm < 0.5$, $W_{urg} \times 1.5$ if $slack\_norm < 0.3$), the service time inference, and the two-step continuation cost calculation.

### 最终代码

```python
import numpy as np

def select_next_node(current_node: int, depot: int, unvisited_nodes: np.ndarray, rest_capacity: float, current_time: float, demands: np.ndarray, distance_matrix: np.ndarray, time_windows: np.ndarray) -> int:
    """
    Select the next node using a savings-lookahead heuristic with adaptive weights and refined continuation cost.

    Args:
        current_node: ID of the current node.
        depot: ID of the depot.
        unvisited_nodes: Array of feasible unvisited node IDs under capacity and time-window constraints.
        rest_capacity: Remaining vehicle capacity before selecting the next node.
        current_time: Current time on the active route.
        demands: Demand of each node.
        distance_matrix: Pairwise distance matrix.
        time_windows: Time window of each node.
    Return:
        ID of the next node to visit, or depot to start a new route.
    """
    if unvisited_nodes is None or len(unvisited_nodes) == 0:
        return depot

    if rest_capacity <= 0:
        return depot

    candidates = np.asarray(unvisited_nodes, dtype=int).ravel()
    if candidates.size == 0:
        return depot

    dmat = np.asarray(distance_matrix, dtype=float)
    tw = np.asarray(time_windows, dtype=float)

    if demands is not None:
        cap_ok = demands[candidates].astype(float) <= rest_capacity + 1e-9
        candidates = candidates[cap_ok]
        if candidates.size == 0:
            return depot

    # Infer service time from median time-window span
    service_time = 0.0
    if tw is not None and len(tw) > 0:
        spans = []
        for i in range(min(5, len(tw))):
            if tw[i].size >= 2:
                sp = float(tw[i][1]) - float(tw[i][0])
                if sp > 0:
                    spans.append(sp)
        if spans:
            med_span = np.median(spans)
            if med_span <= 10.0:
                service_time = min(med_span * 0.1, 1.0)

    n = int(candidates.size)
    if n == 0:
        return depot

    travel = dmat[current_node, candidates]
    arrival = current_time + travel

    tw_start = tw[candidates, 0]
    tw_end = tw[candidates, 1]

    service_start = np.maximum(arrival, tw_start)
    waiting = service_start - arrival
    slack = tw_end - service_start

    feasible_tw = slack >= -1e-9
    if not np.all(feasible_tw):
        candidates = candidates[feasible_tw]
        travel = travel[feasible_tw]
        waiting = waiting[feasible_tw]
        slack = slack[feasible_tw]
        service_start = service_start[feasible_tw]
        n = int(candidates.size)
        if n == 0:
            return depot
        if n == 1:
            return int(candidates[0])

    # Check depot return feasibility
    return_dist = dmat[candidates, depot]
    depot_arrival = service_start + service_time + return_dist

    depot_tw_end = float('inf')
    if depot < len(tw) and tw[depot].size >= 2:
        depot_tw_end = float(tw[depot][1])

    can_return = depot_arrival <= depot_tw_end + 1e-9

    if not np.all(can_return):
        candidates = candidates[can_return]
        travel = travel[can_return]
        waiting = waiting[can_return]
        slack = slack[can_return]
        service_start = service_start[can_return]
        return_dist = return_dist[can_return]
        n = int(candidates.size)
        if n == 0:
            return depot
        if n == 1:
            return int(candidates[0])

    if n == 1:
        return int(candidates[0])

    # Savings metric
    savings = travel - return_dist

    # Urgency: normalized 1/(1+slack)
    urgency_raw = 1.0 / (1.0 + np.maximum(0, slack))
    max_urgency = np.max(urgency_raw)
    if max_urgency > 0:
        urgency = urgency_raw / max_urgency
    else:
        urgency = np.zeros(n)

    # Two-step continuation cost with waiting in lookahead
    cont_costs = np.zeros(n)
    for i in range(n):
        ci = int(candidates[i])
        depart_i = service_start[i] + service_time
        min_cost = float(return_dist[i])  # default: go to depot

        for j in range(n):
            if i == j:
                continue
            cj = int(candidates[j])
            dist_ij = dmat[ci, cj]
            arr_j = depart_i + dist_ij
            if arr_j <= tw[cj, 1] + 1e-9:
                wait_j = max(0.0, tw[cj, 0] - arr_j)
                cost_ij = dist_ij + 0.5 * wait_j
                if cost_ij < min_cost:
                    min_cost = cost_ij
        cont_costs[i] = min_cost

    # Adaptive weights based on average slack
    avg_slack = np.mean(slack)
    max_slack_observed = np.max(slack)
    if max_slack_observed > 0:
        slack_norm = avg_slack / max_slack_observed
    else:
        slack_norm = 1.0

    W_SAVINGS = 1.0
    W_WAIT = 0.2
    W_CONT = 0.3
    W_URG = 0.1

    # Adapt continuation weight: higher when slack is tight
    if slack_norm < 0.5:
        W_CONT *= 1.3
    # Adapt urgency weight: higher when slack is very tight
    if slack_norm < 0.3:
        W_URG *= 1.5

    # Score: minimize savings + wait + cont - urgency
    # Urgency is subtracted to reward tight nodes (lower score for higher urgency)
    score = (
        W_SAVINGS * savings +
        W_WAIT * waiting +
        W_CONT * cont_costs -
        W_URG * urgency * (travel + 1.0)
    )

    best_idx = int(np.argmin(score))
    return int(candidates[best_idx])
```

import numpy as np

def select_next_node(current_node: int, depot: int, unvisited_nodes: np.ndarray, rest_capacity: float, current_time: float, demands: np.ndarray, distance_matrix: np.ndarray, time_windows: np.ndarray) -> int:
    """
    Select the next node using a combined savings-lookahead heuristic with adaptive weighting.

    Args:
        current_node: ID of the current node.
        depot: ID of the depot.
        unvisited_nodes: Array of feasible unvisited node IDs under capacity and time-window constraints.
        rest_capacity: Remaining vehicle capacity before selecting the next node.
        current_time: Current time on the active route.
        demands: Demand of each node.
        distance_matrix: Pairwise distance matrix.
        time_windows: Time window of each node.
    Return:
        ID of the next node to visit, or depot to start a new route.
    """
    if unvisited_nodes is None or len(unvisited_nodes) == 0:
        return depot

    if rest_capacity <= 0:
        return depot

    candidates = np.asarray(unvisited_nodes, dtype=int).ravel()
    if candidates.size == 0:
        return depot

    dmat = np.asarray(distance_matrix, dtype=float)
    tw = np.asarray(time_windows, dtype=float)

    # 1. Filter by capacity
    if demands is not None:
        cap_ok = demands[candidates].astype(float) <= rest_capacity + 1e-9
        candidates = candidates[cap_ok]
        if candidates.size == 0:
            return depot

    # 2. Infer Service Time
    # Assume service time is small relative to window span if spans are small
    service_time = 0.0
    if tw is not None and len(tw) > 0:
        spans = []
        # Sample a few nodes to estimate typical span
        for i in range(min(5, len(tw))):
            if tw[i].size >= 2:
                sp = float(tw[i][1]) - float(tw[i][0])
                if sp > 0:
                    spans.append(sp)
        if spans:
            med_span = np.median(spans)
            # Heuristic: if windows are tight (e.g., < 10 units), assume service time is significant
            # Often in benchmarks, service time is a fixed small number like 1.0 or derived.
            # If we don't know it, 0 is safer for feasibility checks unless it's very small.
            # Let's assume 0 service time for lookahead simplicity unless explicitly tight.
            if med_span <= 10.0:
                service_time = min(med_span * 0.1, 1.0) # Conservative estimate

    n = int(candidates.size)
    if n == 0:
        return depot

    # 3. Calculate Arrival, Waiting, Slack
    travel = dmat[current_node, candidates]
    arrival = current_time + travel

    tw_start = tw[candidates, 0]
    tw_end = tw[candidates, 1]

    service_start = np.maximum(arrival, tw_start)
    waiting = service_start - arrival
    slack = tw_end - service_start

    # 4. Filter by Time Window Feasibility
    feasible_tw = slack >= -1e-9
    if not np.all(feasible_tw):
        candidates = candidates[feasible_tw]
        travel = travel[feasible_tw]
        waiting = waiting[feasible_tw]
        slack = slack[feasible_tw]
        service_start = service_start[feasible_tw]
        n = int(candidates.size)
        if n == 0:
            return depot
        if n == 1:
            return int(candidates[0])

    # 5. Filter by Depot Return Feasibility
    return_dist = dmat[candidates, depot]
    depot_arrival = service_start + service_time + return_dist

    # Get depot closing time
    depot_tw_end = float('inf')
    if depot < len(tw) and tw[depot].size >= 2:
        depot_tw_end = float(tw[depot][1])

    can_return = depot_arrival <= depot_tw_end + 1e-9

    if not np.all(can_return):
        candidates = candidates[can_return]
        travel = travel[can_return]
        waiting = waiting[can_return]
        slack = slack[can_return]
        service_start = service_start[can_return]
        return_dist = return_dist[can_return]
        n = int(candidates.size)
        if n == 0:
            return depot
        if n == 1:
            return int(candidates[0])

    if n == 1:
        return int(candidates[0])

    # 6. Calculate Savings
    # Savings = travel_to_candidate - travel_candidate_to_depot
    # Minimizing this favors nodes that are cheap to add relative to closing the route
    savings = travel - return_dist

    # 7. Calculate Urgency
    # Higher urgency for tighter windows (lower slack)
    # Using 1/(1+slack) to keep it bounded
    urgency_raw = 1.0 / (1.0 + np.maximum(0, slack))

    # Normalize urgency to [0,1] relative to max urgency in set to avoid scale issues
    max_urgency = np.max(urgency_raw)
    if max_urgency > 0:
        urgency = urgency_raw / max_urgency
    else:
        urgency = np.zeros(n)

    # 8. Calculate Lookahead Cost (Continuation Cost)
    # For each candidate i, find the min cost to go to any other candidate j (or depot)
    # such that j is reachable within its time window from i.
    cont_costs = np.zeros(n)

    # Precompute distances between candidates
    # This is O(N^2) which is acceptable for typical VRP instances (N < 100 usually in this context)
    for i in range(n):
        ci = int(candidates[i])
        min_cost = float('inf')

        # Option 1: Go back to depot
        # We already know depot is feasible, cost is return_dist[i]
        # Note: return_dist is distance, not time. But for cost minimization, we usually minimize distance.
        # However, the "cost" of going back is just the distance.
        min_cost = min(min_cost, float(return_dist[i]))

        # Option 2: Go to another candidate j
        # We need to check if we can reach j from i in time.
        # Time at i = service_start[i] + service_time
        # Arrival at j = Time at i + dist(i, j)
        # Feasible if Arrival at j <= tw_end[j]

        # Vectorize inner loop for speed if possible, but N is small so loop is fine.
        # Let's try a vectorized approach for the whole matrix if N is not too large,
        # but the loop is safer for handling the 'self' exclusion and specific logic.

        depart_i = service_start[i] + service_time

        for j in range(n):
            if i == j:
                continue
            cj = int(candidates[j])

            # Distance from i to j
            dist_ij = dmat[ci, cj]

            # Arrival at j
            arr_j = depart_i + dist_ij

            # Check if j is still feasible
            if arr_j <= tw[cj, 1] + 1e-9:
                # Calculate waiting at j
                wait_j = max(0.0, tw[cj, 0] - arr_j)

                # Cost of going i -> j -> depot? Or just i -> j?
                # The heuristic usually considers the "extension" cost.
                # If we pick i, the next step will be evaluated similarly.
                # A common lookahead metric is min( dist(i, k) ) for feasible k.
                # This encourages connecting i to a cluster.
                # Let's use distance as the primary cost metric here, penalized slightly by waiting.

                cost_ij = dist_ij + 0.5 * wait_j

                if cost_ij < min_cost:
                    min_cost = cost_ij

        cont_costs[i] = min_cost

    # 9. Adaptive Weights
    # If slack is low (tight windows), we care more about urgency and continuity.
    avg_slack = np.mean(slack)
    max_slack_observed = np.max(slack)

    if max_slack_observed > 0:
        slack_norm = avg_slack / max_slack_observed
    else:
        slack_norm = 1.0 # Loose windows

    # Base weights
    W_SAVINGS = 1.0
    W_WAIT = 0.2
    W_CONT = 0.3
    W_URG = 0.1

    # Adjust W_CONT: if tight, look further ahead? Or if tight, stick to nearest?
    # If tight, continuity is less about distance and more about feasibility, which is handled by filter.
    # If loose, we can afford to be greedy on savings.
    # Let's keep W_CONT stable but slightly reduce it if windows are very loose to focus on savings.
    if slack_norm > 0.8:
        W_CONT *= 0.8
    elif slack_norm < 0.2:
        W_URG = 0.2 # Increase urgency weight

    # 10. Final Score
    # Minimize score
    # Savings: lower is better (more savings)
    # Waiting: lower is better
    # Cont Cost: lower is better (cheaper next step)
    # Urgency: higher is better (tighter windows), so subtract it.

    # Normalize savings? No, raw distance is usually comparable.
    # Ensure components are comparable.

    score = (
        W_SAVINGS * savings +
        W_WAIT * waiting +
        W_CONT * cont_costs -
        W_URG * urgency * (travel + 1.0) # Scale urgency by travel to balance magnitude
    )

    best_idx = int(np.argmin(score))
    return int(candidates[best_idx])
