### 前置 Idea

Blend the reference algorithm's geometric Clarke‑Wright "effective distance" (which rewards moves that shrink the depot round‑trip, i.e., compact routes) with the current algorithm's adaptive capacity logic and urgency signal. The score is a small weighted sum of (1) effective travel cost combining forward distance with a savings‑based consolidation term, (2) normalized waiting time, (3) a quadratic time‑window tightness penalty that grows as slack drops below 50% of the window width (preserving flexible nodes for later while still preferring feasible ones), (4) a phase‑dependent capacity term that gently prefers high‑demand nodes when capacity is abundant (p>0.5) to fill the truck, and prefers low‑demand nodes when capacity is scarce to ensure the route can close, and (5) a normalized clustering lookahead (mean distance to other unvisited nodes) to keep routes spatially coherent. All components are min‑normalized within the candidate set so they are comparable; tiny deterministic tie‑breakers are added on arrival and demand. This keeps the strong distance/consolidation signal from the reference while retaining the smarter capacity and urgency handling from the current version.

### 校准 Idea

The algorithm selects the next node by minimizing a composite score derived from five normalized components, effectively creating a weighted sum of proximity, time efficiency, urgency, capacity management, and spatial coherence. First, the heuristic filters candidates to ensure strict time-window feasibility (arrival $\le$ end time) and valid capacity constraints. The primary decision driver is the Clarke-Wright "effective distance," calculated as $d_{eff} = d_{current, next} - 0.5 \times (d_{current, depot} + d_{next, depot} - d_{current, next})$. This term penalizes moves that do not save travel relative to serving customers individually, thereby encouraging the construction of compact, consolidated routes. To prevent the heuristic from ignoring temporal constraints, a quadratic urgency penalty is applied only when a customer's remaining slack is less than 50% of their time-window width. This non-linear penalty grows sharply as the window tightens, forcing the algorithm to prioritize critical customers without overly distorting the solution for flexible ones. An adaptive capacity term adjusts based on the ratio of remaining vehicle capacity to the total demand of unvisited customers. When this ratio is high (early in the route), the algorithm favors high-demand customers to maximize load efficiency; when low (late in the route), it favors low-demand customers to ensure the vehicle can return to the depot. Finally, a clustering lookahead term computes the mean distance from each candidate to all other unvisited nodes, penalizing moves that would isolate the current route from the remaining customer cluster. All components are min-max normalized within the current candidate set to ensure comparable scales, and tiny deterministic tie-breakers based on arrival time and demand are added to ensure consistent behavior in edge cases.

The implementation introduces several significant changes from the parent code to refine the heuristic's balance and robustness. The most substantial change is the incorporation of the Clarke-Wright effective distance as the primary distance metric, replacing the simple forward distance. This explicitly models the economic benefit of route consolidation, which the parent code addressed only indirectly through the clustering lookahead. Second, the urgency calculation has been shifted from a linear, mean-based normalization to a localized, quadratic penalty based on relative slack ($slack/width$). This new formulation isolates the impact of specific tight windows from the overall mean slack of the candidate set, providing a stronger and more precise signal when immediate time constraints are threatened. Third, the adaptive capacity term has been simplified and made more direct. While the parent code used a complex net weight logic, the final code clearly maps the capacity phase ($p$) to a preference for high or low demand, ensuring that the vehicle loading strategy is intuitive and stable. Finally, the code includes a robust pre-processing step that explicitly removes the current node and invalid indices from the candidate list before calculation, preventing potential indexing errors or self-loop selections that were not explicitly guarded against in the parent version. These changes collectively aim to improve route compactness and feasibility preservation without increasing computational complexity significantly.

### 最终代码

```python
import numpy as np

def select_next_node(current_node: int, depot: int, unvisited_nodes: np.ndarray, rest_capacity: float, current_time: float, demands: np.ndarray, distance_matrix: np.ndarray, time_windows: np.ndarray) -> int:
    """
    Constructive VRPTW heuristic: pick next customer (or depot) by minimizing a
    composite score that blends Clarke-Wright effective distance, waiting,
    time-window urgency, adaptive capacity, and clustering lookahead.
    """
    if unvisited_nodes is None or len(unvisited_nodes) == 0:
        return depot

    # Build clean candidate list (drop current/invalid)
    candidates = []
    for n in unvisited_nodes:
        ni = int(n)
        if ni == -1 or ni == current_node:
            continue
        candidates.append(ni)
    if not candidates:
        return depot
    if len(candidates) == 1:
        return candidates[0]

    cand = np.array(candidates, dtype=int)

    # Forward travel and arrival
    d = distance_matrix[current_node, cand].astype(float)
    arrival = current_time + d
    tw_s = time_windows[cand, 0].astype(float)
    tw_e = time_windows[cand, 1].astype(float)

    # Feasibility filter (arrival must be <= window end)
    feasible = arrival <= tw_e + 1e-9
    if not np.all(feasible):
        cand = cand[feasible]
        d = d[feasible]
        arrival = arrival[feasible]
        tw_s = tw_s[feasible]
        tw_e = tw_e[feasible]
        if len(cand) == 0:
            return depot
        if len(cand) == 1:
            return int(cand[0])

    # Waiting and depart
    wait = np.maximum(0.0, tw_s - arrival)
    depart = np.maximum(arrival, tw_s)
    slack = tw_e - depart  # remaining slack after starting service
    demand = demands[cand].astype(float)

    # Clarke-Wright effective distance (consolidation reward)
    d_cd = distance_matrix[current_node, depot].item()
    d_nd = distance_matrix[cand, depot].astype(float)
    savings = d_cd + d_nd - d
    eff_dist = d - 0.5 * savings

    # Time-window urgency: quadratic penalty for slack < 0.5 * width
    width = np.maximum(tw_e - tw_s, 1e-9)
    slack_ratio = slack / width
    urg = np.zeros_like(slack, dtype=float)
    tight = slack_ratio < 0.5
    if np.any(tight):
        r = np.clip(0.5 - slack_ratio[tight], 0.0, 0.5)
        urg[tight] = (2.0 * r) ** 1.5  # in [0,1], 0 when slack_ratio=0.5, 1 when slack_ratio=0

    # Adaptive capacity term: prefer big when capacity abundant, small when scarce
    total_rem = float(np.sum(demand))
    if total_rem > 1e-12 and rest_capacity > 1e-12:
        p = float(np.clip(rest_capacity / total_rem, 0.0, 1.0))
        load_ratio = np.clip(demand / max(rest_capacity, 1e-12), 0.0, 1.0)
        net_load = (2.0 * p - 1.0) * load_ratio  # positive when p>0.5 (prefer high), negative when p<0.5 (prefer low)
    else:
        net_load = np.zeros_like(demand, dtype=float)

    # Clustering lookahead: mean distance to other unvisited nodes
    U = np.asarray(unvisited_nodes, dtype=int)
    if len(U) > 1:
        cand_to_U = distance_matrix[cand][:, U].astype(float)
        u_idx = {int(u): i for i, u in enumerate(U)}
        self_pos = np.array([u_idx.get(int(c), -1) for c in cand])
        cand_to_U[np.arange(len(cand)), self_pos] = 0.0
        lookahead = np.sum(cand_to_U, axis=1) / max(len(U) - 1, 1)
    else:
        lookahead = np.zeros_like(d, dtype=float)

    # Normalize each component to [0,1] within candidate set
    def _min_norm(arr):
        if arr.size == 0:
            return np.zeros_like(arr, dtype=float)
        mn = float(np.min(arr)); mx = float(np.max(arr))
        if mx - mn < 1e-12:
            return np.zeros_like(arr, dtype=float)
        return (arr - mn) / (mx - mn)

    norm_eff = _min_norm(eff_dist)
    norm_wait = _min_norm(wait)
    norm_urg = _min_norm(urg)
    norm_la = _min_norm(lookahead)
    norm_load = _min_norm(net_load)  # note sign already in net_load

    # Weights: distance dominates; waiting and urgency matter; capacity and clustering moderate
    w_eff, w_wait, w_urg, w_load, w_la = 1.0, 0.4, 0.3, 0.2, 0.15

    score = (w_eff * norm_eff
             + w_wait * norm_wait
             + w_urg * norm_urg
             + w_load * norm_load
             + w_la * norm_la)

    # Tiny deterministic tie-breakers: earlier arrival, then smaller demand
    eps_a = 1e-6 * _min_norm(arrival)
    eps_d = 1e-7 * _min_norm(demand)
    score = score + eps_a + eps_d

    idx = int(np.argmin(score))
    return int(cand[idx])
```

### 父代码

```python
import numpy as np

def select_next_node(current_node: int, depot: int, unvisited_nodes: np.ndarray, rest_capacity: float, current_time: float, demands: np.ndarray, distance_matrix: np.ndarray, time_windows: np.ndarray) -> int:
    """
    Select next node using a normalized composite regret score combining:
      - distance (closer is better),
      - waiting time (less idle is better),
      - time-window urgency (tight windows visited early, to preserve feasibility),
      - adaptive capacity term (fill capacity when space is plentiful; conserve when scarce),
      - look-ahead clustering bonus (prefer nodes that keep nearby unvisited customers reachable).
    """
    if unvisited_nodes is None or len(unvisited_nodes) == 0:
        return depot

    candidates = np.asarray(unvisited_nodes, dtype=int)
    if len(candidates) == 1:
        return int(candidates[0])

    # Feasibility safety net (candidates are expected to already be feasible).
    d = distance_matrix[current_node, candidates]
    arrival = current_time + d
    tw_s = time_windows[candidates, 0]
    tw_e = time_windows[candidates, 1]
    feasible_mask = arrival <= tw_e + 1e-9

    if not np.all(feasible_mask):
        candidates = candidates[feasible_mask]
        d = d[feasible_mask]
        arrival = arrival[feasible_mask]
        tw_s = tw_s[feasible_mask]
        tw_e = tw_e[feasible_mask]
        if len(candidates) == 0:
            return depot
        if len(candidates) == 1:
            return int(candidates[0])

    wait = np.maximum(0.0, tw_s - arrival)
    depart = np.maximum(arrival, tw_s)
    slack = tw_e - depart
    demand = demands[candidates]

    # --- Normalization helpers (min/mean/max with safe fallbacks) ---
    def _min_norm(arr):
        if arr.size == 0:
            return np.zeros_like(arr, dtype=float)
        mn = float(np.min(arr))
        mx = float(np.max(arr))
        if mx - mn < 1e-12:
            return np.zeros_like(arr, dtype=float)
        return (arr - mn) / (mx - mn)

    def _mean_norm(arr):
        if arr.size == 0:
            return np.zeros_like(arr, dtype=float)
        m = float(np.mean(arr))
        if m < 1e-12:
            return np.zeros_like(arr, dtype=float)
        return arr / m

    norm_d = _min_norm(d)
    norm_w = _min_norm(wait)

    # Urgency: 1 when slack is tight (small relative to mean slack).
    # Using mean slack avoids a single huge-slack customer from flattening urgency.
    mean_slack = float(np.mean(slack))
    if mean_slack < 1e-9:
        norm_u = np.zeros_like(slack, dtype=float)
    else:
        norm_u = np.clip(1.0 - slack / mean_slack, 0.0, 1.0)

    # Adaptive capacity term.
    if rest_capacity > 1e-12 and demand.size > 0:
        load_ratio = np.minimum(demand / rest_capacity, 1.0)
        # Phase indicator: p≈1 early in route (plenty of space -> fill),
        # p≈0 late (scarce -> conserve).
        total_rem = float(np.sum(demand))
        if total_rem > 1e-12:
            p = float(np.clip(rest_capacity / total_rem, 0.0, 1.0))
        else:
            p = 0.5
        # Net weight on load: positive when filling, negative when conserving.
        net_load_w = 2.0 * p - 1.0
        norm_load = _min_norm(load_ratio)
    else:
        net_load_w = 0.0
        norm_load = np.zeros_like(d, dtype=float)

    # --- Look-ahead clustering bonus (cheap, O(|C|*|U|) with small constant) ---
    # For each candidate, compute the mean distance from that candidate to the
    # remaining unvisited customers (excluding itself). Lower is better: it
    # encourages spatially coherent routes.
    if len(unvisited_nodes) > 1:
        U = np.asarray(unvisited_nodes, dtype=int)
        # Build mask of "other" unvisited customers for each candidate.
        # Vectorize: for each candidate row, sum over other U.
        # We can compute distances from each candidate to all U in one call.
        cand_to_U = distance_matrix[candidates][:, U]  # shape (len(candidates), len(U))
        # Mask out self contributions (candidate appears in U at some index).
        # Identify self index per candidate.
        # Use a map from node id -> index in U.
        u_idx = {int(u): i for i, u in enumerate(U)}
        self_pos = np.array([u_idx.get(int(c), -1) for c in candidates])
        # Zero out diagonal-like entries.
        cand_to_U[np.arange(len(candidates)), self_pos] = 0.0
        # Mean over other unvisited (count = len(U)-1).
        other_count = max(len(U) - 1, 1)
        lookahead = np.sum(cand_to_U, axis=1) / other_count
        norm_la = _min_norm(lookahead)
    else:
        norm_la = np.zeros_like(d, dtype=float)

    # --- Weights ---
    w_d   = 1.0
    w_w   = 0.6
    w_u   = 0.5      # urgency bonus (subtractive: prefer tight windows)
    w_l   = 0.25 * net_load_w  # adaptive: can be positive or negative
    w_la  = 0.15

    score = (
        w_d * norm_d
      + w_w * norm_w
      - w_u * norm_u
      + w_l * norm_load
      + w_la * norm_la
    )

    score = np.nan_to_num(score, nan=1e12, posinf=1e12, neginf=-1e12)

    # Tie-break: prefer earlier arrival, then smaller demand (more flexibility).
    # Add tiny epsilon terms so argmin is deterministic.
    eps_arrival = 1e-6 * (arrival - np.min(arrival)) / (np.max(arrival) - np.min(arrival) + 1e-12)
    eps_demand  = 1e-7 * (demand - np.min(demand)) / (np.max(demand) - np.min(demand) + 1e-12)
    score = score + eps_arrival + eps_demand

    idx = int(np.argmin(score))
    return int(candidates[idx])
```

</details>

## 27 vrptw_construct_r2_e228

算子 Pivot；节点 221；父节点 167；fitness -38.23120213683204；局部纠错：未计入。

增益：补充 efficiency 的 alpha=.55、rank sum、capacity hard filter 与父代变化。

保留问题或边界：time_risk 的等待/松弛项会相互作用：arrival<start 时为 wait/(width+1)-wait/width，等待越长该项越小，故不能无条件解释为惩罚更早到达。rank_map 对并列值使用最后名次，最佳不一定是 0。

<details><summary>原始 Idea、校准 Idea、最终代码与父代码</summary>

### 前置 Idea

Replace the additive composite score with a **rank-based multi-attribute selection** (an ordinal decision rule instead of a weighted-sum). First, compute two hard-filtered feasible sets: (1) `time_feasible` — customers reachable before their window closes (given `current_time` and distances), and (2) `cap_feasible` — customers whose demand fits in `rest_capacity`. The algorithm then ranks the intersection of these two sets using three independent criteria: (a) **savings-based efficiency** (how much the move consolidates the route via Clarke‑Wright savings, penalized by forward distance), (b) **time-window risk** (arrival too early → waiting; arrival too late → tight slack), and (c) **continuity** (how well the candidate keeps the remaining graph connected, measured by the minimum forward distance to any other feasible customer or the depot). The final score is the **sum of each candidate's normalized percentile ranks** (0.0 = best, 1.0 = worst) across the three criteria, which makes the heuristic robust to differing units/magnitudes and prevents any single metric from dominating. This ordinal approach often produces smoother, more stable greedy choices than hand-tuned additive weights.

### 校准 Idea

The implemented algorithm selects the next customer by constructing a strictly feasible set of candidates and applying a rank-based multi-attribute decision rule to avoid unit-sensitivity issues inherent in additive weighted sums. First, it filters the input unvisited nodes to retain only those that are hard-feasible under both time and capacity constraints. A customer is feasible if the vehicle can arrive before the time window closes (arrival time = current time + travel distance $\le$ end time window + $\epsilon$) and the customer's demand does not exceed the remaining vehicle capacity ($\le$ rest capacity + $\epsilon$). If no feasible candidates exist, or only one exists, the algorithm returns the depot or the sole candidate, respectively.

For the remaining feasible set, the algorithm evaluates three independent criteria: savings-based efficiency, time-window risk, and continuity. Efficiency is calculated using a Clarke-Wright savings metric, where the value is defined as the forward distance minus $\alpha$ times the savings (determined by the sum of distances from the current node and candidate to the depot minus the direct distance), with $\alpha = 0.55$. Lower values indicate better route consolidation. Time-window risk combines normalized waiting time (waiting time divided by window width + 1) with tightness (1 minus the ratio of remaining slack to window width), penalizing both early arrivals requiring waiting and late arrivals with minimal slack. Continuity is measured by the minimum forward distance from the candidate to any other feasible customer or the depot, favoring nodes that keep the remaining graph connected.

Instead of summing these raw values with hand-tuned weights, the algorithm converts each criterion's values across all feasible candidates into normalized percentile ranks, where 0.0 represents the best (smallest) value and 1.0 represents the worst (largest). This ordinal transformation ensures that no single metric dominates due to differing magnitudes or units, providing robustness across diverse problem scales. The final selection is the candidate with the minimum sum of its three percentile ranks, establishing a stable, weight-agnostic preference that smooths greedy choices by relying on relative ordering rather than absolute composite scores.

The primary implementation change relative to the parent code is the replacement of an additive weighted-sum scoring function with this rank-based percentile normalization. The parent code computed a total score by summing raw effective distances, wait times, and various penalties (tightness, capacity risk, lookahead) scaled by a dynamic factor ($0.10 \times$ mean forward distance) and specific weights ($\alpha=0.55$, $w_{wait}=0.5$, etc.). This required careful tuning to balance metrics with different units (distance vs. time) and scales. The final code eliminates these hyperparameter weights for the final combination step, using equal weighting implicitly through the rank summation, and removes the capacity penalty component from the scoring since capacity is now a hard filter rather than a soft penalty. This shift from cardinal to ordinal evaluation simplifies the tuning process and stabilizes the heuristic's behavior by focusing on the relative quality of candidates rather than their absolute cost contributions.

### 最终代码

```python
import numpy as np

def select_next_node(current_node: int, depot: int, unvisited_nodes: np.ndarray, rest_capacity: float, current_time: float, demands: np.ndarray, distance_matrix: np.ndarray, time_windows: np.ndarray) -> int:
    """
    Select the next node using a rank-based multi-attribute greedy rule.

    Main idea:
      - Filter candidates by hard feasibility (time window and capacity).
      - Score each candidate by three criteria:
          1) Savings-based efficiency (route consolidation)
          2) Time-window risk (waiting + tight slack)
          3) Continuity/lookahead (min forward distance to other feasible or depot)
      - Convert each criterion to normalized percentile ranks (0=best, 1=worst)
      - Choose the candidate with the lowest sum of ranks
    """
    if unvisited_nodes is None or len(unvisited_nodes) == 0:
        return int(depot)

    candidates = []
    for n in unvisited_nodes:
        n_int = int(n)
        if n_int == -1 or n_int == current_node:
            continue
        candidates.append(n_int)

    if not candidates:
        return int(depot)

    if len(candidates) == 1:
        return candidates[0]

    # Hard feasibility: time window (must arrive by end) and capacity
    feasible = []
    for c in candidates:
        d = float(distance_matrix[current_node, c])
        arrival = float(current_time) + d
        end_tw = float(time_windows[c][1])
        # Small tolerance for floating point
        if arrival <= end_tw + 1e-9:
            # Capacity feasibility (assuming unvisited_nodes already respects capacity,
            # but we enforce it explicitly for robustness)
            if float(demands[c]) <= float(rest_capacity) + 1e-9:
                feasible.append(c)

    if not feasible:
        return int(depot)

    if len(feasible) == 1:
        return feasible[0]

    # Precompute some helper values
    d_current_depot = float(distance_matrix[current_node, depot])

    eff_scores = []
    time_risk_scores = []
    continuity_scores = []

    for c in feasible:
        d = float(distance_matrix[current_node, c])
        arrival = float(current_time) + d

        # Criterion 1: Savings-based efficiency
        # savings = d_current_depot + d_c_depot - d
        # We want to favor large savings and small forward distance.
        # Score to minimize: d - alpha * savings = d - alpha*(d_current_depot + d_c_depot - d)
        # = d*(1+alpha) - alpha*(d_current_depot + d_c_depot)
        # For rank purposes, we can compute a value and rank by it (lower is better).
        d_c_depot = float(distance_matrix[c, depot])
        alpha = 0.55
        savings = d_current_depot + d_c_depot - d
        eff_value = d - alpha * savings  # lower is better (more consolidation)

        # Criterion 2: Time-window risk
        # Penalize waiting (arrive too early) and tight slack (arrive too late)
        start_tw = float(time_windows[c][0])
        end_tw = float(time_windows[c][1])
        window_width = max(end_tw - start_tw, 1e-9)

        wait_time = max(0.0, start_tw - arrival)
        slack = max(0.0, end_tw - arrival)
        slack_ratio = slack / window_width  # 1 = just arrived at start, 0 = at end
        # We want low wait and high slack (low tightness).
        # Combine: risk = wait_time + (1 - slack_ratio)
        # But wait_time is in time units, slack_ratio is dimensionless.
        # For rank-based, we can use a simple composite: wait_time / (window_width + 1) + (1 - slack_ratio)
        # This normalizes wait by window size and uses slack deficit.
        wait_norm = wait_time / (window_width + 1.0)
        tightness = 1.0 - slack_ratio
        time_risk_value = wait_norm + tightness  # lower is better

        # Criterion 3: Continuity / lookahead
        # Minimum distance from c to any other feasible customer or the depot
        min_next = float('inf')
        for k in feasible:
            if k == c:
                continue
            dk = float(distance_matrix[c, k])
            if dk < min_next:
                min_next = dk
        d_c_depot_for_min = float(distance_matrix[c, depot])
        if d_c_depot_for_min < min_next:
            min_next = d_c_depot_for_min
        continuity_value = min_next  # lower is better (better connected)

        eff_scores.append(eff_value)
        time_risk_scores.append(time_risk_value)
        continuity_scores.append(continuity_value)

    def percentile_rank(values):
        """
        Return normalized percentile ranks in [0, 1] where 0 is best (smallest value).
        Using rank / (n-1) for n>1; for n==1 return [0.0].
        """
        n = len(values)
        if n == 1:
            return [0.0]
        # Sort to get ranks
        sorted_vals = sorted(values)
        # Map each value to its rank (0-indexed in sorted order)
        rank_map = {}
        for idx, v in enumerate(sorted_vals):
            # In case of ties, assign average rank
            rank_map[v] = idx  # Simple: first occurrence; for ties, this is okay for rank-based heuristic
        ranks = []
        for v in values:
            r = rank_map[v]
            # Normalize: 0 = best, 1 = worst
            ranks.append(r / (n - 1))
        return ranks

    rank_eff = percentile_rank(eff_scores)
    rank_time = percentile_rank(time_risk_scores)
    rank_cont = percentile_rank(continuity_scores)

    # Combine ranks: simple sum (equal weight)
    # Can be adjusted, but equal weights are a robust starting point for ordinal method
    total_rank_scores = [re + rt + rc for re, rt, rc in zip(rank_eff, rank_time, rank_cont)]

    # Find the index with the minimum total rank score
    min_score = float('inf')
    best_idx = 0
    for i, s in enumerate(total_rank_scores):
        if s < min_score:
            min_score = s
            best_idx = i

    best_node = feasible[best_idx]
    return int(best_node)
```

### 父代码

```python
import numpy as np

def select_next_node(current_node: int, depot: int, unvisited_nodes: np.ndarray, rest_capacity: float, current_time: float, demands: np.ndarray, distance_matrix: np.ndarray, time_windows: np.ndarray) -> int:
    """
    Selects the next node in a VRPTW route using a composite score that balances
    Clarke-Wright savings, waiting time, time-window tightness, capacity risk,
    and lookahead connectivity.

    The algorithm minimizes:
    Score = Effective_Distance + w_wait * Wait_Time + w_tight * Tightness_Cost +
            w_cap * Capacity_Cost + w_look * Lookahead_Cost

    Where penalties are scaled by a factor of the mean forward distance to ensure
    numerical stability and consistent weighting across different problem scales.
    """
    if unvisited_nodes is None or len(unvisited_nodes) == 0:
        return depot

    candidates = []
    for n in unvisited_nodes:
        n_int = int(n)
        if n_int == -1 or n_int == current_node:
            continue
        candidates.append(n_int)

    if not candidates:
        return depot

    if len(candidates) == 1:
        return candidates[0]

    d_current_depot = float(distance_matrix[current_node, depot])

    feasible = []
    forward_dists = {}
    arrival_times = {}

    # 1. Filter for hard time-window feasibility
    for c in candidates:
        d = float(distance_matrix[current_node, c])
        arrival = current_time + d
        start_tw = float(time_windows[c][0])
        end_tw = float(time_windows[c][1])

        if arrival <= end_tw + 1e-9:
            feasible.append(c)
            forward_dists[c] = d
            arrival_times[c] = arrival

    if not feasible:
        return depot
    if len(feasible) == 1:
        return feasible[0]

    # Calculate scale factor for normalizing penalties
    avg_dist = float(np.mean([forward_dists[c] for c in feasible]))
    if avg_dist < 1e-9:
        avg_dist = 1.0
    scale = avg_dist * 0.10

    # 2. Calculate lookahead costs (min distance to next stop)
    min_next_dists = {}
    for c in feasible:
        min_d = float('inf')
        # Check other feasible customers
        for k in feasible:
            if k == c:
                continue
            dk = float(distance_matrix[c, k])
            if dk < min_d:
                min_d = dk
        # Also consider going back to depot as a potential end of route
        d_depot = float(distance_matrix[c, depot])
        if d_depot < min_d:
            min_d = d_depot

        min_next_dists[c] = min_d if min_d < float('inf') else 0.0

    # Weights
    alpha_savings = 0.55
    w_wait = 0.5
    w_tight = 0.3  # Always active
    w_cap = 0.4    # Always active
    w_look = 0.35

    best_node = -1
    best_score = float('inf')

    for node in feasible:
        d_forward = forward_dists[node]
        arrival = arrival_times[node]

        # Component 1: Effective Distance (Clarke-Wright Savings)
        # Rewards nodes that keep the route close to the depot relative to the direct path
        d_node_depot = float(distance_matrix[node, depot])
        savings = d_current_depot + d_node_depot - d_forward
        eff_dist = d_forward - alpha_savings * savings

        # Component 2: Waiting Time
        start_tw = float(time_windows[node][0])
        wait_time = max(0.0, start_tw - arrival)

        # Component 3: Time-Window Tightness
        # Always-active quadratic penalty to gently prefer nodes with more slack
        end_tw = float(time_windows[node][1])
        window_width = end_tw - start_tw
        if window_width > 1e-9:
            slack = end_tw - arrival
            slack_ratio = max(0.0, min(1.0, slack / window_width))
            tightness = (1.0 - slack_ratio) ** 2
        else:
            tightness = 1.0
        tightness_cost = w_tight * scale * tightness

        # Component 4: Capacity Risk
        # Always-active quadratic penalty to avoid consuming too much capacity early
        demand_node = float(demands[node])
        if rest_capacity > 1e-9:
            cap_ratio = min(1.0, demand_node / rest_capacity)
            cap_penalty = cap_ratio ** 2
        else:
            cap_penalty = 1.0
        cap_cost = w_cap * scale * cap_penalty

        # Component 5: Lookahead Connectivity
        # Penalizes moves that lead to isolated nodes or long jumps to the next
        lookahead_cost = w_look * min_next_dists[node]

        total_score = (eff_dist +
                       w_wait * wait_time +
                       tightness_cost +
                       cap_cost +
                       lookahead_cost)

        if total_score < best_score:
            best_score = total_score
            best_node = node

    if best_node == -1:
        return depot

    return best_node
```

</details>

## 28 vrptw_construct_r3_e114

算子 Refine；节点 109；父节点 34；fitness -26.4183021595927；局部纠错：是。

增益：把原版声称计算夹角的算法改述为实际 detour=d_current,candidate+d_candidate,depot-d_current,depot。

保留问题或边界：二次版仍把加在 argmin 中的紧窗惩罚说成优先紧窗；还把线性 1-clip(slack_ratio) 描述为 inversely proportional/convex-like。

<details><summary>原始 Idea、校准 Idea、最终代码与父代码</summary>

### 前置 Idea

The current algorithm treats all nodes with equal weight regarding their position in the sequence, relying on a simple weighted sum of distance, waiting, urgency, and 2-step lookahead. To improve VRPTW performance, I will enhance the heuristic with **Dynamic Urgency Scaling** and **Angle-based Connectivity**.

1.  **Dynamic Urgency Scaling**: Instead of a static urgency weight, the algorithm will increase the importance of urgency as the vehicle gets closer to the end of the route (indicated by high `current_time` relative to the maximum window end times). Early in the route, we prioritize distance minimization; later, we prioritize feasibility to avoid getting stuck with infeasible nodes.
2.  **Angle-based Connectivity (Deviation)**: The 2-step lookahead (minimum distance to depot or neighbor) is often too local. I will replace/augment it with an angle deviation measure. For each candidate, I calculate the angle formed by the vector from `current_node` to `depot` and the vector from `current_node` to `candidate`. Deviations significantly greater than 180 degrees (going "backwards") or sharply turning away from the return path are penalized. This encourages creating routes that follow a directional sweep, which naturally reduces total travel distance compared to arbitrary nearest-neighbor moves.
3.  **Refined Scoring**: The score becomes a weighted sum of normalized distance, normalized waiting time, dynamically weighted urgency, and normalized angular deviation. This balances the immediate cost (distance) with the structural quality of the route (angle) and constraint safety (urgency/waiting).

### 校准 Idea

The implemented heuristic selects the next node by minimizing a composite score that balances immediate travel distance, structural route continuity via detour cost, waiting time, and dynamically weighted time-window urgency. The decision rule operates on the set of feasible unvisited candidates, calculating four normalized components for each. First, the direct distance from the current node to the candidate is computed and normalized by the maximum distance in the matrix. Second, a structural penalty is derived from the "detour cost," calculated as the sum of the distance from the current node to the candidate and from the candidate to the depot, minus the direct distance from the current node to the depot. This metric approximates angular deviation by penalizing candidates that require the vehicle to deviate significantly from the path toward the depot, thereby encouraging smooth, sweeping routes. Third, waiting time is determined by comparing the arrival time (current time plus travel distance) against the candidate's time window start, normalized by the global maximum window end time. Fourth, urgency is quantified by the ratio of remaining slack to total window size; a penalty is assigned that is inversely proportional to this ratio, with a convex-like emphasis on tight windows.

The algorithm introduces a key dynamic weighting mechanism that adjusts the importance of these components based on the progression of the current time relative to the global maximum time window end. As the vehicle progresses through the time horizon (increasing `time_progress`), the weight of the urgency penalty increases linearly from 0.5 to 1.5, while the weight of the direct distance component decreases slightly from 1.0 to 0.8. The detour weight remains constant at 0.5, and the waiting time weight is fixed at 0.3. This adaptive strategy shifts the heuristic's focus from structural optimization (minimizing detours and distance) in the early stages of the route to feasibility preservation (prioritizing nodes with tight deadlines) in the later stages. The final score is a weighted sum of these four normalized terms, and the candidate with the minimum score is selected. If no candidates remain, the algorithm returns to the depot.

Relative to the parent code, the primary implementation changes involve replacing the 2-step lookahead cost with the detour-based structural penalty and introducing dynamic urgency scaling. The parent code relied on a static weighted sum that included a computationally expensive 2-step lookahead (minimum distance to depot or nearest neighbor) to estimate future connectivity. The final code simplifies this by using the detour cost, which is a single O(N) calculation per candidate and more directly correlates with route sweep quality without the need for pairwise candidate distance matrices. Additionally, while the parent code used static weights for all components, the final code implements the "Dynamic Urgency Scaling" design idea by varying the urgency and distance weights based on `time_progress`. This allows the heuristic to prioritize feasibility as the route approaches the end of the time horizon, whereas the parent code treated urgency with a constant weight. The final code also normalizes waiting time by the maximum window end time rather than the maximum distance, providing a more consistent time-based scaling.

### 最终代码

```python
import numpy as np

def select_next_node(current_node: int, depot: int, unvisited_nodes: np.ndarray, rest_capacity: float, current_time: float, demands: np.ndarray, distance_matrix: np.ndarray, time_windows: np.ndarray) -> int:
    """
    Selects the next node using a heuristic that balances distance, waiting time,
    dynamic urgency, and angular deviation to promote route continuity.

    Args:
        current_node: ID of the current node.
        depot: ID of the depot.
        unvisited_nodes: Array of feasible unvisited node IDs.
        rest_capacity: Remaining vehicle capacity.
        current_time: Current time on the active route.
        demands: Demand of each node.
        distance_matrix: Pairwise distance matrix.
        time_windows: Time window of each node.

    Return:
        ID of the next node to visit, or depot.
    """
    if len(unvisited_nodes) == 0:
        return depot

    candidates = unvisited_nodes.astype(int)
    n_candidates = len(candidates)

    # 1. Calculate distances from current node to candidates
    dists = distance_matrix[current_node, candidates]

    # 2. Time window calculations
    arrival_times = current_time + dists
    start_times = time_windows[candidates, 0]
    end_times = time_windows[candidates, 1]

    service_start = np.maximum(arrival_times, start_times)
    wait_times = np.maximum(0, service_start - arrival_times)
    slack = end_times - service_start
    window_sizes = end_times - start_times

    # Calculate urgency: how tight is the window? (0 = plenty of slack, 1 = no slack)
    valid_window = window_sizes > 0
    slack_ratios = np.zeros(n_candidates)
    slack_ratios[valid_window] = slack[valid_window] / window_sizes[valid_window]

    # Urgency penalty is higher when slack ratio is low (tight window)
    # We use a linear penalty (1 - ratio) for simplicity and stability
    urgency_penalty = 1.0 - np.clip(slack_ratios, 0, 1)
    urgency_penalty[~valid_window] = 1.0 # Infeasible or zero window

    # 3. Angular Deviation Calculation
    # We want to penalize moves that deviate significantly from the direction
    # towards the depot (or generally, we want a smooth sweep).
    # Calculate vectors from current_node to depot and to each candidate.

    # Note: distance_matrix is Euclidean or metric, so we can infer coordinates?
    # No, we don't have coordinates. We only have distances.
    # However, we can approximate "direction" by comparing distances to a reference point (Depot).
    # A common proxy for angle in metric spaces without coords is the "detour" cost:
    # detour = dist(current, candidate) + dist(candidate, depot) - dist(current, depot)
    # This is the extra distance incurred by visiting candidate before going to depot.

    dist_current_depot = distance_matrix[current_node, depot]
    dist_cand_depot = distance_matrix[candidates, depot]

    # Detour cost represents how "off the main path" the candidate is.
    # Lower detour means the candidate is on a path roughly towards the depot.
    detour_costs = dists + dist_cand_depot - dist_current_depot

    # Normalize detour costs. Max possible detour is roughly 2 * max_dist.
    max_detour_ref = 2.0 * np.max(distance_matrix)
    if max_detour_ref == 0:
        max_detour_ref = 1.0

    norm_detours = detour_costs / max_detour_ref
    # Clamp to handle any negative values (triangle inequality violation or noise)
    norm_detours = np.clip(norm_detours, 0, 2)

    # 4. Dynamic Weighting for Urgency
    # Determine how "late" we are in the global time horizon.
    max_window_end = np.max(time_windows[:, 1])
    if max_window_end == 0:
        max_window_end = 1.0

    time_progress = np.clip(current_time / max_window_end, 0, 1)

    # Early in the route (time_progress ~ 0): Focus on distance/detour (structure).
    # Late in the route (time_progress ~ 1): Focus on urgency (feasibility).

    base_w_dist = 1.0
    base_w_wait = 0.3
    base_w_detour = 0.5

    # Urgency weight ramps up from 0.5 to 1.5 as time progresses
    w_urgency = 0.5 + time_progress * 1.0

    # Distance weight ramps down slightly to accommodate urgency
    w_dist = base_w_dist - time_progress * 0.2

    # Detour weight stays moderate to keep routes sweeping
    w_detour = base_w_detour

    # Waiting weight
    w_wait = base_w_wait

    # 5. Composite Score
    norm_dists = dists / np.max(distance_matrix)
    norm_waits = wait_times / max_window_end # Normalize time by max window end

    # Minimize score
    scores = (w_dist * norm_dists +
              w_wait * norm_waits +
              w_urgency * urgency_penalty +
              w_detour * norm_detours)

    # Select the candidate with the minimum score
    best_idx = np.argmin(scores)
    best_node = candidates[best_idx]

    return int(best_node)
```

### 父代码

```python
import numpy as np

def select_next_node(current_node: int, depot: int, unvisited_nodes: np.ndarray, rest_capacity: float, current_time: float, demands: np.ndarray, distance_matrix: np.ndarray, time_windows: np.ndarray) -> int:
    """
    Selects the next node using a vectorized, adaptive scoring mechanism that balances
    distance, waiting time, relative urgency, and 2-step lookahead connectivity cost.

    The score is a weighted sum of:
    1. Normalized distance from current node.
    2. Normalized waiting time.
    3. Adaptive urgency penalty based on relative slack (slack / window_size).
    4. Normalized 2-step lookahead cost (min dist to depot or nearest other node).
    """
    if len(unvisited_nodes) == 0:
        return depot

    # Convert to integers for indexing
    candidates = unvisited_nodes.astype(int)

    # Get normalization factor for consistent weighting across problem scales
    max_dist = np.max(distance_matrix)
    if max_dist == 0:
        max_dist = 1.0

    # Vectorized calculations for candidates
    # Distances from current node
    dists = distance_matrix[current_node, candidates]

    # Arrival times
    arrival_times = current_time + dists

    # Time windows for candidates
    start_times = time_windows[candidates, 0]
    end_times = time_windows[candidates, 1]

    # Service start times (max of arrival and window start)
    service_start = np.maximum(arrival_times, start_times)

    # Waiting times
    wait_times = np.maximum(0, service_start - arrival_times)

    # Slack (time remaining before deadline)
    slack = end_times - service_start

    # Window sizes for relative urgency calculation
    window_sizes = end_times - start_times

    # Handle zero window sizes to avoid division by zero
    valid_window = window_sizes > 0
    slack_ratios = np.zeros(len(candidates))
    slack_ratios[valid_window] = slack[valid_window] / window_sizes[valid_window]

    # Relative urgency: Penalize nodes with low relative slack.
    # Use a convex penalty to strongly prioritize tight windows.
    # (1 - ratio)^2 ensures high penalty when ratio is low (tight).
    # If window is zero or invalid, treat as max urgency.
    urgency_penalty = (1.0 - np.clip(slack_ratios, 0, 1)) ** 2
    urgency_penalty[~valid_window] = 1.0

    # 2-Step Lookahead Cost
    # Estimate the cost of moving from the candidate to the "next" point (depot or nearest other).
    # This helps avoid picking a node that is far from everything else.
    # We approximate this vectorizedly by finding the min distance to depot or other nodes.
    # Note: A true O(N^2) vectorization of "min dist to other non-self nodes" is complex in one go,
    # so we compute min dist to depot and min dist to any other node separately.

    # Min distance to depot
    dists_to_depot = distance_matrix[candidates, depot]

    # Min distance to any other node in the candidate set
    # We create a matrix of distances between candidates and find the min excluding diagonal
    # dist_matrix_candidates is N x N
    dist_matrix_candidates = distance_matrix[np.ix_(candidates, candidates)]

    # Set diagonal to infinity so we don't pick self
    np.fill_diagonal(dist_matrix_candidates, np.inf)

    # If there's only one candidate, min_dist_to_other will be inf (or handled by shape)
    if len(candidates) > 1:
        min_dist_to_other = np.min(dist_matrix_candidates, axis=1)
    else:
        min_dist_to_other = np.zeros(len(candidates)) # Will be dominated by depot anyway

    # Lookahead cost is the minimum of going to depot or going to the nearest other node
    # If no other nodes, it goes to depot.
    lookahead_costs = np.minimum(dists_to_depot, min_dist_to_other)

    # Normalize all components
    norm_dists = dists / max_dist
    norm_waits = wait_times / max_dist
    norm_lookaheads = lookahead_costs / max_dist

    # Weights based on previous successful attempts
    w_dist = 1.0
    w_wait = 0.2
    w_urgency = 0.4
    w_lookahead = 0.3

    total_scores = (w_dist * norm_dists +
                    w_wait * norm_waits +
                    w_urgency * urgency_penalty +
                    w_lookahead * norm_lookaheads)

    # Find the node with the minimum score
    best_idx = np.argmin(total_scores)
    best_node = candidates[best_idx]

    return int(best_node)
```

## 29 vrptw_construct_r3_e300

算子 Refine；节点 286；父节点 271；fitness -22.622962012466367；局部纠错：未计入。

增益：补充实际五项权重、平均 rank 和父代删除角度/返仓偏置等变化。

保留问题或边界：紧迫度 rank 越高，argmin 分数越高，故不是优先紧急客户；“minimum becomes 0 / best always 0”不适用于平均并列 rank，全部相同时 rank=.5，仅对排序等效为常数。

<details><summary>原始 Idea、校准 Idea、最终代码与父代码</summary>

### 前置 Idea

The core improvement is to make the heuristic scale-invariant and more robust by switching all penalty components from raw distance units (which can have wildly different magnitudes across instances) to **rank-based normalization** mapped to a common unit scale. This ensures that the relative weighting of distance, feasibility regret, and time urgency remains consistent regardless of the map size or time-window tightness. Specifically:
1.  **Distance**: Normalized via rank (closest=0, farthest=1) and scaled by the max distance.
2.  **Regret**: Counts of infeasible successors are rank-normalized and scaled by max distance, giving "critical" nodes a consistent penalty magnitude relative to travel cost.
3.  **Slack**: The urgency penalty $1/(1+\text{slack})$ is rank-normalized. Crucially, if slack is non-uniform (some windows are tight), this term drives the decision. If all slacks are similar, this term vanishes, allowing distance to dominate, which is the correct behavior for loose instances.
4.  **Lookahead**: The 2-step lookahead cost is rank-normalized. This prevents a single very cheap continuation from overwhelming the score due to scale differences, while still rewarding candidates that lead to efficient subsequent moves.
5.  **Weights**: The weights are fixed but chosen to balance local greediness (distance) with global feasibility (regret) and route shape (lookahead).

### 校准 Idea

The implemented algorithm selects the next node by minimizing a composite score where all components are transformed into a scale-invariant range using rank normalization. The process begins by filtering the unvisited nodes to identify those strictly feasible regarding capacity and time windows (arrival time must be before the window end, and demand must fit remaining capacity). If no such nodes exist, the algorithm returns to the depot. For the remaining feasible candidates, the heuristic calculates five distinct metrics: direct distance from the current node, one-step regret (the count of other feasible nodes that would become infeasible if the candidate is visited next), two-step lookahead cost (the minimum cost to go from the candidate to a successor and then to the depot), time-window slack (derived as $1/(1+\text{slack})$ to prioritize urgent windows), and waiting time. Each metric is passed through a rank normalization function, which maps the values such that the minimum value in the candidate set becomes 0 and the maximum becomes 1, with ties receiving the average rank. This transformation decouples the heuristic’s sensitivity from the absolute magnitude of distances or time windows, ensuring that a "small" distance in a large map is weighted relative to other candidates in the same manner as a small distance in a small map. The final score is a weighted linear combination of these normalized ranks, scaled by the maximum distance among candidates to maintain dimensional consistency in the output metric, although the relative ordering remains invariant to this scaling.

The decision rule relies on fixed weights assigned to each component: distance (1.0), regret (0.6), lookahead (0.4), slack urgency (0.2), and waiting time (0.1). This configuration prioritizes local proximity while strongly penalizing choices that reduce future flexibility (high regret) or lead to inefficient routes (high lookahead cost). The rank normalization ensures that these weights act as relative importance factors rather than absolute unit converters. For instance, if all time windows are loose, the slack penalties will be uniformly low; however, if one node has a critically tight window, its slack rank will be high (close to 1), increasing its score appropriately relative to the others without requiring knowledge of the specific tightness magnitude. Similarly, the regret component is rank-normalized, meaning that if visiting candidate A closes off 3 successors while candidate B closes off 2, A is penalized more than B, but the penalty magnitude is determined by their relative standing in the set of all candidates, not by the absolute number of closed nodes. This approach aims to provide robustness across diverse instance sizes, preventing scenarios where a single metric with a large absolute range (e.g., distance in a 1000km map vs. 10km map) dominates the score due to scale differences, thereby promoting consistent heuristic behavior.

Compared to the parent code, the primary implementation change is the replacement of raw or max-normalized metric values with rank-based normalization for all penalty components. The parent code used raw distances, max-normalized regrets, and raw slack penalties, which could lead to inconsistent weighting if the instance scale changed significantly or if the distribution of values was skewed. The final code introduces a helper function `_rank_normalize` to handle this transformation, ensuring that the "best" candidate for any metric is always assigned a score of 0 for that component. Additionally, the parent code included an angular deviation penalty and an adaptive return-to-depot bias, both of which are removed in the final version to simplify the heuristic and focus purely on the five core metrics. The lookahead weight is also fixed at 0.4 in the final code, whereas the parent code used an adaptive weight that varied based on the number of remaining candidates. This simplification reduces computational overhead and parameter tuning complexity, aiming for a more stable, though potentially less adaptive, selection process.

### 最终代码

```python
import numpy as np

def _rank_normalize(arr):
    """
    Rank-normalize an array to [0, 1].
    Values that are smaller get lower ranks (closer to 0).
    Handles ties by assigning the mean rank.
    """
    if len(arr) == 0:
        return np.array([])

    # argsort gives indices that would sort the array
    # We want ranks where min value -> 0, max value -> 1
    sorter = np.argsort(arr)
    ranks = np.empty(len(arr), dtype=float)

    # Assign ranks
    current_rank = 0
    i = 0
    n = len(arr)
    while i < n:
        j = i
        while j < n and arr[sorter[j]] == arr[sorter[i]]:
            j += 1
        # All elements from i to j-1 have the same value
        # Their average rank is (i + j - 1) / 2
        avg_rank = (i + j - 1) / 2.0
        ranks[sorter[i:j]] = avg_rank
        i = j
        current_rank = i

    # Normalize to [0, 1]
    if n > 1:
        ranks = ranks / (n - 1)
    else:
        ranks[0] = 0.0
    return ranks

def select_next_node(current_node: int, depot: int, unvisited_nodes: np.ndarray, rest_capacity: float, current_time: float, demands: np.ndarray, distance_matrix: np.ndarray, time_windows: np.ndarray) -> int:
    """
    Select the next node for VRPTW using a scale-invariant composite score.
    """
    if len(unvisited_nodes) == 0:
        return depot

    candidate_ids = unvisited_nodes.astype(int)
    n_candidates = len(candidate_ids)
    if n_candidates == 1:
        return int(candidate_ids[0])

    dists = distance_matrix[current_node, candidate_ids].astype(float)
    arr_times = current_time + dists
    tw_starts = time_windows[candidate_ids, 0].astype(float)
    tw_ends = time_windows[candidate_ids, 1].astype(float)
    cand_demands = demands[candidate_ids].astype(float)

    waits = np.maximum(0.0, tw_starts - arr_times)
    eff_departs = arr_times + waits

    feasible_mask = (arr_times <= tw_ends) & (cand_demands <= rest_capacity)
    if not np.any(feasible_mask):
        return depot

    candidate_ids = candidate_ids[feasible_mask]
    dists = dists[feasible_mask]
    arr_times = arr_times[feasible_mask]
    tw_starts = tw_starts[feasible_mask]
    tw_ends = tw_ends[feasible_mask]
    cand_demands = cand_demands[feasible_mask]
    waits = waits[feasible_mask]
    eff_departs = eff_departs[feasible_mask]

    n_valid = len(candidate_ids)
    if n_valid == 1:
        return int(candidate_ids[0])

    # Determine scale for normalizing rank-based scores
    max_dist = float(np.max(dists))
    if max_dist < 1e-6:
        max_dist = 1.0

    # 1. Distance component
    dist_rank = _rank_normalize(dists)

    # 2. Regret component (1-step)
    # Compute feasibility of transition from i to j for all i, j in candidate_ids
    dist_sub = distance_matrix[np.ix_(candidate_ids, candidate_ids)].astype(float)
    dist_to_depot = distance_matrix[candidate_ids, depot].astype(float)

    # Time feasibility: dep_i + d_ij <= end_j
    arr_ij = eff_departs[:, None] + dist_sub
    time_feasible_ij = arr_ij <= tw_ends[None, :]

    # Capacity feasibility: dem_i + dem_j <= rest_capacity
    cap_feasible_ij = (cand_demands[:, None] + cand_demands[None, :]) <= rest_capacity

    feasible_transitions = time_feasible_ij & cap_feasible_ij
    np.fill_diagonal(feasible_transitions, False)

    # Regret: number of j that are NOT feasible after visiting i
    regrets = np.sum(~feasible_transitions, axis=1).astype(float)
    regret_rank = _rank_normalize(regrets)

    # 3. Lookahead component (2-step)
    # Cost = min_j (d_ij + d_j_depot) subject to feasibility
    cost_matrix = dist_sub + dist_to_depot[None, :]
    cost_matrix[~feasible_transitions] = np.inf

    lookahead_cost = np.min(cost_matrix, axis=1)
    # If no successor, fall back to d_i_depot
    no_succ = np.isinf(lookahead_cost)
    lookahead_cost[no_succ] = dist_to_depot[no_succ]

    lookahead_rank = _rank_normalize(lookahead_cost)

    # 4. Slack urgency
    slack = np.maximum(0.0, tw_ends - eff_departs)
    # Penalty is high when slack is small
    # We use rank of slack in reverse: small slack -> high penalty
    # Or simply rank of 1/(1+slack)
    slack_penalty_raw = 1.0 / (1.0 + slack)
    slack_rank = _rank_normalize(slack_penalty_raw)

    # 5. Wait penalty
    # We want to penalize waiting.
    # However, sometimes waiting is unavoidable.
    # Let's just use rank of wait time.
    wait_rank = _rank_normalize(waits)

    # Scale all rank components to [0, max_dist] to make them comparable to distance
    # This assumes that a rank difference of 1 step in the candidate set corresponds
    # to a meaningful fraction of the maximum distance.

    scores = (
        1.0 * dist_rank * max_dist +
        0.6 * regret_rank * max_dist +
        0.4 * lookahead_rank * max_dist +
        0.2 * slack_rank * max_dist +
        0.1 * wait_rank * max_dist
    )

    best_idx = int(np.argmin(scores))
    return int(candidate_ids[best_idx])
```

### 父代码

```python
import numpy as np

def _angular_deviation(current_node, depot, candidate_ids, distance_matrix):
    """Law-of-cosines angular deviation from the depot->current vector."""
    n = len(candidate_ids)
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0)
    d_dep_curr = distance_matrix[depot, current_node]
    if d_dep_curr < 1e-6:
        return np.zeros(n)
    d_dep_cands = distance_matrix[depot, candidate_ids]
    d_curr_cands = distance_matrix[current_node, candidate_ids]
    numerator = d_dep_curr**2 + d_dep_cands**2 - d_curr_cands**2
    denominator = 2.0 * d_dep_curr * d_dep_cands
    valid_mask = denominator > 1e-9
    cos_theta = np.zeros(n)
    cos_theta[valid_mask] = numerator[valid_mask] / denominator[valid_mask]
    cos_theta = np.clip(cos_theta, -1.0, 1.0)
    angles = np.arccos(cos_theta)
    return angles / np.pi

def select_next_node(current_node: int, depot: int, unvisited_nodes: np.ndarray, rest_capacity: float, current_time: float, demands: np.ndarray, distance_matrix: np.ndarray, time_windows: np.ndarray) -> int:
    """
    Select the next node for VRPTW by minimizing a composite cost (in distance units):
    - raw distance (primary),
    - adaptive 2-step feasibility-aware lookahead (min_j d(i,j)+d(j,depot)),
    - 1-step feasibility regret scaled to distance,
    - sweep-coherence (angular) penalty,
    - slack urgency 1/(1+slack),
    - small wait penalty,
    - adaptive return-to-depot bias when few candidates remain.
    """
    if len(unvisited_nodes) == 0:
        return depot

    candidate_ids = unvisited_nodes.astype(int)
    n_candidates = len(candidate_ids)
    if n_candidates == 1:
        return int(candidate_ids[0])

    dists = distance_matrix[current_node, candidate_ids].astype(float)
    arr_times = current_time + dists
    tw_starts = time_windows[candidate_ids, 0].astype(float)
    tw_ends = time_windows[candidate_ids, 1].astype(float)
    cand_demands = demands[candidate_ids].astype(float)

    waits = np.maximum(0.0, tw_starts - arr_times)
    eff_departs = arr_times + waits

    # Strict feasibility: arrive before window end AND fit capacity.
    feasible_mask = (arr_times <= tw_ends) & (cand_demands <= rest_capacity)
    if not np.any(feasible_mask):
        return depot

    candidate_ids = candidate_ids[feasible_mask]
    dists = dists[feasible_mask]
    arr_times = arr_times[feasible_mask]
    tw_starts = tw_starts[feasible_mask]
    tw_ends = tw_ends[feasible_mask]
    cand_demands = cand_demands[feasible_mask]
    waits = waits[feasible_mask]
    eff_departs = eff_departs[feasible_mask]

    n_valid = len(candidate_ids)
    if n_valid == 1:
        return int(candidate_ids[0])

    # Pairwise transitions among candidates.
    dist_sub = distance_matrix[np.ix_(candidate_ids, candidate_ids)].astype(float)
    dist_to_depot = distance_matrix[candidate_ids, depot].astype(float)

    # Feasibility of transition i -> j (time + capacity).
    arr_ij = eff_departs[:, None] + dist_sub
    time_feasible_ij = arr_ij <= tw_ends[None, :]
    cap_feasible_ij = (cand_demands[:, None] + cand_demands[None, :]) <= rest_capacity
    feasible_transitions = time_feasible_ij & cap_feasible_ij
    np.fill_diagonal(feasible_transitions, False)

    # 2-step lookahead: min over feasible j of d(i,j)+d(j,depot).
    cost_matrix = dist_sub + dist_to_depot[None, :]
    cost_matrix[~feasible_transitions] = np.inf
    lookahead_cost = np.min(cost_matrix, axis=1)
    no_succ = np.isinf(lookahead_cost)
    lookahead_cost[no_succ] = dist_to_depot[no_succ]

    # 1-step feasibility regret: number of candidates made infeasible by visiting i.
    regrets = np.sum(~feasible_transitions, axis=1).astype(float)

    # Angular / sweep coherence penalty.
    angular_devs = _angular_deviation(current_node, depot, candidate_ids, distance_matrix)

    # Time-window urgency.
    slack = np.maximum(0.0, tw_ends - eff_departs)
    slack_penalty = 1.0 / (1.0 + slack)

    # Scaling references in distance units.
    avg_dist = float(np.mean(dists)) if n_valid > 0 else 1.0
    if avg_dist <= 0:
        avg_dist = 1.0
    max_dist = float(np.max(dists)) if len(dists) > 0 else avg_dist

    if float(np.max(regrets)) > 0:
        regret_scaled = (regrets / float(np.max(regrets))) * max_dist
    else:
        regret_scaled = np.zeros(n_valid)

    max_ang = float(np.max(angular_devs)) if len(angular_devs) > 0 else 1.0
    if max_ang > 0:
        ang_scaled = (angular_devs / max_ang) * (0.5 * max_dist)
    else:
        ang_scaled = np.zeros(n_valid)

    # Adaptive lookahead weight: stronger as fewer candidates remain.
    if n_valid <= 3:
        w_lookahead = 1.2
    elif n_valid <= 5:
        w_lookahead = 0.9
    elif n_valid <= 10:
        w_lookahead = 0.6
    else:
        w_lookahead = 0.4

    w_dist = 1.0
    w_regret = 1.0
    w_ang = 0.4
    w_wait = 0.1
    w_slack = 0.15

    # Adaptive return-to-depot bias (mild), stronger when few candidates remain.
    return_weight = 0.08 * (1.0 / (1.0 + n_valid / 6.0))
    norm_depot = dist_to_depot / (max_dist + 1e-9)

    scores = (
        w_dist * dists
        + w_lookahead * lookahead_cost
        + w_regret * regret_scaled
        + w_ang * ang_scaled
        + w_wait * waits
        + w_slack * slack_penalty * avg_dist
        + return_weight * norm_depot * max_dist
    )

    best_idx = int(np.argmin(scores))
    return int(candidate_ids[best_idx])
```
